### O10: The endpoint module and the protocol package (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** It gives every machine one authenticated, encrypted way in. The endpoint module inside `mawsd`:
- terminates the hub socket (RL1), device channels (RL2), subscriptions (RL3) and discrete actions (RL4);
- enforces the machine's policy and each session's grades;
- hands lane A work to sminos over RL10.

One protocol package holds both halves, the endpoint's and the client's, with shared test vectors. The app's main and desk-web's WebCrypto build (O13) import it, so each end of a channel runs the same code.

**Acceptance.**
1. **One implementation.** The test vectors for RL2's handshake and frames, RL4's canonical action and digest, and RL8's event signature pass in both halves, under Node crypto and WebCrypto.
2. **Resume without loss.** Against O11's staging hub, a device opens a channel to a machine and subscribes to a lane A and a lane B session. It resumes by cursor, with no loss and no duplicate, across:
   - a dropped hub socket;
   - a hub deploy (stale-generation);
   - a `mawsd` restart.
3. **The trust cases.** Each of these holds on the staging hub:
   - an action replayed by the relay is refused;
   - an action past 60 s is refused;
   - a reseal with the same opId returns the stored result and runs nothing;
   - a lost acknowledgement resolves through `query {opId, opCreated}`, and an op older than RL4's `OP_HORIZON` is refused `expired`, so a pruned ledger never reopens an id;
   - a frame altered in the relay closes the channel;
   - a device removed by a new org state is refused at once, and so is one removed by a direct revocation. The machine acknowledges only after the removal is durable.
4. **Policy moves intact.** Today's `sminos_policy` behaviour holds, now at the endpoint:
   - read-only mode refuses every action;
   - a spawn above the ceiling is refused;
   - a spawn into an unknown checkout is refused;
   - a local `sminos spawn` stays unbound.
5. **sminos hands over.** With the endpoint enrolled, sminos runs no DX2 uplink. RL10 carries:
   - log events both ways, each signed once and resent byte-identical from the signature ledger;
   - lane A RPCs by opId, with `query` and the tombstone;
   - revocations down;
   - the lane A plane and listings up.
6. **Keys.** The machine key and the software device keys are 0600 files in 0700 directories. Step-up behind LocalAuthentication works in an unsigned build (U5).
7. **Live.** The devcloud acceptance VM, cut over with O14's first brief, passes items 2–5.

8. **Namespace grades (OX9).** The endpoint authorizes every forwarded command by its namespace's declared grade, and refuses a namespace with none (default deny). A test sends one command of each grade class through a channel and checks that each is refused below its grade: a view-grade read on another session's checkout, a manage-grade write, `pty.spawn` without the shell right, and a machine-admin setting. A second test shows that an undeclared namespace added to the registry is unreachable remotely until it declares a grade. A machine owner opening a terminal on their own machine needs no step-up; a holder of the shell right who is not a machine owner does; the same-uid local app is exempt.
9. **Either carrier.** The protocol package's RL2 runs unchanged over the hub multiplex and over a direct socket on the tailnet, with one channel suite driven over both. The direct listener binds only to the tailnet interface, runs nothing before the handshake verifies, and rate-limits handshakes (RL §4.1).

**Edges.** Blocked by O2, by O11 for items 2, 3, 5 and 7, and by O3 for item 2's lane B session, whose events reach a `mawsd` with no app attached only through O3's per-client subscriptions (Decision Log 29). Blocks O7, O9 (remote), O12 (RL10 parts), O13 and O14.
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator. **Contracts.** RL1–RL4, RL7, RL10, OX1.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** executing: its document landed (PR #214, master 1f0fc13ec), eleven milestones with a PR each; M1 to M9 build against its own `EndpointServices` (Decision Log 45); M10 landed (PR #310, master ae73b0546, Decision Log 124); M11 landed (Decision Log 148).

### O11: Hub v2 (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** The hub stops being a party to trust and becomes a relay and a store of signed things. It:
- keeps the org-state chain (RL5) and refuses forks and rollbacks;
- relays sealed frames between apps and endpoints without being able to read them (Machine DO);
- stores the project log with RL8 signatures (Project DO);
- manages members and devices;
- serves the app's own hub connection (OX8/RL11);
- sends push only to a session's owners.

Through the cutover it keeps today's DX2 and DX6 paths for every machine, and for the phone, until each one moves.

**Acceptance.**
1. **Staging.** `desk-hub-staging` exists with its own Access app and serves as the test org (owner gate: an Access app and its secrets).
2. **The registry.**
   - A successor state is accepted only if it links to the head, and rollback is refused.
   - Members can be added, removed, re-roled and have their email changed.
   - Adding a device takes the member's signature and an admin's countersignature.
   - Provisioning keys are re-issued as delegations, and machine certificates are served beside the state.
3. **The Machine DO** relays RL1 and RL2 frames opaque and under 1 MiB, with DX2's lease, `hb` and generation fencing. A deploy drops channels, and they resume by cursor.
4. **The Project DO** stores v2-signed events (key id, `-v2` domain). It accepts dual-coded events during migration and keeps v1 verification for history. Its conflict rule holds with stored signatures.
5. **Push** goes only to the session owners' devices and says only that a session is waiting or wrote.
6. **The app's connection.** The MAWS app gets past Access (U9) and holds one socket per hub object it uses.
7. **U2 measured.** Sealed-relay latency and throughput, app → Machine DO → endpoint, are measured against RL's targets. Whether a direct tailnet transport is needed is decided and recorded.
8. **The phone never breaks.** Every deploy in this child keeps DX2, DX6 and desk-web's current routes working for the machines and the phone not yet moved.

**Gates.** Deploys go through the desk's pre-approved deploy scripts. Durable Object schema migrations, Access changes and secrets wait for the owner.
**Edges.** Blocked by nothing; it starts beside O1. Blocks O7, O8, O10 (live parts), O13 and O14.
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator. **Contracts.** RL1, RL5, RL8, OX8/RL11.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** landed: M1 to M8 (PRs #195, #202 to #206, #208, #210), acceptance 1 to 8 run live on staging with item 5's live push carried to O13 (Decision Log 39), and its closing xhigh panel's fixes (PR #212, Decision Log 41); O8's H1, an admin's read of another member's pending devices, landed after it (PR #306, Decision Log 121).

### O12: sminos under the endpoint: owners, words and the lane B observer (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** sminos stays the project family and the delivery path. It gains the owner model and stops being a network party. In detail:
- every session record carries its owners and its inference route;
- a person's words reach a session only from someone who may drive it;
- to-human messages and asks stay off the hub's log;
- the RPC ledger is keyed by opId;
- the bridge observes lane B sessions without controlling them.

**Acceptance.**
1. **The record.** Every member record carries:
   - `owners` and `ownersProof`;
   - `origin {kind, requester?, spawnerSession?}`;
   - `principal` and `inference`, per RL6, whose `owners` and `principal` admit the token `@machine` (remote layer Decision Log 17).
   Defaults:
   - a terminal session gets `@machine`, its machine's owners as the endpoint module resolves them against signed org state;
   - a `sminos spawn` child copies its parent's owners, proofs and route at spawn and records the parent's session id;
   - records that predate the change read as `@machine`.
2. **Words.**
   - A `human` event is delivered as words only in two cases: the endpoint verified that its signer's member holds `converse` on the target session, or it comes from the machine-attested operator path into a same-machine session that the machine owner owns.
   - Anything else is delivered as a peer message.
   - The check runs again at delivery, against revocations from RL10, so an owed delivery from a removed device never lands as words.
3. **The label.** A `sminos say` from a cut-over machine carries RL9's attestation, built by the endpoint from the record. A receiving session sees "from `<agent>`, owned by `<owner>`" as a peer message.
4. **The ledger.** The RPC ledger is keyed by opId, with `query` and the tombstone over RL10. MW5's upload id becomes the opId.
5. **Local-only.**
   - With RL10 present, sminos runs no DX2, no HMAC coding and no hub enrollment.
   - Without it, on a machine not yet cut over, behaviour is unchanged.
   - Both modes pass the existing sminos and bridge test suites.
6. **To-human off the hub.**
   - On a cut-over machine, `<message>` exports and async asks go to a machine-local store served over RL3, not to the hub's project log.
   - `sminos chat --host` keeps working for the machine's own sessions.
7. **The lane B observer.** Under MAWS's lane B spawn marker, the bridge:
   - honours the marker only in the process MAWS's engine host launched; a `claude` started from inside a lane B session inherits its environment (a command in its Bash, or a `--bg` seat whose supervisor that session started), stays lane A with full control, and a test shows it;
   - posts subagent deltas;
   - holds the `turn.step` reaping gate;
   - refuses every control operation, reporting the controls in `capabilities.controls` as unavailable with the reason "MAWS controls this session";
   - reads `launchedBy` on a remotely spawned session's record and claims the member with its sealed action as `ownersProof`, so the requester owns what it spawned (O7 M4; Decision Log 166).
8. **The mini.** The mini runs this sminos, installed through mini-ops at the owner's request.

**Edges.** Items 1, 4 and 7 start at once. Items 2, 3, 5 and 6 are blocked by O10 (RL10). Blocks O8, O9 and O14.
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator. **Repos:** doperpowers (sminos plugin) and MAWS (the shared shapes). **Contracts.** RL6, RL8, RL9, RL10, OX10 (provider).
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** done: its last milestone, M4 (the reaping gate's sminos side), landed as M4a and M4b (Decision Log 215); the Tracking Map has every landing.

### O13: `web-cards`, the phone (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** Keep the owner's phone loop through the cut. Desk-web keeps push and the question and permission cards, and nothing else. It now runs as a `web-cards` device: RL2 over WebCrypto, with a WebAuthn assertion on every answer. So the phone keeps answering sessions on machines that took the cutover.

**Acceptance.**
1. **Registration.** The phone registers as a `web-cards` device: a non-extractable WebCrypto P-256 key plus a passkey. It is approved from the owner's Mac and listed in the org state.
2. **A full round trip.** On a cut-over machine, a permission prompt and a question each:
   - push to the phone;
   - show their card contents over the phone's channel;
   - are answered with a passkey gesture.
   The machine checks that the assertion's challenge is the action's digest and that the member owns the session. This holds for one lane A and one lane B session. The push reaches the owner's phone and no other member's device, which O11's staging run could not show live (Decision Log 39).
3. **The class's bound.** Send, steer, spawn, commands and every other action are refused at the endpoint for this class. It may query the outcomes of its own answer acts (`session.answer`, `sminos.answer`, `sminos.decide`) on a session its member owns, as O10 M5's review set (Decision Log 64); lane A's query refusal stands (Decision Log 79).
4. **Side by side.** Sessions on machines not yet moved keep their DX6 cards on the same phone.
5. **The rest freezes.** Desk-web's other views are frozen.

**Edges.** Blocked by O10 and O11. Ships before the cutover of the first machine whose sessions the owner answers from the phone; in the recommended M1, that's the mini. Before O14's B4, desk-web deploys at or after O14 M5a's `org-protocol`, with a way to read the phone's running build (Decision Log 75).
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator. **Contracts.** RL2, RL4, RL5 (device entry), OX8/RL11 (push registration).
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** executing; document landed (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-o13-web-cards.md`, PR #241, Decision Log 69); its M4 needed O10 M7 and M8, both landed (Decision Logs 73 and 77); production's org is rooted and pinned (Decision Log 119).

### O14: Cutover (desk side) — brief per machine

**Purpose.** Move each machine to `mawsd` and RL1 for good without breaking the phone or the logs, then retire the shared org key. The order is the devcloud acceptance VM, then the mini, then the MacBook.

**Acceptance, per machine.**
1. **Re-enrollment.** The machine re-enrolls in place under its name, through a member's code or a delegation. A devcloud VM's ref takes its delegation's id from the seed at first run, and a revoked delegation's VMs re-enroll under a new delegation with a new prefix (Decision Logs 30 and 31). A delegated VM gets its delegation's private key from devcloud's secret store through a v2 provisioning code that O14 builds, carrying `enrollDelegated`'s fields, the redemption token as well as the delegation key (Decision Logs 35 and 45). It is listed in RL5, and executes nothing until the state arrives.
2. **History.** Its history is re-attested once. The app draws its old events as "attested at migration by `<machine>`".
3. **Single-trust inbound.** From the cutover on, DX6 and DX1 execution input is refused. Outbound events are dual-coded until the key retires, so machines not yet moved still receive its events.
4. **The phone.** The phone keeps answering its sessions: through O13 if it's phone-answered, otherwise through DX6 until it moves. A re-enrollment in place ends the machine's DX2 at its redemption, so the phone's loop for that machine pauses until it is listed and cut over, inside a window the owner names: 30 minutes, entered with no session waiting and nothing owed, a card test within 10 minutes of the cutover, and an undo to DX2 within 60 minutes (the remote layer's Decision Log 29; Decision Log 75).
5. **sminos.** Its sminos is local-only (O12). O12 phase 2's sminos is installed on the machine before its endpoint enrolls, since an older sminos's DX2 uplink takes the hub's per-name generation and drops the endpoint's RL1 socket (4409). `hub.json` and the legacy key stay until the final brief, and undoing a cutover is O14's one idempotent sequence, the same before or after sminos's welcome (Decision Log 75, amending Decision Log 51's bare removal of `daemon/identity.json`). The sminos floor for a cutover is 1.27.3: 1.27.0 brought the `sminos daemon owed` that the nothing-owed gate runs (Decision Log 80), and 1.27.3 the operator writer's wait for the upstream's head at a re-enrolment (Decision Log 96). A machine is cut over only once its endpoint's enrollment is org-listed: a MAWS build from master runs an endpoint long before O14 enrolls it, and that alone never takes the machine off the desk hub (Decision Log 55).

**The final brief.** Once the last machine and the phone have moved:
- the org key is retired;
- the hub deletes the wrapped copies;
- the endpoint ignores DX1;
- dual-coding stops;
- DX2 and DX6 leave the hub.

**Gates.** The mini through mini-ops at the owner's request. Any hub migration, Access change or secret waits for the owner. Hub v2's first production deploy runs from steps kept in the repo, in `apps/desk-hub/deploy/` beside O11's staging runners, never from wrappers under `/tmp` (Decision Log 40).
**Edges.** Blocked by O10, O11 and O12. The mini is also blocked by O13 and by mini-ops' sminos upgrade.
**Owner:** desk-sminos-orchestrator.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** document landed (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-o14-cutover.md`, PR #249, Decision Log 75): a shared build, M1 to M5b, and the briefs B0 to B4; the shared build executing.

### O16: The holder — plan

**Purpose.** Harden the engine host as the holder of the holder design (§3.1–§3.3; owner F3, 2026-10-24): a session host per lane B process, the output seq ring and stdin offset, the writer fence, roster re-adoption, and the verified-empty 24-hour release (F2), so an engine host on a server that never idles can be upgraded without ending a session.
**Acceptance.** Acceptance 13's engine-host case on the mini and the VM; the verified-empty release replaces the servers' wall-clock rule, armed through the observer bridge's `turn.step` gate; the `-p` reaping gate is measured (U6); the session host's language and its darwin-arm64 and Linux builds are recorded; OX7 holds with the session host's hop.
**Edges.** Blocked by O3, and by O12 (its item 7, the lane B observer). Blocks O15.
**Contracts.** OX1, OX6, OX7, OX10 (consumer). It keeps O10's lane B action ledger (`src/shared/host/op-ledger.ts`) where the effect lands when it re-homes lane B processes into session hosts (Decision Log 45).
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Status.** execution document landed (PR #394, master b91f19d60, Decision Log 167): seven milestones; M0 landed (PR #401, master c46a16289, Decision Log 169), M1 built, M2 and M5 executing, one landing at M6; O12's M4 builds the gate's sminos side against OX10 as adopted.

### O17: The agent's browser with no app, and its remote view — plan

**Purpose.** Give a session's agent a browser on its own machine when no app is attached there, with the render host serving cua as a windowless tab engine, and let a person on another machine see and operate that tab by screencast, so the tab still reaches the session machine's localhost (§6's browser control, survey §7.5; §13 U3, settled by O5 M7: promote, offscreen, `spikes/u4-render-service/FINDINGS.md` §5.6; about 6,100 new or changed lines by O17's document, an estimate and not a promote bar: local 4,475, remote 1,590, range about 4,900 to 7,300).
**Acceptance.** The local stage closes items 1 to 9, the remote stage item 10.
1. **The first browser on a machine with no app.** On a Mac on the daemon shape with the render runtime and no app attached, a lane B session started through the ordinary start path gets the daemon's socket in `CUA_BROWSER_BACKENDS` (a stub engine that comes ready: O5 M5's unresolved e2e), and `session.capabilities` reads `browser: {available: true, viewed: 'screencast'}` before Chromium starts. cua's inventory answers `[]` from the cold broker with `render.starts` 0. cua's real creation sequence (`windows.query`, `windows.create`, `tabs.create`) starts the render host and gets its tab within `CUA_BROWSER_START_MS` (4,000 ms, or the request's smaller remaining budget). The agent navigates to that machine's localhost, attaches, screenshots and closes the tab, and `render.starts` reads 1 afterwards, with no artifact made or fetched before. A rejected peer gets no hello, loads no broker chunk and starts nothing; no capability read, inventory read or app attachment loads the broker, feed or render chunk. This amends O5's acceptance 3 for a machine with a usable runtime; a machine with neither runtime nor app still refuses as O5 ships it. With an app attached, M5's relay is unchanged, and O5 M5's scene 3 is extended: A's own relay works while A's peer reaches neither B's app nor B's render-host backend.
2. **The cold broker is bounded** (draft §M; the review's P2). Admitted-cold connections have a per-session and a daemon budget, cold buffered bytes an aggregate daemon budget, and one connection's pending bytes `CUA_PROMOTION_MAX_BYTES` (1 MiB). The broker stops reading while a write is backpressured and resumes on `drain`. Tests over a real socket: an inventory flood with the reader stopped, and fragmented input across many connections, each holding the daemon at its budget and closing the offender. Promotion: one suppressed hello and one delivered request for a fragmented or coalesced trigger with following bytes; a start timeout answers the triggering RPC with an error and closes; an old asynchronous ready after a handover cannot splice; O5 M5's `PENDING_BUDGET`, extended (8 per session, 64 per daemon), counts peer-root waits and promotions as one counter.
3. **Handover is destructive and generation-fenced.** With an app attaching during the start, an active lease, an in-flight browser command and a concurrent export: the session's epoch advances; every old waiter settles once; cua sees `lost` and retries into the app within its 5 s cycle; the render host's tabs for that session close, and their hold clears only after the drop is confirmed; another session's tabs and the export survive. The same holds through attach, quit and reattach, and for a reused pid with another start. An app leaving returns the path to cold service, never to an automatic Chromium start.
4. **The release fence** (`host.browserWork`; its seam with O16 under Contracts). A session holding a headless tab is released neither by the budget release nor by the reap's verified-empty step while it holds it. At its due time the idle release (the machine role's clock, 10 minutes on a laptop and 24 hours on a server, `host.exitWhenIdle` included) first has `mawsd` close the session's headless tabs, and proceeds once the hold clears without restarting its clock. At zero tabs each release proceeds; one session's close leaves another's hold. The fence is acquired and acknowledged before any zero-to-one tab transition, and a failed fence creates no tab. After an engine-host reconnect, and in an O16 successor, a session adopted before `mawsd`'s first snapshot on that link reads `work.holds.browser: 'unknown'` until the snapshot applies, never empty in between; one adopted later takes the kept snapshot's verdict. The compatibility matrix: an app's tabs, from any build, keep today's release behaviour (at a release an agent tab stays in the panel as the person's tab), by rule and not as a gap; an engine host without `host.browserWork` gets no headless browser, `browser` reading unavailable with the reason "the engine host on <machine> predates browser work holds; it is replaced when idle". The review's test: a legacy app takes over, and a reconnect or adoption happens before any browser-work report.
5. **A retained headless tab closes after recovery** (the review's P1). The creating cua connection is lost, a cua host reconnects and closes the retained last tab, the render seam's hold and `host.browserWork`'s hold both clear, and the render host exits after its quiet period. The app's protection of the person's tabs is unchanged.
6. **The producer ends one subscription on its own** (the review's P2). After a successful `open`, a terminal notice bound to connection, epoch and subscription ends that subscription (`frame_too_large`, a destroyed or suspended view, a producer failure), disposing its pending operations and pressed inputs exactly once. Test: the local viewer over the real machine-local wire, failing after a successful open.
7. **M7's seven conditions** (FINDINGS §5.6; O5 Decision Log "M7's verdict"), each with its test:
   1. Tab views are offscreen at 1280×800 in their nonshown sizing container, main tabs and adopted popups alike, with no window shown and no Dock icon.
   2. Viewer frames come from Electron's frame subscription, one unacknowledged frame per viewer, the newest unsent image kept. A 60 Hz page read by a 2 fps viewer for 60 s keeps at most one unsent source image and one encoded frame per viewer; the image after each acknowledgement is from the latest paint within 250 ms; queues and heap stay flat. Pass 3's scene (the stream across a cua screenshot, detach and reattach) repeats on Mac and Linux.
   3. One bounded macOS offscreen-clock probe (one CI-opt slot, at most 20 minutes, under `spikes/u3-remote-browser/`, not a gate) records its remedy or the limit: about 110 ms click to paint, 100 ms timers near 6 Hz, `requestAnimationFrame` measured.
   4. No `powerSaveBlocker` on the product path.
   5. A uid peer policy for MAWS's cua server on Linux; on darwin a missing addon still fails closed.
   6. `CuaEventScope`: with two consumers on one tab debugger, the cua host receives only what its own lease asked for, and a screenshot gets its frame from its own `Page.startScreencast`, and cua's `Page.enable` at a tab's first use subscribes no frames.
   7. A held tab's footprint is background work, recorded outside the idle scene (Mac physical footprint, Linux RSS; M7 read 134,000 to 178,000 KiB and 0.5 to 0.6 GiB).
8. **Linux reads unavailable, with its reason.** On a Linux VM with the runtime and no app, `browser` reads `{available: false, reason}` naming cua's missing Linux peer policy until cua's owner pins a build with that policy and a two-repo scene passes. MAWS's Linux primitives are proved through the protocol fixture alone, and no artifact claims a real Linux agent browser before that scene. Acceptance 10's browser clause holds on the VM by this refusal.
9. **The machine's cost and boundary** (acceptance 14 and 15; OX7). On the local stage's merge tree, on the mini under CI-opt's hold:
   - `e2e:daemon-idle` three valid runs from a cold daemon start: no session listener, no broker or feed chunk, no new timer.
   - `e2e:daemon-idle-render` three runs with a browser warm-to-idle case: a real cua connector kept across two quiet periods and two 5 s retry intervals; at the window no render child, tab, frame timer, pending broker request or browser-work hold; the resident broker code, the quiescent listener and the connected cua socket counted, never excluded.
   - A kept-session, no-app scene: each enrolled session's one bound, quiescent listener (no timer, no polling, no Chromium), its cost read.
   - Each within the bound in force (99,145 KiB, Decision Log 128) by two of three; `e2e:local-latency-daemon` once within OX7.
   - Every new socket holds RL §5.11: symlink, foreign-owner, direct A-to-B and stale-epoch attempts (replies from a connection no longer selected for the session, or for a request already settled; the engines carry no epoch on the machine-local wire) against both backend roots are refused.
10. **The remote view** (O5's "the agent's browser for a remote session follows U3's spike outcome"; O5 acceptance 7's promoted half). Two profiles, B with no app. A opens B's session and sees the tab B's agent made against B's localhost, inventory and pixels under `view`. After a `machine.browserControl` grant (`approve` on the session, bound to the session and its epoch, revocable on its own and never implied by `view`; a non-owner steps up once per grant), A's input changes the page and the next frame shows it. Also:
    - a `view`-only member sees the tab and never obtains input;
    - a revoked grant drops queued input and releases pressed state before any further input;
    - B's app attaching mid-lease closes A's stream with `engine_changed`, and A then views the app-owned agent tabs through the same producer;
    - another session's tab is unreachable;
    - a slow carrier holds one frame plus 4 KiB and shows the newest frame, frame credit refunded only after draw or drop;
    - a hidden Browser tool closes its feed, not B's tab;
    - a peer without `rl2.browser-screencast` draws a reasoned unsupported view.

    The live mini-to-MacBook surface is O7's scheduled sitting.
**Edges.** Blocked, both stages, by O5's landing 2 (the daemon-owned cua socket and relay, `session.capabilities`, M7's verdict). The local stage's landing is blocked by O16's landing, since the fence edits O16's engine-host files (O17's document, route item R4: `budget.ts`, `work-state.ts`, `idle-release.ts`, `process-table.ts`, `launcher.ts` and others; `holder/reap.ts` tests only). The remote stage is blocked by O7 M5 (the session-bound read open on O7 §N, with the `browser.screencast` kind and `rl2.browser-screencast` reserved); O10 accepted `machine.browserControl` at `approve` on 2026-11-01. A Linux agent browser is blocked by cua's owner (a pinned peer policy and a two-repo scene, cua #242; the broker's protocol behaviours under contract tests, cua #251); the local stage lands without it. Blocks nothing: O7 M5 lands without the browser kind but reserves its name, and O15 does not wait on O17 (Required).
**Contracts.** OX1 (every name additive; `host.browserWork`, `browser.engine` and `rl2.browser-screencast` are capabilities, with the matrix of item 4), OX5 (browser work is not interest; the render host reports none), OX7, OX9 (the engines' commands in a new namespace `browserEngine`, uniform `internal`, so `browser` stays uniform `client`; `host.browserWork` `internal`; the `browser.inventory` feed at `view` in place of a `machine.browserInventory` act; `machine.browserControl` `approve`; `browser.remote.*` `client`, never offered remotely), RL2 (the `browser.screencast` kind), RL4 (the `machine.browserControl` act), RL §5.11. Neither the engine (D-1) nor cua changes for the broker or the event scope.
- **The fence's seam with O16** (O16's answer, 2026-11-01; it replaces the draft §P's adoption and journal text). No generic hold set exists. All three releases pass through one chokepoint, `holdsWork(s)` in `src/engine-host/budget.ts`, which reads `status.work`, a `WorkState` (`src/engine-host/work-state.ts`: `{crons, unknown, descendants}`; `unknown` already counts as work).
  - The budget release reaches it through `hardReasons()`, the idle release through `isIdleRow`, and O16's verified-empty release through `isVerifiedEmpty` (`src/engine-host/holder/reap.ts`).
  - O17 adds a per-session external-hold map to `WorkState`, surfaced as the additive status field `work.holds?.browser`, `'held'` or `'unknown'`. `holdsWork` is true while any entry is present. `budget.ts` folds it into `background_tasks`, with no new `HardReason`. The idle exception (O17's D20): `IdleRelease`'s clock reads the row as if no browser hold were present; at its due time a row held only by the browser hold waits with no timer and emits `host.browserIdle`, `mawsd` drops the session's headless tabs, and the row is reaped right after the status change that clears the hold, its clock not restarted.
  - The map is fed by `host.browserWork`: a full current snapshot, `internal`, sent on `mawsd`'s permanent engine-host link and refused from forwarded callers. A link that ends turns its held entries `unknown` until the next snapshot.
  - Nothing enters O16's checkpoint or journal. `createAdopter` (`src/engine-host/launcher.ts`, beside M3a's `host_gap`) marks a session adopted before the link's first snapshot `browser: 'unknown'` until that snapshot applies; one adopted later (O16 adopts in the background, `adoptAtStart`) takes the verdict of the snapshot the engine host keeps, which is per process, so a successor starts with none. `mawsd` sends that snapshot first on every fresh engine-host link, as it sends `sendInterest(true)` (`src/mawsd/daemon.ts:751`).
  - O16 builds none of this; O17 edits those files after O16 lands.
- **The remote stage's surfaces with O7 and O10.** O17's document (§K, route item R1) is the proposal to O7 M5: the kind's envelope, the session-bound read open, the `browser.inventory` feed and the one-frame backlog exception. O7 M5 owns the session-bound read open, which its own `http` and `document` streams need, and reserves the kind and `rl2.browser-screencast`; O17 M9 builds the browser kind on it after O7 M5 lands, with no joint landing. O10 accepted `approve` with four pins for §K and M9's tests: a non-owner's step-up per grant and per epoch, web-cards devices refused, the act idempotent under the endpoint ledger's `orphanAtOpen` with the grant dying with its epoch, and input re-authorized from the Authorizer's record read.
**Required.** Not for parent acceptance, by the owner's answer of 2026-11-01. Acceptance 10's browser clause holds by O5's refusal (and on Linux by item 8's), and acceptance 4 names no agent browser. O17 carries §6's intent that browser control moves onto the machine past the bar P5 set, and O15 records any stage not yet landed as carried. The owner declined the alternative, reading §5's "everything the panel shows for a local session works for a remote one" as binding on the Browser tool, which would have added the agent's tab viewed and operated from a remote app to acceptance 4 and made O15 wait on both stages.
**Review.** The xhigh panel runs at both stage landings, by the owner's answer of 2026-11-01. At the local stage's, it reviews everything but M7 while M7 waits for O16's landing; M7's range and the master merge then get reviewer-high, and a second panel is the architect's call if M7 rewrites much of what the panel read (Decision Log 196).
**Status.** section written from O5's `[cut]` (Decision Log 187); execution document landed (PR #430, master ab0c95788, Decision Log 189): ten milestones, the local stage M1 to M8 as one landing (M7 after O16's landing), the remote stage M9 and M10; executing since O5's landing 2 (Decision Log 191), the local stage's M7 waiting for O16's landing.

### O18: Message reach (desk side) — plan

**Purpose.** Decide who may message a session's agent, by the owner's direction of 2026-11-05 (Decision Log 219) and ruling (Decision Log 223). A person lists a session so other people's agents can talk to it, keeps it unlisted so no one else's can, or opens it to selected people. Today none of this holds: a peer `say` is never checked for reach, sminos sends no session's visibility, and every org machine's logs carry every session's lifecycle events, so every session is listed in effect. Needed before a second member joins.

**Design** [binding: the owner's ruling, and the joint view of the sminos, endpoint and hub halves]:
- **Reach is visibility.** A member's agents may message a session when the member's rank on it is at least `list` (`src/endpoint/grades.ts`). A `listed`, `org-view` or `org-converse` session takes messages from any member's agents, and a `private` one only from its owners and the members it is shared with. A share gains the grade `list`, shown as "Can message": the member's agents may message the session, and the member cannot read it. `listed` stays the default.
- **Same owner first.** A sender whose verified owners intersect the recipient's resolved owners is never restricted. Owners are per session, so a session someone else spawned on my machine is theirs. An operator event (`<machine>/human`, or a `say` from a terminal outside any session) counts as from its machine's owners, as `words?` reads it.
- **The receiving machine's endpoint decides.** Its sminos asks `reach?`, beside `words?`, in `deliver_next` before the push, for every peer kind (`say`, `send` and wakes). The ask carries the event's writer, its `verified` block and the recipient. An endpoint that can't be asked leaves the delivery owed. An unverified sender reaches only a `listed` or org-wide session. sminos holds no grade logic.
- **The sender's check is resolution.** A sender's projection holds only the sessions its person may reach. A tag naming an unlisted session fails as a missing one does, and a broadcast wakes only the sessions it sees.
- **A refusal happens before delivery.** The hub refuses at upload any event that names a session its org-wide policy registry doesn't know, or whose audience doesn't admit the event, with the same `not-reachable` for a hidden session as for a nonexistent one. The refusal returns to the sender over its own upload path as a notice, and a content-free tombstone keeps the sender's writer contiguous. The hub can only refuse: the receiving endpoint's `reach?` still authorizes each delivery (Decision Log 224).
- **Unlisted means the logs don't carry it.** Each event of a private session carries a signed `audience`: everyone whose rank on it is at least `list`. That covers its lifecycle events, the events addressed to it and the events it writes. Such events go to writer streams that each carry one audience, and the hub serves a stream only to machines whose owners intersect its audience, through one read check on every surface (Decision Log 224). Going private writes an org-wide `unlisted` event, and a removed share writes one for that member; a new share gets a fresh snapshot. History already fanned out is not taken back, and the UI says so when a session goes private. Hiding a session in the UI alone is a stopgap while the org has one member, and it does not meet "unlisted".

**Design** [advisory]: the desk track's draft, carried into O18's document with the alternatives it rejected: a separate reach setting (declined by the owner), a grade check inside sminos, an outcome lane through the hub, and a private project per person.

**Acceptance.** On the staging org with a second member (acceptance 12's test org):
1. A person's own sessions message each other across machines, whatever their visibility.
2. The second member's agent resolves and messages a `listed` session. The message arrives as a peer message with its owner label and counts as nobody's words.
3. Once a session goes `private` with no shares, the second member's machine receives none of its later events. `sminos list --org`, `find` and `@short` don't name it, and the machine's log files carry none. A `say` forged, or sent from a stale view, is refused before delivery (at the hub's upload, with the recipient's endpoint as defense in depth; Decision Log 224), and the sending session gets the `not-reachable` notice.
4. A "Can message" share lets the second member's agents see and message the session, while the member's transcript read is refused. Removing the share drops the session from their projection.
5. With the recipient's endpoint unreachable, a peer message stays owed and is delivered once the endpoint answers.
6. No mix of versions the org can run fans a private session's event out to a machine outside its audience.
7. A private session's agent messaging a session outside its audience is refused at its own CLI, and nothing is written.
8. From an agent's shell, widening a session's reach is refused for an agent that follows its instructions, and narrowing it succeeds. The CLI's cutover, which comes before a second member joins, closes widening to every agent that goes through sminos on a machine whose endpoint offers the app's record acts; a machine not cut over keeps the terminal gate (Decision Logs 229, 231 and 235).

**Edges.** Blocked by nothing unlanded. Blocks a second member's joining (Deferred).
**Owner:** p5-desk-track-owner. **Repos:** doperpowers (sminos) and MAWS (the hub, `apps/desk-hub`; the protocol package; the endpoint's `reach?`, audiences and policy cache; desk-web; the `list` share grade; and O8's share picker and help text, O18's M5, which the MAWS architect builds with O8 M6). **Contracts.** RL §6.2 (the `list` share grade), RL9 (the signed `audience`, `refused` and `unlisted` events), RL10 (`reach?`), the hub's fan-out (O11) and OX1. It is cross-repo as O12 is: MAWS lands first, and every new field stays optional.
**Required.** Not for P5's close: the owner tied it to a second member's joining, which P5 defers. O15 records it as carried if it has not landed.
**Review.** reviewer-high, with a security lens, on each landing that touches `reach?` or the hub's fan-out.
**Status.** document landed (PR #483, master 95000f84a, Decision Log 224): M1 to M6 with M2b, M5 an external contract built with O8 M6; M1, the hub, landed (PR #484, master ff1b9d581, Decision Log 225), M2, the endpoint, landed (PR #486, master 617fde4b5, Decision Log 226); M2b landed (PR #487, master 9a3848039, Decision Log 227), desk-web's DX6 client kept frozen; M2's follow-up for M4 landed (PR #489, master a6bae52c0, Decision Log 228); no production hub deploy until every enrolled machine's `mawsd` carries M2 (Decision Log 227); M3 and M4 (sminos 1.35.0 and 1.36.0) built, merging after the post-soak swaps, their records landed (PR #491, master d85d2a42d, Decision Log 229); sminos 1.35.0 to 1.39.0 merged on doperpowers main after the swaps, the CLI's widening cutover included, their records landed (PR #509, master 199a9074b, Decision Log 243); M5's Share sheet landed with O8 M6b (PR #522, master ec2eaf93c, Decision Log 251); M6 passed live on staging (PR #513, master 40f593579, Decision Log 244), so O18's acceptance is met; M1's production hub deploy ran, the owner's, at `hub@14ec1177` (Decision Log 247); board #477.

### O15: Recomposition and the owner's week — plan

**Purpose.** Verify the parent acceptance as a whole, check that O13's deployment admin device is no longer a root once the app's device is (Decision Log 69), delete main's embedded composition root (§2), and record the owner's verdict (acceptance 16).
**The deletion.** After O15 the app is a client of `mawsd` on every profile: no setting, environment variable or failure runs the machine's services inside the app, and the main profile's machine runs under its unit or not at all. When the main profile's `mawsd` cannot start at a launch, MAWS opens a repair window that says why, shows the daemon's last log lines and offers the repair, and runs nothing of the machine's (the owner, 2026-11-10; Decision Log 266); a private daemon that fails, on a development or test profile, shows in the supervisor's status as today. What main keeps of the machine is its answers for an older `mawsd` (OX1), until their own retirement.
**The verification.** The acceptance is checked as 67 claims on one final build, the master that holds the deletion, once it runs on the three machines: by the named gate, by the swap itself, live, on staging, by the real-network bench, or by the owner's hands. A claim that fails is fixed or goes to the owner as a ruling; the architect waives nothing. The results and the owner's verdict, asked from 2026-11-14, are written into Outcomes.
**Edges.** Blocked by every other child, O16 included; not by O17 or O18, which are not required: O15 records any O17 stage, and O18, not yet landed as carried.
**Required.** For parent acceptance.
**Execution.** `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p5-o15-recomposition.md`, M1, M2, M2b and M3 to M5.
**Status.** document landed (Decision Log 266) and amended with M2b, the resume after a wake (Decision Log 267); M1 and M2, the deletion, landed (PR #562, master 10e30f17f, Decision Log 268); M2b, the resume after a wake, landed (PR #567, master f0ff96253, Decision Log 269); the final build, master f1852de3a, runs on the three machines and production's hub (Decision Log 270); the closing run, M4, is run on F under the owner's rulings, with 4m to 4p refused at the bench's precheck, 14a and 14b void by load, row 7 deferred by the owner and the repair window a finding (Decision Log 271); acceptance 14's margin, after the registry's app side landed over it (Decision Log 272), recovered for the render and browser cases by the idle collection's quiet entry, the four-kept case still over by 602 KiB (merge 4109f5e25, Decision Log 273); M5 recorded (Decision Log 274); done at the owner's verdict of 2026-11-14, "완료, 예외는 이월" (Decision Log 276).

## Cross-child contracts

- **RL1–RL10** as the remote layer's Interfaces section states them, lifted whole and binding (RL §Interfaces).
- **OX1 Wire compatibility.** Every hello (app to `mawsd`, `mawsd` to the engine host, endpoint to hub, device to endpoint) keeps the socket preamble's stable fields (`t`, `protocol` as an integer, `token`; `src/shared/host/socket-protocol.ts:6-8`), with `protocol` read as the major, and adds `minor`, `build` and `capabilities` beside them. Peers accept any minor of their major; changes within a major are additive; a removal or rename takes a major, with the old form served for one release. One exception (Decision Log 101): a landing that moves a piece of state's ownership out of the app (O3-D, the spine into `mawsd`) may refuse, within the major, an app's hello that lacks the capability saying it obeys the new owner, since that app would be a second writer; the refused app is told to update, and what it can still write beside the new owner, by hand or in the window before the new owner yields to it, is the transition's stated residual, which the moving landing bounds and measures (Decision Log 163).
- **OX2 Machine-scoped address.** `MachineRef` is a stable id minted once per profile at its first run, by whichever of the app and `mawsd` runs first (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,64}`, at least 128 random bits), kept in `<profile>/machine.json` through one module both use, and never changes, independent of the machine key; RL5's machine entry carries it as `ref`, set at enrollment, immutable and unique, and every contract field named `machine` carries the ref. The enrolled name is shown from the org state. Every machine-bound id travels as `{machine, id}`; layout, drafts and stores key by it; the local machine is a machine like any other. In the renderer an address is the string `<ref>/<id>`, joined and split only by O6's codec, and drafts stay in their session's machine's index, reached through the address (Decision Log 27). Artifacts are the one exception to the key: an artifact id is a v4 UUID and the artifact scheme's origin, so artifact stores and tabs key by the id alone and carry the machine beside it on every row, tab and invoke (Decision Log 195).
- **OX3 Org project.** `OrgProject {id, name, checkouts: [{machine, path, projectId}]}`, derived from sminos's project identity. Every checkout yields a project: a repository with no remote, or a folder with no repository, is a project of its own on its machine rather than folded into the machine's project, so every project the sidebar shows today stays. Only sessions with no checkout belong to their machine's project. A checkout is a project someone opened on its machine; a session belongs to its machine's project when its directory's org project has no checkout on any visible machine, or when it has no project. `OrgProject.id` is a key enrollment doesn't change (`smns:`, `remote:`, `repo:`, `folder:` or `machine:`), and the record carries `kind` and `smnsId`, sminos's id for joins. Persisted keys survive enrollment (Decision Logs 21 and 27). **Amended by Decision Log 265:** an org project is the registry's, grouped by its id, with declared locations stated by each machine's signed record (`docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-11-10-project-registry-design.md` §13); the text above stands until that design's build lands.
- **OX4 Client port.** `{pick, save, confirm, reveal, openExternal, notify, badge, focus}`, called by the daemon through the requesting client; a request with no client answers `noClient` and the caller degrades, never hangs. `openExternal` takes a URL or a path, and `focus` either brings the window forward or requests attention (the dock bounce). `confirm` is a yes/no question parented to the client's window (Decision Log 20).
- **OX5 Interest.** Each client reports its open sessions and subscriptions; the daemon's idle release and visible set read the union.
- **OX6 Session record.** RL6, plus the lane B binding (holder design §3.1) and the inference route.
- **OX7 Local latency budget.** §10. Delta-to-paint is measured as delta to frame: from the engine's write to the end of the frame that renders it on the renderer's main thread, GPU presentation excluded (Decision Log 20).
- **OX8 App ↔ hub.** The app's own hub connection, beside RL2's channels: the org-state chain, signed listings (the sidebar and its toggle), the project log plane (`sminos say` traffic and the project channel's agent rows), push registration and device enrollment. O11 builds the hub half; O6 and O8 consume it; the app verifies RL8 signatures against the org state it holds.
- **OX9 Namespace grades.** Every forwarded namespace declares its grade in the registry: reads of a session's checkout (`fs.read`, `git.diff`, `scm.status`, `artifacts.read`) are `view` on that session; writes (`fs.write`, git mutations, `artifacts.edit`) are `manage`, or `spawn` on the machine; `pty.*` is the machine owners' or an explicit shell right; machine settings, connector management (credentials and grants) and the catalog's machine half are `machine-admin`; a connector widget's leases are `view` on its artifact, with `call` and `refresh` at `converse` by effect, and delivery's `status` and `receipts` are `view` on the artifact, with `send` and `cancel` at `manage` (the owner's answers A13 and A14, Decision Log 197). The endpoint refuses an undeclared namespace. The grades are three kinds, and no kind is ranked against another: the session ranks `list` to `manage` in RL §5.5's order; the machine grades `spawn`, `shell` and `machine-admin`; and the routing classes `client` (served by the app, never sent to a daemon) and `internal` (a daemon's own services only), which no caller holds. A command whose grade depends on its input declares a floor and is graded by its effect at the endpoint (Decision Log 20).
- **OX10 The reaping gate (adopted).** desk-sminos-orchestrator proposed it. O12's M4 provides it and O16 consumes it. It binds from O16's execution document's landing (Decision Log 167), U6 (O16's M0) gating it, with the text of that document's Interfaces §E, which O12's M4 builds against: the engine host's own gate server at `gate.sock`, `origin: 'peer'`, `delivery`, `hello.childPid`, a 5 s reconnect with a fresh `hello`, and the settlement below. The text as proposed, in O12's execution document (M4 and Interfaces §C): the engine host serves `MAWS_HOLDER_SOCKET` (0600). The observer bridge sends `hello` and a 5 s heartbeat. The host arms an acknowledged fence with a lease (`arm {fence, leaseMs}`, answered `armed`) and signals only after `armed` and within the lease. While armed, the observer's `turn.step` asks `turn {fence, origin}` and gets `proceed` or `stop`. With no answer, it proceeds only after the lease has expired plus one heartbeat. A stopped turn's trigger stays owed. That needs a delivery settlement correlated with the turn's verdict, because today sminos's `deliver_next` settles an inbox delivery when `push_live` hands it to the engine, before `turn.step` decides. O16 defines that settlement when it adopts OX10, and O12 builds the sminos side. O16 defines the settlement: sminos defers settling a peer delivery only while the observer holds an armed fence, settles it on `proceed` or the lease's expiry, and keeps it owed on `stop`. A session on which no observer was ever seen may be released on verified-empty alone, on both roles; an observer seen and then silent holds it (Decision Log 167).
- **The protocol package.** RL2's handshake and frames, RL4's canonical action, RL5's org state and RL8's signatures are one package with shared test vectors, `apps/org-protocol` in the `apps/*` workspace. O11 creates it with RL5 and RL8 and O10 extends it with RL2 and RL4. It is imported by the hub, `mawsd`, the app's main and desk-web's WebCrypto build. No consumer reimplements them.

- O11 has no MAWS dependency and starts on day one beside O1; O12's data model (owners, origin, the principal and inference fields, the opId ledger, the lane B observer) starts at once.
- O10's live acceptance needs O11's staging hub; O12's local-only and words-rule parts need O10's RL10.
- O13 ships before the mini's cutover, since the owner answers the mini's sessions from the phone. The MacBook's own sessions are local to its app and need no cutover in M1.
- O12 and O14 change the doperpowers repository as well as MAWS, cross-repo as MW1–MW5 were: MAWS lands first each time, and every new field stays optional.

**Milestones the owner sees.**
- **M1** (O1, O2, O6, O9, O10–O13; the VM's cutover, then the mini's): every session on the MacBook, the mini and the VM in one sidebar, terminal sessions controllable from MAWS, the phone on the new protocol for the mini, and MAWS's own sessions local as today.
- **M2** (O3, O7, the MacBook's cutover; O4 and O5 lighting their capabilities as they land): MAWS sessions at full depth on any machine, outliving the app.
- **M3** (O8, O16, O14's final brief, O15): the org interface, upgrades that end nothing, the shared org key retired, and the owner's week.

## Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The cut degrades daily local use | Every step keeps the app working (survey §3); OX7 stops a step that slows it; the e2e groups run on the daemon shape since O3-B's landing (Decision Log 137; from O2 only the supervisor, unit and latency groups did, Decision Log 101) |
| The wire freezes once main's commands cross a process boundary (settings went v14 to v18 in five days) | OX1 lands first, in O1 |
| S3 is one large step | O3 decomposes at dispatch; S3 moves code verbatim behind O1's seams; two composition roots let the owner's profile flip back |
| Tests or dev runs hit the owner's daemon | One daemon per profile; non-default profiles get private daemons (§2) |
| Retrofitting authorization onto 300 forwarded commands | OX9 declared from O1, enforced in O10 before any namespace crosses a channel |
| Two trust models drift | One document (RL); desk-side children own its code |
| Phone loop broken mid-cutover | O13 before the first phone-answered machine's cutover; O11 keeps DX2 and DX6 for every machine and the phone not yet moved |
| Live tests against the owner's hub | A second deploy, `desk-hub-staging`, with its own Access app, made once at the owner's hand (O11's first step); it is also acceptance 12's test org |
| Acceptance 14's memory bound is tight | Decision Log 57's early check at O2 M5 (07b91743e; mini runs 40533404486, 40535972777 and 40549288255, two more invalid on load alone and agreeing within 0.7%) reads `mawsd` alone, idle with no hosts, at a median of 26481 KiB. With the engine host's 64481 KiB that is 90962 KiB of the 96721 KiB bound: about 5.6 MiB of headroom before M9 adds the relay's buffers and the interest union. O2 M9 measures the daemon shape. If it reads over, look first at `mawsd`'s own 13 MiB above the runtime floor (13153 KiB), what it loads at start O2 M9 measured the daemon shape at 94947 KiB of the 96721 (mawsd 28865 with an adopted upstream, about 2.4 MiB over the mawsd-alone check, plus the engine host's 66081; mini run 40606738519): 1.8% headroom, about M1's run-to-run spread, and local latency's p50 at 25.9 of 26.45 ms. O2 lands on it, and a later miss on noise falls to the two-of-three rule. The first lever is fewer eager imports in mawsd's graph; At landing the bound was re-derived on the landing tree, 101113 KiB and 1.11 s, and the daemon shape read 97683 and 99219 KiB, about 1.9% to spare (Decision Log 107, a tracker row); after the mini's reboot it was re-derived again, 99145 KiB and 1.12 s, and the A-B-A case reads the daemon shape against the detached supervisor inside one job, x1.452 of the x1.5 allowed (Decision Log 128). O3, which moves services toward the daemon, budgets against this bound before it adds to either process. |
| Production's org root is a software key (corrected 2026-11-13, Decision Log 274; the row read "until O8") | It did not end at O8. Since O8 M8 (Decision Log 263) the one root is this Mac's step-up key, and it is still a software key: a P-256 private JWK in `<userData>/device/keys.json` (directory 0700, file 0600, excluded from Time Machine where the exclusion succeeds), beside the device's channel key. Touch ID gates the app's signing flow, not the key: a process running as the owner that can read the profile can read both keys. What O8 ended is the deployment admin device, which the remote layer's Decision Log 28 had bounded (a durable 0600 keys file outside sync and backup scopes, confirmations read from `/dev/tty`): it was revoked once the app's device was a root, and O15 checked it (9x in Outcomes; Decision Logs 69, 263 and 264). What bounds the risk now: disk encryption at rest (FileVault, whose state on the MacBook this record did not read) and the paper recovery key, kept offline. What would end it: keys bound to the Secure Enclave, which need a build signed with a Developer ID and are deferred with it (Deferred). The tracker's row of 2026-11-13 on the org root's key carries it |

## Deferred

- The encrypted recent tail on the hub (lid-closed reading): a later child (owner, 2026-10-26).
- Account-managed inference through a server-side gateway, and with it who may drive another person's session (owner, 2026-10-26).
- Signed builds, Enclave keys, notarization and updates (C12 P1): when the Apple Developer ID arrives.
- Shipping MAWS to a second member.
- The MAWS web port (class `web`).
- Name reuse for an enrolled machine, such as a replaced mini, whose name stays bound to its first ref (Decision Log 23). A devcloud VM on a delegation certificate already frees its name when the certificate retires (Decision Log 24).

## Surprises and discoveries

- The engine's remote-tools channel is gated to managed cloud workers, so client-bound tools go through MAWS's own tool server (RL §3.1).
- Claude Code 2.1.311's "SSH mode" strings are a Desktop entrypoint marker, config metadata and a telemetry name; there is no session service to reuse (remote-architecture's note).
- Moving main freezes the wire: the fingerprint leaves main out on purpose (survey §7.1).
- Anthropic's terms bar making a personal account available to anyone else (identity §3), which bounds what co-owners can do until inference is managed per account.
- Lifting ids to the machine dimension (O6 M2) has a class neither the lowerer's refusal nor the coverage snapshot catches: an id carried in a plain string field, or a compound id built on main's bare session id. Four were silent with one machine: `session.takeOver.paneId` (the pause-first takeover never suspended the CLI), mark ids and engine event ids (Mark read matched nothing, side threads' later summaries were dropped), and session ids inside artifact content (dashboard labels read "by Claude"). O6 carries the check into M3 and M4; any child that builds an id on a session id or carries one in a string checks it at the boundary too.
- The endpoint's Notifier (presence counts, notify's waiting and settled) is fed only by RL10's pending, and sminos's pending set holds only bridge-raised requests, which are lane A's. Lane B's broker items reach the endpoint only inside a plane B subscription, so with no phone subscribed a lane B session shows no count and raises no waiting push. O13 M5 found it (2026-10-28); it breaks O13 acceptance 2's lane B push leg and would have met the mini at O14's B2. O10's executor feeds the same Notifier from the engine host's broker items for every listed lane B session, subscribed or not, restated whole on each engine-host reconnect, bounded with no polling, additive on the wire; it lands in its own PR (or in O10 M10 if it needs O2) before O14's B2. It landed as PR #272 (master 96b86b5c9, Decision Log 90). O13 M5 lands with lane A proven and lane B's push leg open for that fix, and its fake sminos drops the lane B pending entries that modelled a contract sminos does not have.

## Decision Log

1. **2026-10-26, owner.** Desk folds into MAWS; MAWS is the one interface, in the org shape; Desk Web pauses; the web port comes later; sessions belong to their spawner, cooperation runs through agents.
2. **2026-10-26, owner, round 1.** The org is the owner alone for months, with the people model built in; an Apple Developer ID is coming; a remote session's controller lives on its machine; the phone loop stays; agent messages are today's `sminos say` with an owner label; the sidebar shows one's own sessions with others behind a toggle; the lid-closed tail is a later child; an offline recovery key; no step-up on routine approvals of one's own session.
3. **2026-10-26, owner, through remote-architecture.** Per-session visibility like a shared document; shares at view, converse or approve; machine owners' grants; app first.
4. **2026-10-26, owner.** Keep this design's credential model; account-managed inference comes in a later phase.
5. **2026-10-26, the architect.** The endpoint is a module of `mawsd`, not a process; the engine host stays separate (RL Decision Log 6).
6. **2026-10-27, the architect.** Main's services go to a services host that is the engine host's single client (survey §3), not into the supervisor. Rejected: the merge, which puts synchronous SQLite on the supervisor's loop and rewrites every single-client row.

7. **2026-10-27, desk-sminos-orchestrator's review, adopted.** M1 includes the mini (with O13 before its cutover); O11 starts on day one; O10's live parts wait for O11; the app ↔ hub contract is OX8; one protocol package owned by O10; to-human messages assembled per machine over channels; a staging hub; U9. Its text for O10–O14 is the children's sections.

8. **2026-10-27, the critique's round, adopted** (doperpowers:critique, converged): the holder is scheduled, not assumed; namespace grades (OX9); one daemon per profile, two composition roots until M2, and a `mawsd` command line; O3 owns the data migration; the tailnet is a first-class carrier of RL2 (RL at 65d7fa11e); O7 no longer waits for O4 and O5; one Electron process or two is U4's to decide by a stated criterion; `MachineRef` is a minted id; OX1 keeps the socket preamble; per-role idle release; acceptance 2, 4, 7 and 12 reworded and 13–15 added; `show_pane` follows the sending device.
9. **2026-10-27, the architect.** The holder is child O16 in P5, not a later phase: an engine host with live sessions on a server may never idle out, so without re-adoption an upgrade either never takes effect or ends every lane B session. Rejected: deferring it, which the critique first proposed and conceded.
10. **2026-10-27, the architect.** O6 is verified against two daemons on one Mac, each with its own `MachineRef` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, not through a development adapter on the old hub. Rejected: the adapter, which would be thrown away.

11. **2026-10-27, owner.** The design is approved. The project channel shows question and permission cards inline. The MacBook releases an idle session 10 minutes after the last interest, and servers after 24 hours. Release must be strict: no background Bash job, subagent, Monitor, cron or MCP task (§2, O2).
12. **2026-10-27, the architect: the verification.** The design was reviewed by doperpowers:critique (converged, Decision Log 8–10) and by desk-sminos-orchestrator (Decision Log 7). The remote layer had its own adversarial review and two code reviews. Each plan child's execution document gets doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer's buildability review before it executes. Each child's branch gets doperpowers:review-code at the rung its execution document names, medium by default and xhigh for O3.
13. **2026-10-27, owner, under O11.** The staging hub is approved. desk-sminos-orchestrator creates `desk-hub-staging` on the owner's Cloudflare account with the stored Access setup tokens: a second Worker with its own Access apps and its own push key, reusing the service-token API token. The owner signs in to it once and runs `cloudflared access login` for it once.
14. **2026-10-27, the architect, from remote-architecture's check against the code and the holder design (PR #184).** Strict release names its sources (§2, O2 item 3). Ambient tasks don't count, keeping E10's rule. A workflow, the engine's fifth task kind, counts. Crons are read from Stop's `session_crons` through a new Stop hook callback, because nothing reads them today. An unreadable state is narrowed to three cases. Rejected: counting ambient tasks, which would keep any session with an auto-started watcher from ever releasing. Flags O2 and O16.
15. **2026-10-27, the architect.** Each child gains Required and Status fields, per the composite template of doperpowers:decomposing 7.208.0, which this spec had followed without them. No decision changes.
16. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O11, O12 and O16, from desk-sminos-orchestrator's execution documents (branch p5-o11-o12-plans, d012d84c9).** The protocol package is `apps/org-protocol`. O11 creates it, because O11 starts first, and O10 extends it. It uses WebCrypto only and imports with `.ts` extensions so Node loads it unbundled. O12 follows RL6's split of `principal` and `inference` and its `@machine` token (remote layer Decision Log 17, PR #185 landed at 7fcf43afd), so O12's acceptance 1 is reworded. The lane B marker (`MAWS_LANE=B` from `buildEnvironment` in `src/engine-host/launch.ts`) holds only in the process the engine host launched. sminos's `launch_env` copies `os.environ` into every `claude --bg` it starts, and a lane B session's Bash passes its environment to any `claude` it runs, so a marker read from the environment alone would silence the controls of sessions MAWS never drove. OX10, the reaping gate, is added as proposed, with a stopped turn's trigger kept owed. Flags O10, O12 and O16.
17. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O11 and O12, at their execution documents' landing (PR #186, master 1068fe922).** Under O11: RL1 carries DX2's log-plane vocabulary unchanged beside the channel multiplex, so the Project DO's lease, backlog and writer rule stay. Rejected: a new log-plane protocol, which would fork the Project DO. Its buildability review's eight findings are adopted, among them a v2 log signature that binds org and project, a state cache that fails closed, Access admission that follows the org state, and RL1's hibernation state tested across eviction. M7 settles U9 by a spike and doesn't touch the MAWS app. Under O12: a spawned child's provenance travels with its launch as a pending record the child claims once (`SMINOS_LAUNCH`). The controller lane is current state, kept apart from provenance. opIds get a replay horizon (`expired`, `opCreated`), and `opCreated` is asked of RL4. OX10 is revised to an acknowledged fence with a lease, so a host never signals while the observer believes a turn may proceed. Phase 2's milestones are written when O10 fixes RL10. O12's re-review of the folded milestones is still out, and its findings land on the same document.
18. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10 and O12, from the remote layer's Decision Log 18 (PR #187, master 1c5637466).** RL4 gains `opCreated`. It is set once with the `opId`, kept across reseals, and covered by the seal and the canonical action. An op older than `OP_HORIZON = KEPT_S − 3600` s is refused `expired`. An `opCreated` more than 60 s later than `created` is malformed. `query {opId, opCreated}` gains the answers `expired` and `unavailable`. O10's acceptance names the horizon, and the app's outcome-unknown states (O7) follow RL4. Flags O7, O10 and O12.
19. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O12 and O16, at O12's second buildability round (PR #188, master 8af7513de).** O12 keeps provisional ownership out of the published log. The launch claim is SessionStart's first act. The lane B verdict has three states, and an unresolved verdict stays an observer. `opCreated` is required with every opId. The round found that OX10 as proposed can't keep a stopped turn's peer message owed: `deliver_next` settles at push, before `turn.step` decides. OX10's adoption therefore includes a settlement correlated with the verdict, which O16 defines and O12 builds. Flags O16.
20. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O1, at its execution document (master a768c31ef).** O1's nineteen residue decisions are accepted as its document records them. Among them: one gone moment per page instance for all per-client state; O1 retags no production namespace; the forwarding seam carries the client (`req.client`, `clientGone`); protocol 3 with protocol 2 served for one major; additive means new commands, events and optional fields only, so an added enum member is breaking; a tolerant reader at cross-build receivers, the one exception to charter §12's never-transform rule; and one committed wire manifest. Contracts: OX4 gains `confirm`. OX7 is measured as delta to frame. OX9's vocabulary has three kinds that are never ranked against each other, a condition the architect added so that O10 can't rank `internal` below `list`. Charter X2's owners gain `machine`. OX1's proof at O1 is two registries a minor apart over the real socket in one process, because no earlier protocol-3 build exists. The real two-build check waits for the first change that bumps `WIRE_MINOR`. O1's review rung is high, not Decision Log 12's medium default: every later child builds on OX1's rule and the forwarding seam. Rejected: medium, for a panel-sized branch at a compatibility boundary.
21. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O6, O2, O3 and O11, from O6's planner (a `[parent-impact]` and three overturns).** The edge: O6's two-machine check needs machine services that answer the sidebar's main-owned reads (`session.list`, `session.projects.list`, `session.digests`, `attention.list`, drafts), and neither a detached supervisor nor O2's `mawsd` serves them; only O3's services host does. O6 stays blocked by O1 only. Its stand-in is a second profile's app serving its machine services over a same-uid socket on protocol 3, which a packaged build can't serve. Its serve adapter is deleted at O3, whose acceptance now reruns the scene on the daemon shape. Ids are addresses in the renderer, lifted and lowered at its client by branded-schema identity, with a coverage test for unbranded id fields, one codec, and a loud failure for a payload that mixes machines. Rejected: §3's id-to-machine map, which needs the same schema walk plus a mutable index that misses an id seen before its record. `MachineRef` is minted once and never changes, independent of the machine key, because the key moves to the Secure Enclave with signed builds. RL5's machine entry gains `ref`, set at enrollment and immutable after (asked of the remote layer and O11). Rejected: the key's fingerprint, which would change at enrollment and again at the Secure Enclave move. OX3: every checkout yields a project on its machine, and only sessions with no checkout go to the machine's project, keeping acceptance 1's every project present. Persisted keys survive enrollment, because sminos's repository id includes the org. Flags O2 (mints the ref), O3, O10 and O11.
22. **2026-10-27, the architect, correcting Decision Log 21's reasoning, from the remote layer's Decision Log 19 (PR #189, master f94f56b8c).** The machine key stays a 0600 file under RL §2.3, because a headless daemon can't use the Secure Enclave. So the Enclave move concerns device keys, unless U4 makes `mawsd` a signed Electron process. The ref's independence stands on the enrollment and rotation grounds: a key change keeps the ref. RL5 fixes the ref's format and its rules. Every contract field named `machine` carries the ref, never the name. The enrollment MAC covers the ref, and a contested ref is refused for every claimant. O6's codec and O2's mint follow it. No decision changes.
23. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O11, O12 and O14, from the remote layer's Decision Log 20 (PR #190, master 06da0ef8f).** Log writers stay `<name>/…`. RL5 gains an append-only `machineNames: {name, ref}[]`, so a name is held under one ref for the life of the org, and RL8 verifies a machine's event only when that binding matches the signer. The visible costs are accepted for now. A used name never passes to another machine, so a replacement mini or a recreated VM takes a new name, and a rename splits the machine's log history. Reuse after retirement is deferred, because no machine is retired in this initiative. Rejected for now: name transfer, which needs a retirement transition and the log position of each retirement.
24. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10, O11 and O14, from the remote layer's Decision Log 21 (PR #191, master a2aeed0e0).** Retirement and revocation are distinct. Revocation is for a key that may be compromised, and it is retroactive. Retirement is for a key that leaves honestly: it records `{keyId, notAfter}`, and events stamped at or before `notAfter` verify for good. The anchor is the signer's own timestamp, which is trusted only because a retired key is destroyed. A retired key found leaked is revoked. A delegation certificate can be retired by its own delegation key, so `devcloud vm delete` retires its VM unattended, and a retired certificate frees its name for a recreated VM. The delete hook lives in the devcloud plugin and is advisorily O14's, built with the VM's cutover. Decision Log 23's deferral narrows to enrolled machines.
25. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10, O11 and O12, from the remote layer's Decision Log 22 (PR #192, master bf7e6233a).** A retired entry is `{keyId, pub, notAfter, holder}`. Dropping a retired key revokes it, and a key enters `retired` only in the step where it stops being current. Every v2 event signs its `created`, and retirement compares against that, never the hub's `ts`. A delegation's expiry retires its certificates. The enrollment MAC's encoding (`smns-enroll-mac-v1`) and the `contested` and `name-retired` verdicts are fixed. No P5 text changes.

Its gate was run 40430118784, whose tree equals the head, and master's moves since were docs only. It changes nothing until sminos reads the marker. From the remote layer's Decision Log 23 (PR #193, master 8a944a589): a revoked key never becomes current again. Only a signer holding the whole chain can check that, so it is the admin's app, built in O8, the child that edits the org state. remote-architecture named O7; the Org page is O8's. The hub and every machine that saw the revocation also refuse such a state from their own record.

A profile's `MachineRef` is minted once at its first run, by whichever of the app and `mawsd` runs first, into `<profile>/machine.json` (`{version: 1, ref, mintedAt}`, 0600). (2) OX3's checkout is a project someone opened on its machine (`origin: 'user'`). A session sits under its machine's project when its directory's org project has no checkout on any visible machine, or when it has no project, which keeps today's rule that only opened projects are rows. `OrgProject.id` is a key enrollment doesn't change (`smns:`, `remote:`, `repo:`, `folder:` or `machine:`), and the record gains `kind` and `smnsId`, sminos's id kept for joins. (4) Four id kinds are lifted to addresses (`AppSessionId`, `ProjectId`, `PtyId`, `AttentionId`). The engine's `SessionId`, event ids, ids inside one session and artifact ids are not, and path-only commands take an explicit machine. Rejected: lifting every machine-scoped id, which rewrites every engine frame on the delta path OX7 measures. `machine.identity` is the first production `machine`-owned command and bumps `WIRE_MINOR`. If it is master's first bump, O6's executor tells the architect at M1, and O1's two-build check (the tracker's row) is scheduled then. The new `e2e:machines` group starts on the self-hosted Macs and joins the hosted list only through gate-offload's admission rule. Flags: O8 joins the hub and the project log by `smnsId`. Until O10 supplies the enrollment, O9's terminal sessions agree with MAWS's project key by the rule, not by id. O3 and O7 own notifications, the badge and the attention shortcut across machines. The taste decisions T1 to T5 (the machine chip, an offline machine's rows, the machine's row, an org row spanning machines, an offline machine's tab) go to the owner and are built provisionally until answered; T6, the toggle for others' sessions, is O8's.

30. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O8, O2, O10 and O14, from the remote layer's Decision Logs 24 and 25 (PR #199, master d0728d67c) and O10's answers to O2's seam questions.** Revocations live in the signed state: RL5 gains an append-only `revoked: {keyId, holder}[]` for every key class, every admin retirement and revocation is a state change, delegations are immutable in `id`, `pub` and `prefix`, and dropping a delegation revokes it. So a check against the predecessor sees a revoked key made current again, and O8's acceptance no longer rests on holding the whole chain. O8 gains five duties: after a root transition it offers the superseded states' revocations; it warns before dropping a delegation, since that revokes the delegation's certificates and their history; it re-signs a re-enrolled machine's grants, showing each grant before it signs, since the hub that serves them isn't trusted; it refuses a delegation prefix that equals or nests in another or covers an existing name; and it renews a delegation by extending `expires` while it is live, a lapsed one being replaced by a new delegation with a new prefix. A grant is `{machine, machineKey, member, rights, version}`. A daemon that loses its state keeps its ref, because the ref lives in `machine.json` outside `mawsd/` (Decision Log 27), and re-enrolls with a new key under which every earlier grant is refused. For O2: the grant ledger sits beside the machine key in `mawsd/endpoint/`, a daemon holding only one of the two starts unenrolled, and `<profile>/mawsd/` is excluded from backups, while `machine.json` stays in them. For O14 and O2's M8: a devcloud VM's ref is `<delegation id>_<random>`, 32 lowercase hex digits from the seed, then at least 128 random bits, inside RL5's 64 characters, so the identity module takes an optional prefix at first run. From O10's answers (desk-sminos-orchestrator), folded into O2's document by its author: the endpoint starts once `mawsd`'s sockets are up, not after a host's first connect, since parked hosts would leave the machine unreachable. The seam gains `machine.kind`, a `hold` that alone keeps a private daemon from its idle exit, a binary frame path with its own `documents.open` channel range, and an attachment signal. A remote viewer never takes pty credit. `mawsd enroll` reads its code from stdin or a hidden prompt, never argv, and `mawsd endpoint <cmd>` passes through to the endpoint so O10 adds commands without touching O2. `daemon.restart` is machine-admin, `daemon.endpoint` and `host.interest` stay internal, and `session.setKeep` stays manage.
31. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O8 and O14, from the remote layer's Decision Log 26 (PR #200, master ca99cca27).** A delegation's prefix and id are each used once in the life of the org; a revoked one stays reserved through its `revoked` entry, reversing the remote layer's Decision Log 25 on reuse. Dropping a delegation moves its certificates' retired keys into `revoked`. An enrolled machine's ref never takes a delegation's `<id>_` shape, which a 22-character ref minted without a prefix (Decision Log 27) can't. O8 refuses to sign a delegation whose prefix or id was used before; devcloud (O14) takes a new prefix after a revocation.

O2's executor changes O1's `grades.test.ts` internal set when it builds them. What `daemon.status` returns to a remote caller carries no paths, log location, tailnet address or key material: those fields are marked local-only, and a test fails an unmarked path field. O10 holds its own `status()` to the same rule (desk-sminos-orchestrator).
