36. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10, O8 and O3, from O1 M4's 29 grading calls (O1's Decision Log, branch p5-o1 at 36215b896).** All 29 are accepted, including `session.bash` as `shell`, content-free machine reads as `list`, and `engine.sessions`, `session.search` and `attention.list` as `view`. Four rulings settle what O1 left to O10. First, `session.acquire`'s resume under the session's stored spec stays `converse`. Strict idle release ends most sessions' processes, so a resume that took `spawn` would make a `converse` grant lapse whenever its session goes idle. A resume that changes the spec takes the grade of the change it makes, and the resumed process still runs as the session's spawner. Second, `session.memoryAppend`'s `user` target, the machine-wide `~/.claude` memory, is `machine-admin`. Third, `session.update`'s reader fields stay at `view`, but today they are columns of the shared session row (`src/main/index/migrations.ts:38`, `register.ts:27-35`), and drafts live in the same index. A second viewer marking a session read would clear its owner's badge. O8 keeps reader state and drafts per member, keyed by session and member in the machine's index, so a person's devices share one entry and no two people share one. O8's acceptance gains it. Rejected: keeping them in the client's profile, which splits one person's devices. Fourth, the five bridged commands only main sends (`fs.scan`, `fs.bind`, `fs.unbind`, `maws.artifact.captured`, `maws.download.recorded`) become main-only at O3, and with that `internal`. Until then they stay `machine-admin`. Flags O3, O8 and O10.

42. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O1, O2 and every child that touches the host socket, from O1's branch review (branch p5-o1).** For all of protocol major 3, the lines a pre-O1 peer parses strictly on a protocol-2 link are frozen: the v2 `hello` and `welcome`, `refuse`, `exit`, `req`, `ready`, `res`, `evt`, `documents.open`, `documents.close` and `documents.closed`. `req` keeps a frozen `HostRequestV2Schema` beside the `client`-carrying form O1 M5 added. Any change to a frozen line other than an annotation is breaking. So until major 4 drops the protocol-2 window, a field added to one of them takes one of two routes: either its v2 form splits off and the writer keeps the new field off v2 links, or the change takes a new line kind, which is itself a major bump. Rejected: classifying the retained forms like any other line, which would pass an optional field as minor while a pre-O1 peer refuses it. O2's document channels need neither route. The app-below-2³¹ and endpoint-at-or-above split is a value convention inside `Channel`'s existing `0..2³²−1` range, and the refusal uses `documents.closed`'s existing `host` reason. So `mawsd` enforces it in code, and the split must never become a narrowed schema bound, which would be a breaking change to a frozen line. Flags O2, O3 and O10.

Its ten items for this log are accepted. The edge on O2 narrows: M1 to M9 build against the endpoint's own `EndpointServices`, which matches O2's `DaemonServices` field for field, and only M10 and M11 wait for O2. The module is `src/endpoint/`, with its own entry, tests and lint boundary, sharing `src/shared` with `mawsd`. RL10 reuses DX2's frame vocabulary on a 0600 socket, `rl10.json` gaining `token` and `protocol`. The session lane stops at the endpoint and only `notify` reaches the hub, and the DX1 org key moves into the endpoint once, for dual-coding until O14's final brief. Grants come only from devices, and the hub stores none. The endpoint keeps a 30-day index of inbound v2 events, so a hostile hub can't repost a person's signed words at a new position. O12's phase 2 gains duties: it builds sminos's RL10 client against M6's `conversation.json`; it fences every remote lane A RPC against its launch at the effect, or refuses it; its ledger entries record member, device, fingerprint and target; and it keeps spawn proofs in `ownersProof`. O14's provisioning code carries `enrollDelegated`'s fields, the redemption token included. Three rulings. First, lane B's RL4 ledger is O10's (M7), in the engine host on `src/shared/host/op-ledger.ts`, with `host.op`, `host.opQuery` and M8's `session.pendingItems`. The effect lands in the engine host, and O10 sends the first remote actions. O16 keeps the ledger where the effect lands when it re-homes lane B processes. O7 never owns it, because O7 adds surfaces, not the action path. These engine-host changes take Decision Log 44's re-bump rule against O2, whose own engine-host changes run at the same time, and their PRs run class 1a plus the supervisor e2e groups on a Mac. Second, only commands in the endpoint's offer table cross between machines, and everything else answers `not-offered`. O7 adds lane B spawn, remote terminals and documents, `git.*` and `fs.holders`. O8 adds owner, access and share changes, which sminos executes under its ledger. O9 adds what its lane A controls need. Acceptance 4's full depth is reached as those rows land. Rejected: forwarding every graded command, which round 2 showed exposes commands no machine meant to serve. Third, the app's RL2 `MachineLink` is O7's, built on O6's connection-manager carrier as O7's first milestone. That milestone is blocked by O6, O10 and O11 alone, not by O3, and O9's remote lane A waits on it. Rejected: O9 building its own link, which two children would then keep in step. Two tracker rows: a host-side content fence so a queue promote or unpause can take its entries' grade (until then they take `manage`), and the probe of `session.ui.promptEdit`'s keyed variant (refused until then). Flags O2, O7, O8, O9, O12, O14 and O16.
46. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2 and O16, from O2's Stop-hook probe (branch p5-o2-probe, 5db935c01; the pinned engine 2.1.283 against the stub, on a throwaway devcloud VM since deleted).** The probe's answers: Stop arrives before `result` and carries the exact cron list; an interrupted turn sends no Stop; `-p` has no keepalive, so Decision Log 29's conditional fourth unreadable state is dropped; and a subagent under MAWS's launch is offered no cron tool. One finding extends §2's sources. A durable cron (`CronCreate durable: true`) never appears in any Stop's `session_crons`. It persists in `<cwd>/.claude/scheduled_tasks.json` and fires by itself, including after `--resume` and before any Stop, so reading crons from Stop alone would release a session holding a durable job. The engine host therefore reads that file at each Stop and at a resume's launch, and merges into the work's crons the entries whose `createdBySessionId` matches any engine session id the session has had. Matching the lineage, not only the current id, holds however `--resume` treats ids. A file that exists but can't be read counts as unknown work, and that replaces §2's resume-after-crash state, since the file is what survives a crash. Rejected: marking a session sticky-unknown after a durable `CronCreate`, which can't tell when the job is gone and misses a resume that finds one already in the file. O16 carries the same reading when it re-homes lane B processes. Flags O16.
47. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2 and O16, correcting Decision Log 46 from O2's executor (the probe's question 4, `spikes/protocol-probe/findings-p5-o2-stop-crons.txt` on p5-o2-probe).** Decision Log 46's clause that the unreadable scheduled-tasks file replaces §2's resume-after-crash state is wrong: the file holds durable jobs only. `--resume` also resurrects the session-only crons the transcript holds (`resume: resurrected 1 session cron task(s)`), and the probe saw one fire, in a turn the engine started, before the launch's first Stop. So a resume that doesn't follow a release counts as unknown work until its first Stop, which lists the resurrected job. A resume after a release stays known-empty: strict release never releases a session whose crons are listed or unknown, and the file read covers its durable jobs. §2 now lists four unreadable states, the original three and the unreadable file; Decision Log 29's conditional keepalive state stays dropped. Rejected: unknown for every resume, which would keep a session resumed after a release with no prompt from ever releasing again until a turn ran. O2 tests a durable job firing before a resume's first Stop in case (m)'s family at M7, with unit cases at M2. Flags O16.
48. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2, from O2's baseline runs at M1's commit (5527067ed, the mini: 40483272622, 40485317066, 40487334697).** Acceptance 14's memory bound reads each process's physical footprint, the figure macOS charges a process (`ri_phys_footprint`, what Activity Monitor's Memory column shows), not its RSS. Three valid runs of the same idle supervisor read 77,520 to 153,952 KiB of RSS, a 2x spread. RSS drops when macOS compresses an idle process's dirty pages under other jobs' pressure and counts shared clean pages, so it moved with the mini's load, not with the supervisor, and a 1.5x bound would pass or miss on that noise alone. The footprint keeps compressed pages and leaves out shared clean ones, which is the cost the owner gets back when a session is released. O2 re-records the baseline at M1's commit with three more runs, keeps RSS in the output line as a diagnostic, and reports the footprint's spread before deriving the bound if it is still wide. The multipliers of O2's residue 29 stand (1.5 times the footprint, the CPU plus 1.0 s per 600 s). Rejected: keeping RSS and judging a miss by reruns, which spends three runs per miss on noise and leaves the bound unable to catch a real regression of under 2x.
49. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2 and O16, from O2's executor at M2 (p5-o2 9c8750c83).** Decision Log 47's resumed state, as written, never ends for a resume that gets no turn. A session resumed after an engine-host crash or a major upgrade, and then prompted by nobody, would be held by both the idle release and the memory budget until a cron fired or someone sent a turn. One crash could keep every session it touched for good, which is the cost the laptop's rule exists to give back. So the engine host persists each session's cron knowledge in its state file, beside the `engineSessionIds` lineage M2 adds: the last Stop's session crons and whether a cron tool ran after that Stop. A resume that doesn't follow a release starts known-empty when that record shows no session cron listed and no cron tool since, and unknown until its first Stop otherwise. A listed recurring cron fires, and its Stop ends the unknown. What stays unbounded is a crash in a turn that ran a cron tool and a listed one-shot that never fires, both narrow, and the tracker carries them. Rejected: releasing a resumed unknown after a fixed time, which needs a probe to ground the longest cron interval and would still release a session whose cron is real; and keeping Decision Log 47 as written, which turns a crash into a permanent hold. Flags O16, which re-homes processes with the same record.
50. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10 and O8, at O10 M1's landing (PR #217, master b49e29192).** O10's protocol package is on master: the RL2 channel, RL4's proofs and their vectors, reviewed clean at reviewer-high after five fixes, class 1a green on the head's tree. Three of its calls bind other children. An owner add's RL4 proof is action `record.owners` with args `{add: true, member}`, and O8 signs it that way. `verifyAttestation` takes the bare `OrgState` and is all-or-nothing: one failing attestation fails the state. The endpoint keeps the org-state chain it adopted (`chain.jsonl`, 30 days), so `h2` can page a device's catch-up from any state in that window, which closes a gap in O10's §E. O10 M2 creates `src/shared/host/private-path.ts` to O2's Interfaces, and whichever of O10 M2 and O2 M3 lands second takes the first's file. Flags O8 and O2.

The label's owner is shown by the member's email: O10's M6 builds the attestation with each owner's `email` from the signed org state (a member carries `id`, `email`, `role` and `devices`, no display name), so O12's label reads `owned by <email>` rather than a member id; a project-wide view of what a person's sessions said is O7's. 53. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10, O7 and O8, at O10 M9's landing (PR #221, master e7bed1d07).** The device's keys and step-up are in main: a channel key and a step-up key, both P-256, kept as private JWKs in `<profile>/device/keys.json` (0600, in a 0700 directory) as `{version 1, keyStorage 'software', channel, stepUp}`, imported non-extractable and never sent to the renderer. 54. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2, from O2's footprint baseline (d14e92991, the same `src/` as M1's 5527067ed; the mini: 40490756731, 40492851056, 40495339208).** The footprint reading holds: three runs spread 1.03x (63,969 to 66,033 KiB) where RSS had spread 2x, and `proc_pid_rusage` agreed with top in each. The baseline is 64,481 KiB and 0.11 s per 600 s. But a combined bound of 1.5 times that leaves `mawsd` about 31 MiB, and `mawsd` runs on the Electron binary as Node (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`), as the engine host does, whose bare runtime alone is likely most of that. So the combined bound would judge the daemon shape's second process, which P5 chose, not a regression in either process, and P5 §10's remedy would fire by construction. The memory bound is therefore per process: the engine host within 1.5 times the supervisor's baseline footprint, and `mawsd` within 1.5 times the footprint of the bare runtime it runs on (the same Electron binary as Node with an empty script that stays alive, three runs on the mini under the load rule, the median). The CPU bound stays combined: the baseline plus 1.0 s per 600 s. Rejected: the combined 1.5 times, for the reason above; and a fixed allowance in MiB for `mawsd`, which would be a guess rather than a measurement.

57. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O2, correcting Decision Log 54 from O2's runtime floor (side branch 2d434d1af at 36704c984; the mini: 40498387866, 40500435129, 40504203813, spread 1.04x).** Decision Log 54's premise was wrong. The bound returns to O2's residue 29 as first written: `mawsd` and the engine host together within 1.5 times today's supervisor's footprint (96,721 KiB), and their CPU within the baseline plus 1.0 s per 600 s. That is also the statement acceptance 14 makes to the owner: the daemon shape costs the laptop at most half again what today's supervisor does. It leaves `mawsd` about 32 MiB, 19 MiB above the runtime. The floor stays recorded as a diagnostic. When O2's M5 builds `mawsd.js`, `mawsd` idle with no hosts attached is measured three times on the mini, so a budget miss shows at M5 rather than at M9; a miss there comes to the architect as a P5 §10 finding. 67. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O10 and O12, at O10 M6's landing (PR #236, master a7ceac66c).** The endpoint serves RL10, the sminos socket, after three reviewer-high rounds. Round one found 10 Important problems. Words were decided without current membership, without the state-age backstop, or while an authority change raced them. A position was signed twice across a connection replacement. A torn JSONL tail swallowed the next append, in M2's shared helper. The legacy key was not scoped to its org. Its calls, joint with O12 (O12 builds the sminos side as M7b, whose text in PR #235 agrees with M6's frames, fixture path and members-before-uploads order): (1) RL10 gains three frames, since nothing has cut over and no compatibility path is needed. Endpoint to sminos: `spent {project, writer, seq, reason}`, sent only for a verdict no later head can change, so one forged or replayed hub event no longer stalls a member's writer for good; sminos logs each spent position and counts it in `daemon status`. `words?` requires `project`. sminos to endpoint: `restated {}`, sent once after the post-welcome restatement, after which the endpoint withdraws the sessions it did not hear about; it replaces a 15 s timer. (2) The hold rule. `unknown-signer` is held and re-judged at each new head, new certificates or legacy key, capped at 1000 events and one hour each; an event let go comes back from the hub at sminos's next `hello`. `revoked`, `bad-signature` and `malformed` are final. `expired` is final under a key the state retired, and held when the signer is a machine certificate, since a renewal not yet received may cover it. A key that is both retired and certified reads as held. By the remote layer's Decision Log 24, a removed member's keys enter retired or revoked in the same step, so a removed member never reads `unknown-signer`. (3) The endpoint's verdicts map onto the hub's four rejected reasons: replayed to `seen-nonce`, forged to `bad-code`, an own non-echo to `conflict`, a bad shape to `bad-shape`. `revoked` names a device or member id only once the head no longer holds it, so revoking one of a member's devices does not revoke the other. (4) A `words?` whose authority moves while it is being decided gets no answer; sminos re-asks after `WORDS_WAIT`, so the delivery stays owed and a person's words are never downgraded to peer on a race.
68. **2026-10-27, the architect, under O12 and O10, at O12 M7b's landing (doperpowers PR #410, sminos 1.26.0; record PR #237, master 6d8b50f67).** sminos builds its side of Decision Log 67's frames, after two reviewer-high rounds (three P2s, then none). Each `spent` position is logged to daemon.log and counted in `daemon status`. A `spent` for one of this machine's own writers, under its current or a former name, at or below that writer's last seq, is a logged no-op, so a lost endpoint ledger cannot spend real echoes. The conformance replay of O10's `conversation.json` found 15 lines that sminos does not send as written: members without principal, inference or controller; `raised` in DX4's draft shape with no kind or at; a `words?` addressed to `*`; a decide digest of empty input; a missing `proof:spawn` binding. PR #238 (master 6d57a6160) brought the fixture to the frames sminos 1.26 sends, and the replay passes 99 of 99 with no amendments. No endpoint code changed, since the endpoint never read those shapes. A sminos patch now makes an amendment fail the replay. Its calls: RL10's settings ceiling, when the file names none, applies in both directions; this is RL10 only, and DX2 keeps `machine_policy()`. Board ticket 148's cause is known: `convert_v2_nodes` walks v2 groups with an unsorted glob, and it had failed CI four times.
69. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O13, at its execution document's landing (PR #241, merge 1ce67af0e; the remote layer's Decision Log 28, PR #242, merge 587f6fcea).** O13's residue, as its document builds it. (1) Production's org is rooted before O8 by an owner-run Mac command, `deployment admin` (`init`, `genesis`, `adopt`, `pair`, `devices`, `remove-device`, `refresh`), whose own software-keyed admin device is the org's first device and root. The remote layer admits a software first root until signed builds (its Decision Log 28), bounded: a durable 0600 keys file that `init` refuses under `/tmp`, `$TMPDIR`, a git work tree, `~/.claude`, `~/Library/Mobile Documents` or `~/.config/claude-secrets`, excluded from Time Machine; every confirmation against a deployment read from `/dev/tty`, so an agent's shell signs nothing; the genesis written to disk before it is posted, so an interrupted run finishes rather than makes a second one, and `adopt` for an org rooted earlier; the recovery key written at `genesis` only to an offline destination the owner names and read back against `recoveryPub` before anything is signed, the medium a USB drive or another removable volume mounted for the step, with nothing left on the Mac (the owner's choice, 2026-10-28); `refresh` signs the weekly no-change successor until O8's signer takes over, every subcommand prints the head's age and the phone warns from day 21, against the endpoint's 30-day backstop. Its end is O8's and independent of key class, since the app's device is software too until signed builds: once the app's device is a root, the app signs the successor that revokes the deployment admin device (it signs no log events, so revoking discredits nothing) and its key file is deleted; O8's acceptance and O15's recomposition carry it. The app's device key file gets the same Time Machine exclusion in an O10 follow-up, which O8 depends on; an app device whose key file may have been copied before the exclusion re-registers with new keys before it becomes a root. Rejected: reading main's device keys from a script, rooting from desk-web's browser, and waiting for O8, which would push the mini's cutover past M1. (2) The pairing code (`smns-pair-v1.`), its MAC (`smns-pair-mac-v1`, over the org, the member and the device entry) and `pairingFingerprint` (10 base32 characters of SHA-256 over the MAC's input without `k`, every credential of the entry included) join the protocol package. The code travels by the Mac's clipboard (the owner's choice), which every process under the uid can read, so `k` admits nothing alone: both sides show the fingerprint for the owner to compare, and two entries verifying under one `k` are refused and the code spent. (3) The lane A `cards` snapshot carries `launch`, null when there is no bridge (O10 M8, 76f45de42); lane A's inline failed and refused outcomes carry only a code, which the card draws. (4) The phone never queries, since a `web-cards` query of a session target is refused (Decision Log 64): a lost acknowledgement is resealed under the same opId with a fresh gesture, or settled by the next snapshot. The lane B request digest is mirrored in the browser, pinned byte-equal to `requestDigestOf`. Once registered, desk-web's registry socket opens `?app=1` and feeds a pinned org store and the listings. The phone's list of waiting sessions comes from the org state's machines and each Machine socket's `presence` frame, since a cut-over machine sends no DX2 summaries. (5) The cards passkey is a credential of its own (ES256, non-discoverable, no PRF), not the org key's PRF passkey, made under the hub's hostname, which is the endpoint's WebAuthn origin. (6) A to-human message reaches the phone as a notice that sends the owner to MAWS, until MAWS has a web or phone version (the owner's choice); the v2 push notice draws without a title and lands on the cards route. (7) desk-web takes `org-protocol` as a direct workspace dependency. (8) The freeze's one seam is a capability reason, moved to MAWS, for a session whose machine the org state lists, and frozen views show one reason string. (9) Production's deploy steps carry staging's mechanism with the roles swapped and run only at the owner's hand, behind a commit confirmation equal to origin/master's head, from a durable checkout path; `access-sync` waits for O14's first production machine. (10) The phone stays on the hub relay. (11) The live acceptance runs the real endpoint module in-process on staging over the fake engine host and fake sminos with the owner's real phone; the run with `mawsd` on the VM is O10 M11's and O14's. 73. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O10, at M7's landing (PR #246, merge 96eeed03c).** RL4 actions now reach the side that executes them, each with its op ledger. reviewer-high ran six rounds (3, 3, 3, 2, 1 findings, then none), each finding reproduced before its fix: results routed by opId alone, a ledger left writable after a failed fsync, `web-cards` authorized under a name derived from the binding, lane A ownership lost at an endpoint restart, and a closed-ledger warning cleared by a late `done`. (2) Op ledgers fail closed until their process restarts, shown in `endpoint.detail`'s `opLedgers` and the log, and the engine host reports `ledger: <word>` on every answer. 74. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O13 and O14, at O13 M2's landing (PR #247, merge a147a2481).** The pairing code, its MAC and the entry's fingerprint join `org-protocol`, and the owner-run `deployment admin` lands with its no-fork rule. As built: the code's grammar takes its canonical spelling only, the hub as an exact origin and `k` of 16 bytes; the MAC refuses an endorsed or out-of-grammar entry, and the fingerprint ignores the endorsement; device ids and rpIds are held to a grammar and every output escaped, since control characters from the hub could otherwise draw a false fingerprint and prompt. The keys file is `{device, keys}`, a file without `device` naming `a1` so O11's staging file still reads; the Time Machine exclusion runs on the empty file before any key is written; `--hub` is loopback only. Against a deployment `/dev/tty` opens before any key, token or request is touched, and the pairing code and a paper recovery key go to `/dev/tty` alone. `pair` signs only the entry it showed, byte for byte. The recovery destination must be removable or external media as diskutil reports it, a disk image refused, and a recovery file is never written over; the check has not yet met a real USB drive, so O13 M7's owner sitting runs a loopback genesis to the owner's own removable volume before production's. `adopt` refuses when an anchor exists, `remove-device` refuses the admin device itself, revoking a root device removes its key from the root keys, and resuming an interrupted genesis verifies the head before anchoring it. The no-fork rule: the pending proposal is written into the anchor before its POST and reconciled first (adopt it, resend the same bytes, or refuse a fork); there is one amend path, which O14's verbs call; a per-anchor lock covers every subcommand but `init`, and a held or stale lock is refused with its holder named, never taken over; a not-head answer is never re-signed; no verb drops a pending proposal, and whether O14 adds an abandon verb is O14's to weigh. The vector generator rewrites its files whole with fresh keys (a tracker row).
75. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O14, at its execution document's landing (PR #249, merge 60074ada2; the remote layer's Decision Log 29, PR #251, merge 4e1f33f49).** O14's residue, as its document builds it. (1) O14 administers production's org state through verbs on O13 M2's `deployment admin` (`show`, `enroll`, `delegation create` and `drop`, `machine drop`, `code strip-legacy` and `inspect`, `keys retire` and `purge`, `provisioning revoke`), run through O13 M1's runner, under the same admin device, anchor, lock, no-fork rule and `/dev/tty` confirmations; it roots nothing, pairs nothing, writes no recovery key, and leaves the admin device's revocation to O8 (Decision Log 69). Enrollment codes travel as the phone's pairing code does, the owner comparing name, ref and key id with `mawsd keys --json` before listing; a provisioning code goes from `--code-to-stdout` through the runner's secret stream into Secret Manager and is refused on a terminal, so no code is ever a file on a Mac. (2) The phone's loop at a re-enrollment in place. Re-enrollment ends the machine's DX2 credential at its redemption, as the hub does today, so the phone's loop for that machine pauses until it is listed and sminos is welcomed, inside the owner's window (30 minutes with no session waiting and nothing owed, a card test within 10 minutes, undo within 60); O14's acceptance 4 now says so, and the remote layer's §9 carries the exception. Single trust is enforced at sminos's welcome (O12 M5); the hub carries availability only. The hub refuses DX2 (`403 v2-credential`) to a credential whose enrollment has a posted machine key, so the credential a re-enrollment minted cannot open DX2 in the window; the instant before the key post is accepted under the window's gate. Rejected: O14 M6, which kept the DX2 credential until the endpoint's first RL1 claim. Its focused adversarial round found four P1s and two P2s against the code: the cutover deleted the credential and set the irreversible mark before the claim succeeded; a DX2 principal resolved before re-enrollment could reach the name-only cutover after listing; a confirmed enrollment never expired, so a prior token could live forever; the claim is no ordering fence on local DX6; deletion by client id left the token; and a second re-enrollment lost the prior credential. Making it safe meant a migration state machine for a security-sensitive credential, to save a few minutes once per machine inside a window the owner had approved. (3) The cutover undo is one idempotent sequence, the same before or after sminos's welcome: `mawsd endpoint unenroll` (the private key destroyed, `machine.json` kept), `identity.json` removed if present, the hub-side drop, then DX2 back with a v1 code; a certificate machine uses `retire`; a VM born on v2 is recreated, not undone. This amends Decision Log 51. (4) Provisioning: the v2 codec lives in `apps/org-protocol/src/provisioning.ts` (correcting Decision Log 35's placement); during the migration the code carries the legacy org key, so a VM born after M3 never enrolls on DX2; `provision` is a resumable four-state transaction written before its first authenticated request; the endpoint keeps the delegation key and retires its own certificate at departure, a forced or unreachable delete printing the admin command; `unenroll` and `retire` destroy private keys, current and archived (RL §5.2). An existing DX2 VM is converted by dropping its hub row first. (5) Re-attestation is a signed per-project, per-writer digest checkpoint over the hub's inventory, carrying the machine's own former names, binding only where no other ref holds a name; O7's log views and O8's project channel draw "attested at migration by <name>", and B4 waits for one reader to draw a migrated event unless O8's reader lags the soak (Q10). (6) The org key's retirement is a field of the signed state, `legacyRetired`, which every endpoint follows into a tombstone until a per-machine purge; O10's per-machine `legacy retire` verb is removed; the hub purges the copies only on the head's word after a 7-day soak and refuses a successor that un-retires after the purge. The field is published only once every deployed verifier carries M5a's `org-protocol`: the hub, each endpoint, O13's running bundle, and O8's app if landed. M5 is two builds: M5a deployable at any time, M5b (the phase-1 transports' removal) merged only in B4. Browsers that unlocked the phase-1 key forget it in B4; Secret Manager's earlier versions are destroyed at the irreversible boundary. (7) The MAWS app's dual-coding of the owner's words is not built: every machine moves before O8 ships them, so O8 sends v2-only and its words wait for O14's last machine. (8) What other children provide. O2: `mawsd install` says when it ignores `--delegation-id`, its documents say the id comes from the driver profile through `/etc/devcloud/env` rather than the seed, and its packaged job on the mini checks that it leaves the canonical plist unchanged rather than absent, since B2 installs it. O10: M10 (the endpoint inside `mawsd`), M11's item 7 against the cut-over VM, and a status field saying whether RL10 is served. O12, before B2: an RL10 mode for `bench_scale.py`, a bounded `sminos daemon owed` that the nothing-owed gate reads, and a test for the un-cutover branch; the sminos floor is 1.26.0. O13: production's deploy steps before B0, and a desk-web deploy at or after M5a with a way to read the phone's running build before B4. (9) The owner's answers (2026-10-28, each the document's recommendation): the acceptance VM converted, or one kept VM, plus a throwaway at B1 and at B4; the mini's window as above; mini-ops builds the packaged app on the mini and the owner pastes the enrollment code over screen sharing; the endpoint's listener port opened from the Macs to `tag:devcloud` and the mini before O10 M11's item 7; a VM created without `--project maws` stays local-only after B4 (devcloud installing `mawsd` at boot is doperpowers board #150, deferred); the MacBook moves a week after the mini is stable, not bound to O3 or O7; a 7-day soak before the keys' purge; a `human` event owed from before a cutover and asked afterwards is an accepted one-time cost. (10) Two conflicts stay recorded rather than designed away: the attestation label's readers may lag the soak (Q10 above), and staging's `OrgAdmin` trusts the hub's head, which production's anchored admin does not. Verification: four adversarial buildability rounds on the draft (19, 11, 10, 2 findings), one after the reconciliation with O13 (5), and the round on M6 that withdrew it; reviewer-high per milestone PR.

91. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O10, at the landing of the carriers' socket-buffer bound (PR #275, merge 2ffc23dde): Decision Log 77's carrier residue is closed.** reviewer-high ran six rounds (4, 2, 1, 2 and 1 findings, then correct on 5cad3dc05..0413e3894), each finding reproduced with a real-socket or real-relay probe before its fix. Gate: class 1a 40664747575 on the head's tree, based on master's head 872a6c309, so it is the merge gate. Class 1a alone covers it: nothing under `src/engine-host`, `apps/org-protocol`, `apps/desk-hub/src` or desk-web's sources changed; no e2e suite imports `src/endpoint`; desk-web's local cards suite, which does, runs in the unit job through `apps/desk-web/vitest.local.config.ts`; and the wire is unchanged. As built: (1) every frame either carrier sends, pongs included, goes through a send buffer (`src/endpoint/send-buffer.ts`) that feeds the socket one frame at a time; the connection ends at a total ceiling (40 MiB on the hub link, 16 MiB on the direct listener) or at a 30 s stall read from the socket's own writes, and a graceful close drains the buffer first. (2) The direct listener has a 1 MiB soft cap and terminates a connection at its limits; the client resumes from its cursor. (3) The hub link applies back-pressure and does not reconnect: a relayed stream pauses once 1 MiB is buffered until the socket drains, and the 32 MiB per-channel data budget closes only the channel that crosses it. (4) The log-plane uplink runs off RL10's loop in a queue capped at 8 MiB. An overflow drops the upload and marks a hole for that writer; each hole is filled by asking sminos to resend, one ask at a time from a fixed 1 MiB reserve, the ask released after 30 s; a writer refused as final is never asked again, and an upload's writer is held to the hub's name rule. Residue, bounded, no row: a sminos that accepts a resend and never answers delays other writers' asks by at most 30 s in every 60 s, with memory bounded. O10 now has only M10 and M11, both waiting for O2; its xhigh panel runs from p5-desk-track-owner's session once M11 has landed.

The daemon sweeps a bridge's kept file after a day idle, and a child's failed save leaves the mark behind the mod's, so the bridge mark is no floor; a reused seq against a live mark answers a false `duplicate`, against a swept one it delivers once, so the worst case at 1.27.4 was a false failure, never a double delivery. sminos 1.27.6 (doperpowers #427, 1f4bff14, board #159) makes `sends.json` version 2, `{version, floor, seq}`: `floor` is the highest seq of any dropped entry, a session with no entry starts at the larger of the floor and the bridge mark plus one, pruning runs at most hourly and only for sessions not running, with no bridge socket and nothing in flight, a version 1 file reads as floor 0, and a 1.27.4 daemon refuses a version 2 file rather than reuse a key; reviewer-medium ran two rounds and both reproductions now allocate above the old seq. The tracker row closes. sminos 1.27.7 (#428, board #157) fixes `test-sync.sh`'s setup race, 20 of 20 under load. sminos 1.27.8 (#429, f8718cb0, boards #156 and #158) merges the release queues: file order holds within a queue, a former name goes first on a timestamp tie, and what waits after a hub leave stays queued and is named by `hub leave`; `daemon owed` counts it as having no upstream and exits 1 on it, a behaviour change no O14 gate meets, since none runs `owed` on a machine that has left its hub and devcloud's departure reads only `hub leave`'s exit. O3-F: native rewind across `--resume` is not broken on the pinned engine, so RL §12.3's lane B half is answered and O3-D needs no rewind refusal at the launch boundary; O3-D takes a crash-window repair (a rewind's anchor lost to a SIGKILL within about 100 ms of its answer, re-sent at resume with its original target and last-seen cursor). Two residuals outside the launch boundary: the resume chooser's preview shows a rewound turn as the latest after a "Load now" rewind, because that rewind writes its anchor without `lastPrompt` and `LastPromptAnchorSchema` requires it, which lane A shows today and a one-line fix closes; and `currentChain`, used only by the replay sources, ignores rewind anchors between a rewind and the next prompt, a tracker row. O3-G: Node's recursive watch on Linux watches every file, rescans per event and fails silently at exhaustion, so O3-E builds a directory-level watch tree MAWS owns (O3's Decision Log 9). The engine host's store watcher makes the same recursive call over `~/.claude/projects` and already runs wherever O2 puts the engine host on Linux, so O14's VMs carry it from B1: harmless on a fresh VM's few transcripts, it degrades silently as they grow, and it shares O3-E's module (a tracker row until then). O3's Ordering drops O3-G from O3-E's blockers. The run drove the VM partly through the ptc kernel's shell, which the worktree guard does not cover, before switching to the documented `devcloud vm ssh <name> < file` route; nothing ran locally outside its worktree, and later VM briefs name the documented route.
104. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O3, at the landing of O3-F's preview fix (PR #292, merge 8e1ada2a4), and master's unit suite red from #289 until it.** The fix: `LastPromptAnchorSchema`'s `lastPrompt` is optional, since a "Load now" rewind on a resumed session writes its anchor without one. The spike's recorded MAWS-side replay (`p5-o3-rewind-resume-maws.json`) and the probe test that reads it keep the pre-fix preview as the spike's record; a regeneration changes four snapshots and must update that test's assertion with them. A further residual of the same cause: `summarize.ts` takes `lastAssistantText` and `leafUuid` from the last record in file order, so until the next prompt the sidebar digest of a session MAWS does not hold, and `markSeen`'s cursor, name the rewound turn's reply (measured on the fixture, not seen in the app); it joins the `currentChain` tracker row. Master's unit suite was red from #289's merge e949d75d6 until this merge: `replay-fixtures.test.ts` expects every probe journal under the fixture tree by directory, #289 added `fixtures/p5/`, and #289 was landed on a probes-only gate, the architect's error. 105. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O8, O7 and O13, at M1's landing (PR #287, merge 790002644): the app's org session.** Settings' Org section signs a Mac in from a `pair --class native-mac` code: Access's login transfer in the system browser, `/api/me` naming the code's org and member, device keys made at that moment, the chain read from genesis and verified against the code's root pins, the entry posted with the code's MAC and its fingerprint shown. Main holds the chain whole under `<userData>/org/` behind an anchor a damaged file, a rollback, a fork or a stale hub cannot move, and follows the hub through the Registry feed. The production `OrgSession` replaces Decision Log 99's `signedOut()` arm, so O7's links come up in production; an ended Access token sets `hub` to null and keeps the direct carrier, and a hub that stops admitting the member reads `denied` and changes no trust. A profile that never signs in makes no keys, files or requests. New dependency `tweetnacl` 1.0.3; no `WIRE_MINOR`, settings version, `IpcError` code or e2e group. Residue, each with its rejected alternative in O8's Decision Log and report: (1) one org session per run, none in production under `MAWS_DEV_ORG`; (2) up to 120 s of backoff when Access refuses a fresh token, never on the Worker's `not-a-member`; (3) `org.*` is graded `client` on the link surface, and `routes.json` is regenerated after a merge, never hand-merged; (4) `ORG_REFUSALS` gains `directory` and the feed gains `onClose`; (5) a registration with no readable `access.json` reads signed out; (6) the chain is re-read from a recovery's `fromVersion`, or from genesis when the served head is below the held one; (7) the held view as built (files, `certificatesChanged`, `highWater()`, rollback and fork judged only when nothing was adopted); (8) the transfer's box key comes from WebCrypto, and cloudflared's metadata `iat` check is not done until the staging sitting shows whether Access sets `iat`; (9) one serialized commit queue for every durable write and trust change; (10) hub answers sanitized at the boundary, malformed certificate items dropped. Evidence: reviewer-high two rounds (3 P1 and 5 P2 fixed, each with a failing-first test; the second round clean); the sign-in against desk-hub's Worker in Miniflare with O13's real `pair` (7 passed); final gate 40683839806 on the head's tree: class 1a, `unit:darwin`, `e2e:default` and 29 of 32 hosted groups green; `e2e:c7` (`composer.spec.ts:183`) green on rerun 40699088283; `e2e:design-systems` (`design-systems.spec.ts:140`, the preview iframe's context destroyed after the accent edit) reaches nothing #287 changes; `packaged` 199 passed with the one known red `layout-transfer.spec.ts:541`, which O2's branch skips, so every packaged spec that launches main with `tweetnacl` bundled passed. Master had moved in code only under `.github`, the fixtures and `transcript.ts`, none imported by #287. It conflicts with O2 in `src/main/index.ts` and `src/shared/ipc/grades.ts`; it landed first so that O2's final set on its merge covers both, one hosted set instead of two. Owed: the owner's staging sitting (the fingerprint on a real Access login, `/api/me` from the app, a relaunch with no browser trip), folded after O13 M7's in the desk track's sitting.
106. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O14, at the landing of the desk track's PR #291 (merge 258b13632): `provision.test.ts`'s pool flake found and fixed.** The local hub never died on its own: `apps/desk-hub/local/run.ts` threw. Node's HTTP server takes its own `error` listener off a socket before it emits `upgrade`, and the stand-in's proxy (`apps/desk-hub/local/proxy.ts`) added one only once the hub had upgraded. In the retire case that reconciles a lost DELETE answer, the restarted endpoint's hub-link upgrade is refused 401 and `HubLink.stop()` resets the socket while the refusal is unread, so the reset raised an unhandled ECONNRESET and ended the process: the next case failed ECONNRESET and the six after it ECONNREFUSED, both pool sightings' signature (Decision Logs 95 and 102). Reproduced locally with instrumentation, 1 run in 20 under load on the unfixed tree. The proxy's upgrade handler now owns the socket's errors from the start, and a client gone mid-handshake closes the hub's side; `apps/desk-hub/test/proxy.test.ts` covers resets before the answer, after a refusal, and before and after the hub upgraded; `test/endpoint/local-hub.ts` prints the hub's output tail when it exits unasked. No retry, no test case changed. Evidence: 20 local runs of unit 4/4's file set at 2753c929c (provision and proxy 20 of 20, no hub deaths); class 1a 40694837226 there, and on the head's tree after merging master, class 1a 40703392641 and hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` 40704168662 (the group ci-gate's coverage note named, since its specs reach `local-hub.ts`); reviewer-medium no material findings; master had moved since only in #293's scripts. The tracker row closes. A local observation, not from CI: on the loaded MacBook `src/main/files/service.test.ts`'s listing cap case, which writes 5,001 files inside the 5 s default timeout, timed out in 8 of the 20 runs; never seen on the pool, so no row.
107. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O2, O3, O10 and O14, at O2's landing (PR #296, merge 7a5893b2c): `mawsd`, the per-user daemon.** `mawsd` is a per-profile daemon outside Electron that owns the engine host and the pty host: on macOS the default profile's LaunchAgent runs it, which a packaged app installs or repairs; on Linux a systemd user unit with lingering, installed by `devenv up` into a content-named release with its own Node 24.21.0; any other profile starts a private one. Main reaches the two hosts and `daemon.*` through three relayed sockets. Release is strict by interest: a session with no tab and no keep mark is released after the idle time even with the app open, and never while it holds crons (Stop's and the durable `scheduled_tasks.json`), background tasks or work it cannot read. Terminals outlive the app. Settings version 20 makes the daemon shape the default, with embedded as the owner's fallback; the wire is minor 5 (master's 242 manifest entries plus O2's 18). A test or harness launch on the default profile exits non-zero before any host work (O3's planner P9). Acceptance 14's bound: Decision Log 57's fixed 96,721 KiB is superseded. After the landing merges the first daemon run read 101,411 KiB against it, `mawsd` having grown to about 31 MiB with O10's endpoint wiring and the engine host spreading 66 to 70 MiB run to run, so the bound is re-derived on the landing tree as 1.5 times the detached supervisor measured there, re-measured whenever a landing misses: 65,729, 70,881 and 67,409 KiB in CI-opt's quiet window, median 67,409, so 101,113 KiB and 1.11 s. The daemon shape read 97,683 and 99,219 KiB there, a third run invalid on load 7.6 from ci-kit's VM lane (Gate offload Decision Log 137): within, with about 1.9% to spare, and the local-latency bench's p50 at 25.9 of 26.45 ms. Later merges changed no code under `src/mawsd`, `src/engine-host` or `src/pty-host`. The lever is `mawsd`'s eager start-up graph, and O3 budgets against the bound before it adds to either process. Gates on the code head's tree 892d99838: class 1a 40703662055; hosted 40704450189, 36 of 37 with desk-web-hub and cross-build (3.5 against 3.4), `connectors-direct.spec.ts:591` green on rerun 40710872036; the mini's packaged, `e2e:default` and `unit:darwin` 40704479546. Master then moved in #291, #293 and #294, which share `gate.yml` and `gate-plan.test.mjs` with O2 and the endpoint's local hub with its `src/endpoint` changes, so the merge tree was gated as well: class 1a 40714473721 and hosted desk-web-hub, supervisor-daemon and daemon-unit 40714568127. Three merge-resolution reviews and the high-rung branch review were Ready; CI-opt reviewed the `.github` changes (Gate offload Decision Log 141). Residue in the tracker: the idle margin, `daemon.ts`'s size, three host supervision gaps, two Linux pack items, the ad hoc signature's TCC prompts, and unit flakes (`cli-prompt.test.ts`'s ^C case, `ipc.test.ts:1140`). The devcloud VMs `dc-o2probe-ac5c` and `dc-m8accept-dcef` may linger in the desk's Machines list, the desk track's to drop. With O2 in: O3-A and O3-B dispatch; O10 M10 and M11, O14 M1's `src/mawsd` half and B1 are unblocked for the desk track; CI-opt's packaged admission runs its final trials on a tree that carries the daemon shape's packaged check.

115. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O10 and O7, at the landing of the desk track's PR #301 (merge 683ac3e54): `ws` in main's bundle stays on its JavaScript paths.** O10 M10's scene found that `ws`, bundled into main (O7's `MachineLink`) and into `mawsd` (the endpoint's hub link), threw `bufferUtil$1.mask is not a function` on every masked client frame of 48 bytes or more: its optional native helpers `bufferutil` and `utf-8-validate` are installed nowhere, the bundler stands an empty object in for each, and `ws`'s `try { require(...) }` takes the stand-in as present. Every client-to-server frame of that size on main's remote links failed in a built app since O7 M1; the unit suites run `ws` from source, which never meets the stand-in. The fix defines `ws`'s own switches, `WS_NO_BUFFER_UTIL` and `WS_NO_UTF_8_VALIDATE`, in main's build (`electron.vite.config.ts`), and `src/engine-host/bundle-guard.test.ts` runs the emitted `ws` against source `ws` both ways from 31 B to 70 KB (failing on master's build, 11 of 11 with the fix). Evidence: run 40723847211 on the head's tree, class 1a and hosted `e2e:machines`, `e2e:desk-web-hub`, `e2e:supervisor-daemon` and `e2e:daemon-unit` (the groups whose processes load `ws`), no flaky line; reviewer-medium no material findings, UTF-8 validation still refusing malformed input through the JavaScript fallback. Master had moved since its base only in docs. Residue in the tracker: no e2e group drives main's RL2 link in the built app, which is how this escaped.

121. **2026-10-28, the architect, under O11 and O8, at the landing of the desk track's PR #306 (merge 409920137): H1, an admin reads another member's pending devices.** `GET /api/devices/pending?member=<id>` serves O8 M4, where an admin approves a member's first device. With no id, or the caller's own, the Worker calls today's `pendingDevices` unchanged, so the read works against a Registry of any age. Another member's id calls a Registry method of its own, `pendingDevicesOf(you, of)`, and not an argument on `pendingDevices`, which a Registry from before it would drop, answering the caller's own entries for another member's id. A Registry that lacks the method answers 503 `hub-updating`. The method checks the caller is an admin first, so a non-admin's 403 `not-admin` never tells whether the id is a member; then 404 `no-member` for an id the members table lacks; then the entries as stored, their MAC unchecked (the approving device checks it). Posting is unchanged. A removed member's entries stay stored until they expire and read as 404 `no-member`, the same as any id the head doesn't list. Tests came first, and the four new cases failed on the old code, where an admin's read of another member answered the admin's own empty list: `org-members.test.ts` covers the admin's read, no id, one's own id (admin and not), a non-admin's 403, an unknown id and a removed member's lingering entry; `org-boundaries.test.ts` adds `pendingDevicesOf` to the admin-only calls refused to a demoted or removed member, and the old Registry's 503 while one's own read still answers 200. reviewer-high found nothing material in one round. 129. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O8, at M5's landing (PR #323, merge 0db3870a0): rows name their owners, and a People toggle shows others' sessions where there are any.** M5 (O8's Interfaces §L) puts owners on the sidebar. A project or session row whose owners include someone besides you reads them as a label of two entries at most, 'you' among them ('you, Ana +1'), with names derived from the held head's member emails by `memberName` (`src/shared/org/member-name.ts`: the local part, capitalised, widened to the whole email where two collide); an id the head no longer lists reads 'former member'. `OrgRows` now carries `members` and the signed-in `member`, and a project counts others' sessions as '+N others'. The People toggle, on project and machine rows, hides or shows others' sessions; hidden rows add no activity, so what you can't see doesn't reorder your list, and the choice persists under `sidebar.people`. Two decisions changed in the build. The toggle shows only where it can act, when the row has others' sessions or while it is on: the first build showed it on every row, a dead control for anyone with no org, and its hover action took the line-center click of `e2e/shell.spec.ts:50` at the 200px floor in the final gate (run 40786951312); the alternative, every org-keyed row, still offered a toggle with nothing behind it. 130. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O8, at the landing of M4's re-plan (PR #328, merge 0f5e4ee87): the Org page is three milestones, because the app would be the first implementation of member and grant administration, not a port.** O8's research on master found no admin verb anywhere that adds or removes a member, changes a role or an email, renews a delegation or issues a grant; nothing signs a grant outside test vectors. Grants are not in `OrgState` but separate `Signed<Grant>` records held by the hosting machine's ledger and delivered through `endpoint.grant`, so a re-enrolled machine refuses every old grant and the app needs its own record of the grants it issued. The app's `StateSigner` signs whole states only, so pairing your own device needs `endorseDevice` under the step-up key and a grant needs `signRecord`, both new signing paths behind Touch ID. The architect's rulings, as re-planned: M4a, executable, carries Q1's four groups, the read-only views, member edits (add, remove, role, email; Q1's People group has 'Add person', so members stay in M4a), device edits (pairing from the app with your own devices endorsed in one Touch ID, another member's first device through H1, removal, root add), each through a preview and change pair bound to a digest of what was shown, `refused.attempt`, and `OrgSection.tsx` split by group; M4b (machines and grants) and M4c (delegations) stay advisory with these facts written in. No app-side copies kept in step by parity tests: `successorProblems`, one pure pre-sign function for ordinary successors, joins `apps/org-protocol` beside `verifySuccessor`, which is unchanged, and both admin's `amend` and the app run it; the pairing judges move there too, as a mechanical first commit the desk track reviews, with its three conditions in M4a's steps (admin from the deploy checkout loads the moved code, checked read-only and by a direct unbundled import since `deploy/bin.ts` bundles admin; a clean wrangler dry-run when M4b moves `encodeEnrollmentCodeV2`; #261 and the move merge, never rebase). `close()` fences and joins every writer under `org/`, which retires the two test workarounds and closes Decision Log 129's tracker row. One correction to the architect's wording, adopted: a member's later device can be paired by that member only if the member signs, since only a root signs a state; a non-signing member's later device has no route until the countersign flow (RL §5.2, deferred by Decision Log 34), and the page says so where the action would be. The hub rebuilds its members table from each accepted head, so 'Add person' and then that person's first device through H1 works end to end, which M4a's local test proves with a second identity. Buildability: two doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer rounds. The first found 1 P1 (pairing approval not bound to the fingerprint compared; the candidate is now frozen when found and approved by its generation and digest, and a same-id repost with other keys ends the pairing), 5 P2 and 1 P3, all adopted; the second confirmed them closed and found two more, adopted as the reviewer prescribed (liveness checked after Touch ID's confirmation, since `apply` runs before it, and `close()` also joining the held view's own adoption queue). Both rounds kept members in M4a.

133. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O2, at the landing of the desk track's PR #324 (merge ce9d875cf): MAWS's three long-lived Node processes run one memory-reducing collection at each entry into idle, which puts the daemon shape 6.6 to 7.8% under acceptance 14's bound on master's own tree and settles Decision Log 124's owed (2).** Why: each process grows V8's young generation to 16 MiB in its start and, when V8 shrinks it, keeps those pages for a run-dependent share of runs, which no later collection returns; collected at the first idle entry, while the pages are still the heap's, `mawsd` held 27.8 to 29.8 MB and the engine host 64.9 to 67.1 MB in the daemon scene on the MacBook, against 29.2 to 40.9 and 65.5 to 88.6 plain. The lever: `mawsd` (`DaemonClocks.quiet`), the engine host (`Supervision.quiet`) and the pty host (`PtyTable.quiet`) each tell the collector their quiet at every reading, and the collector asks again synchronously at the moment it collects, so a late busy signal can only delay a collection; an entry whose heap grew under 1 MiB collects nothing unless a dropped holding forces it; the gc extension is made in one fresh context with `--expose-gc` on only for that moment, so no global `gc` exists; each collection logs its duration and heap; the busy-source tables pin the class, not the instance (17 rows on the engine host, 4 on `mawsd`, 2 on the pty host). The pause measured 26 to 123 ms over 23 MacBook runs up to 30 long replayed sessions, driven by live small objects (about 25 ms plus 0.4 ms per MiB of object-dense live heap), with one unexplained 104 to 108 ms outlier at about 96 MiB used; the architect ruled no design change and no heap-size guard, since the cost is a one-time delay to the next request after an idle entry and a guard would take the reclaim from the heaviest users, and no row unless real use logs a pause past 100 ms. Rejected: a 1 MiB semi-space cap, the same footprint for a flag at every spawn (`--js-flags` in utility processes and the LaunchAgent's argv); `--semi-space-growth-factor=1` set in-process, which works only because V8 reads it at heap setup. The proof ran on the mini inside CI-opt's measured hold and was judged by the burst-tolerant load rule the architect ruled at 03:25Z, before any of these runs: a window is void only when it peaks at 5 or more and two adjacent 30 s intervals of the deconvolved load reach 4.7. Its verdicts came from the shipped `invalidReasons` and `loadPair` at a2fc850c6 of the load-rule branch, replayed on each run's recorded samples, never by hand, and every e2e:daemon-idle run of the series that the rule finds valid counts. Four runs on the merged head ad096787f: 92,579, 92,083, 91,891 and 91,395 KiB (40814726160, 40820891730, 40820904200, 40822960789), each at most 94,188, 5% under 99,145, the smallest margin 6.62%; `mawsd` 28,193 to 28,817, 624 KiB; CPU at most 0.13 s against 1.12 s. Two of them were void under the peak rule on silent thread wakes in which no process showed CPU. The A-B-A on the head (40816219223) was void under both rules on XProtect Remediator's scans; as data it read x1.454 against x1.452 before the lever, so the lever lowers the engine host, which both shapes run, and the margin comes from there rather than from the daemon shape's own cost. Master's additions since the lever's own tree 781b88cb0 (lane A's ipc tables, the people-sidebar layout schema and `OrgMemberSchema` in the host chunks, `mawsd`'s re-split index chunk) are a measured +0.8 MiB in the engine host's mean over six valid windows on each tree; the architect's earlier condition that the head read within 1 MiB of 781b88cb0 was retired once the landing tree carried its own series. The enrolled increment on the same head, run 40826529043, read +5,456 KiB (unenrolled 90,643, enrolled 96,099, `mawsd` +5,904 and the engine host -448), inside Decision Log 114's 8,192 KiB budget and valid by the same rule (void under the peak rule on one sample of 5.5), the first valid reading on a tree with the collection (the earlier valid one, 40735795915 at +5,728, predated it, and 40745657584's +9,184 was `mawsd`'s young-generation spread). One record stated as it was: O2 landed (Decision Log 107) on a series in which 40634705341, at 103,683 KiB, sat above the then-bound 101,113; void as a burst under the peak rule, it is a valid miss under the burst-tolerant rule, so O2's daemon shape was over its bound in one valid reading. This PR is what puts master inside 99,145; no earlier record is restated. Gates on the head's tree: class 1a with the supervisor, supervisor-daemon, daemon-unit, terminal, widgets and desk-web-hub groups (40812803836, five of them placed on the EC2 Mac by the overflow gate-offload Decision Log 145 meant only for unlaned calls); the other hosted groups, browser-comment, unit:darwin and packaged (40825261353); e2e:default on the mini (40831490156). Master had moved since the PR's base by #329's presentations export, #335's gate scripts and #334's lane A fixes, whose engine host changes (`fs/backfill.ts`, `fs/summarize.ts`, `shared/wire/transcript.ts`) no file or test of this PR imports, so the merge commit was not gated again. The tracker row for acceptance 14's headroom closes, and a row holds the burst-tolerant rule until its own PR lands it on master.
134. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O8 and the desk track, at the landing of the desk track's PR #338 (merge 291491219): the hub refuses a machine key that another ref already holds, whatever route the key came by, and the org has one member email grammar, strict where an email is written; the PR also lands O8 M4a's first two tasks, which it was built on.** `POST /machine/key` answers `409 key-held` when the posted key is held under another ref by any holder in the head's state (machines, devices, delegations, the recovery key), by any key the head holds retired or revoked, the machine's own included, or by another enrollment's posted key; the architect ruled that kept certificates count in both directions, since otherwise posting order would decide which ref a key speaks for and `machineSigners` and `machineOwners` could name different owners for one key, so `/machine/key` refuses a key a kept certificate holds under another ref and `storeCert` refuses a certificate whose key the head or another enrollment holds under another ref (mawsd's provision then exits on `CertRefused`, `409 key-held`); each direction has a case that fails without it. remote-architecture agreed and needed no change to the trust model: everything a machine key signs binds its own ref inside the signature, so the hub's refusal is a courtesy that verifiers need not mirror. `isMemberEmail` (org-protocol `names.ts`) is the one grammar, the address as Access names one; `successorProblems` (`email-grammar`) and the admin's genesis hold writers to it, readers stay tolerant (`isOrgState`, `verifySuccessor`, the hub's lower-cased resolve), and the app's `hub-client` reads `/api/me`'s email only in that grammar: the architect's earlier word that this reader was tolerant was wrong about the code, and it stays strict on purpose, so that a hostile hub can't reflect the bearer token as an email, with that reason in the code; a later grammar change checks the stored emails first. The regular expression is the one the hub client already had, so the app's behaviour does not change. Carried in from O8 M4a, as sequenced: Task 1, the pairing judges moved from `deploy/admin.ts` into org-protocol (336af704b), and Task 2, `successorProblems` and `stateProblems` with diagnostics bounded before authentication and a comment that their full mode is not for unauthenticated input (5935f62a5, 592211929, 37ce2077d), both reviewed clean under O8 and approved by the desk track. O8 M4a, landing second, switches `edits.ts` to `isMemberEmail`, adds `email-grammar` to its plan's pre-sign codes, and takes this PR's change to its 'twin posts m1's key' case, now a key another signer lists because the hub refuses the twin. Gates: class 1a with hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` and `e2e:desk-web` on the head's tree (40835319298, no flaky line; 40834069677 before master's merge); reviewer-high clean on 37ce2077d..a650031ce and on the certificate delta. The hub change takes effect only with a production deploy of `desk-hub`, which is the owner's. Residue in the tracker: staging's `OrgAdmin` composes without `successorProblems`, and no case revokes an enrollment during `keepMachineKey`'s key-id await.
135. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O9 and the desk track, at the landing of the desk track's PR #339 (merge 2505e72e3, with sminos 1.27.12, doperpowers #442 at 28f5a49f7): DX4's `step-end` carries the step's end time `t` and its `model` and `effort`, each optional and additive, so that a remote lane A session gets the two fixes of O9's sitting that read them.** O9's remote half found that Stop's partial text places a cut reply by its step-end's `t` (`placeCuts`), and that the model chip reads the latest main-loop step's model and effort, while DX4's `step-end` carried only `{turnId, agentId, step}` and DX4 has no step start; a time stamped on the receiving Mac is the wrong clock and is wrong under replay. The architect asked the desk track for the fields, ahead of B2's sminos update on the mini. Semantics: `t` is the DX5 stamp of the event that ended the step (its step end, or its turn's completion for a step cut off by it); `model` and `effort` come from the step's DX5 start with the main loop's bridge override applied, `effort: null` passed through when the request named none; a field the plane doesn't know is absent (a watch that joins mid-step has the snapshot's `model` and no `effort`); the bridge holds a step's end up to 1 s while its blocks get rows, so `t` can trail the request's finish by up to 1 s, as local lane A sees. MAWS's side: `StepEnd` in `apps/desk-hub/src/protocol/session.ts`, the fake machine and the DX4 test reader pass the fields through, and lane A's overlay reads `t` as optional. O9's M9 builds the fallbacks (a remote cut stays unplaced; the chip shows the override, else `read status`'s model) and reads the fields whenever present, with cases for a step-end with and without them, so a sminos carrying them needs no MAWS change. The mini ran sminos 1.27.11 at B2's step 1, before #442; the desk track has mini-ops update it to 1.27.12 before O9's M10 sitting. Gates: class 1a with hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` and `e2e:desk-web` on the head's tree (40835962872, no flaky line); reviewer-medium found nothing in either repository; master had moved since the head's base by #324, #337 and #338, none of whose files this PR touches, and the change is additive, so the merge commit was not gated again.
136. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O14 and O2, at the landing of PR #343 (merge 5d31f621f, from the desk track): `mawsd endpoint attest-history` waits up to an hour, and one run goes at a time.** B2's step 9 on the Mac mini (about 390 DX1 writers) never got a verdict: the command reached the daemon as `daemon.endpoint` under the client's default 10 s deadline (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`), while it reads every inventory writer's events from the hub page by page, so the CLI gave up and the daemon ran on with its outcome unseen. The session running B2's steps then retried on the timeout: two attest runs, overlapping, and about 24 checks, one about every 25 s, between 08:45 and 08:56Z, each a full read of the production hub's history beside the ones still running. The hub answered normally after it (08:57Z), the mini's `mawsd` was idle by 08:57Z, and its log shows no hub error; nobody read the hub's own logs or Durable Object state, which only the owner reads (wrangler against production), so that check of 08:45 to 08:57Z is on the owner's list. The two overlapping attest runs may have posted the same writers twice; the first `--check` after this fix shows whether the stored attestations verify. The fix: `attest-history`, attesting and `--check` alike, sends `daemon.endpoint` with a 60 min deadline (`ATTEST_HISTORY_TIMEOUT_MS` in `src/mawsd/cli.ts`), every other endpoint command keeping 10 s and the shared default unchanged; a fixed deadline rather than `--wait`, since the module waits only on its own hub reads, each bounded at 30 s by the hub client. The endpoint module keeps the run in progress per endpoint and refuses a second one, check or attest, naming the run and its start, rather than joining it (a second caller's names, project or output may differ, and a check beside an attest would judge records still being posted); each run logs its start and its outcome in `mawsd`'s log and holds `mawsd` against its idle exit until it ends. The tests fail first with the fix stashed: through the real `machine.sock`, attest-history is answered past 10 s while `status` beside it still gives up at 10 s; against a stand-in hub holding its first read, a second check, an attest and a narrowed check are refused without reading the hub, and a run goes ahead after a finished or failed one. The rule this leaves for any long endpoint command, which the desk track's O14 record writes into B3's and B4's runbook text: one run, the caller waits for the command's own deadline, never a retry on a timeout, then one `--check`. Gates: class 1a pool and hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` (39 passed), run 40847959810 on eb72b2ced, its tree the head's, no flaky line; the head sat on master c6c523547, unmoved. reviewer-medium found nothing material (its lens the hold and its release paths: the hold affects only the idle exit and quiet collection, never quit, restart or status).

138. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O10 and O14, at the landing of PR #345 (merge a05b85331, from the desk track): a lane A question raised after its project opened reaches the hub's presence and the phone.** B2's step 7 (the phone card test, the Mac mini on the RL10 plane) failed: no pending request from any mini session reached the hub, so the Cards view showed nothing waiting and the endpoint kept no `notified.json`. The endpoint's Notifier counted only a project's whole pending set, which sminos sends when it opens the project (after every RL10 welcome), while each question raised afterwards, and each answer, travels as a single sub-less `raised` or `settled` session message that the link handed only to PlaneA; so no raise after a project opened ever reached `notify waiting`. Now the Notifier keeps each project's lane A requests by id for each session: a whole set replaces what was held for its project, a sub-less `raised` adds its one request and a `settled` naming it takes it away (a raise already held, or a settlement of a request not held, moves nothing), and every change of a session's count is told to the hub once as before (`src/endpoint/notify.ts`, `src/endpoint/rl10/link.ts`). Tests fail first without the fix: the records suite drives raises and settlements between sets, and the RL10 conversation fixture gains the raise-and-decline exchange (`test/endpoint/rl10/conversation.json`; sminos keeps a copy of that fixture, which the desk track refreshes in doperpowers after this landing). On desk-hub-staging a blocking ask set `notified.json` to pending 1 and the hub's presence to waiting 1, both cleared on decline; the control run without the fix stayed at `{}` after 90 s. Gates: class 1a pool and hosted `e2e:desk-web-hub` on the daemon-shape harness (39 passed), run 40852192868 on a9796a478 (the fix with master bd6b4b097 merged), its tree the head's, no flaky line. reviewer-high found no material defect; its one P2 is the sminos copy above. The mini's app is rebuilt from master with this and #343 for B2's steps 7 and 9, outside the measured series' hold.
139. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O2, at the landing of the desk track's PR #340 (merge b22b7e419): the measured idle windows void on sustained load, not on a burst (Rule B, as Decision Log 133 ruled it).** `test/support/daemon-idle.ts` rebuilds each 30 s interval's own load from the 1-minute series (the exact inverse of its 60 s exponential average for a load constant over the interval), takes the window's 30 s pair as the highest load two adjacent intervals both held, and voids a window on load only when its peak sample reached 5 and its 30 s pair reached 4.7; the pair only guards the peak, so every window valid before stays valid, which a seeded spread of 4,003 series pins, and the void reason names both numbers. Every line that prints a load prints the pair beside the peak, and each sample records the machine's CPU times, busy share and top process (a process table read in the test runner, outside the measured set). The boundary windows are unit fixtures copied literally from their runs' recorded series: the sustained-indexer baselines stay void, and four burst-only windows (40805962574, 40809164967, 40502284016, 40634705341) now pass. This supersedes the validity sentence of the O2 plan's M1 acceptance (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-o2-mawsd.md`, the 1-minute load under 5 at every sample), which now points here. Proof: one e2e:daemon-idle run on the mini on the merged head e78150ab3 inside CI-opt's measured hold, run 40852439950, green: peak 3.8 (the burst sampler's 5 s peak 3.88, none at 5), footprint 91,987 KiB (median of 21) against the bound 99,145, a 7.22% margin (`mawsd` 28,625, `maws-supervisor` 63,361), CPU 0.12 s over 600 s against 1.12; inside every range of #324's landing set (91,395 to 92,579 KiB, `mawsd` 28,193 to 28,817, CPU 0.11 to 0.13), so the new per-sample table left the measured set unchanged. The table's own cost on the mini is unread, since a valid run uploads no `daemon-idle.json`; the MacBook's reading is about 60 ms of CPU per table, once every 30 s, and the architect took the unchanged measured set as enough. Class 1a pool 40851347615 on e78150ab3, its tree the head's; the master moves after it (#345 under src/endpoint, and records) touch nothing the branch reaches. The tracker row that held Rule B off master is closed.

The app is the first place a member is added or removed, a role or an email changed, a device paired or removed, or a root added: pairing your own device endorses it in one Touch ID, another member's first device goes through the hub's admin read (H1), and every change goes through a preview and a change bound to a digest of what was shown, so a change the person did not see is refused, and a refusal carries the attempt (`OrgRefusal.attempt`). 144. **2026-10-29, the architect, under O9, at the landing of PR #355 (merge 01d07b75e): lane A shows the permission mode a terminal session reports, from sminos 1.27.14 on.** sminos 1.27.14 (doperpowers #445, board #160) carries `permissionMode`, `permissionModeAt` and `permissionModeFrom` on the bridge's heartbeat and `read status`. `src/shared/lane-a/mode.ts` parses them, a report counting only when all three are present, well typed and the word is one of the engine's six modes, and guards the bridge's version; `LaneAState.mode` carries the reading. Locally the driver reads it from the state stream's heartbeat (`src/services/lane-a/state.ts`, `driver.ts`); remotely `src/main/lane-a/remote-state.ts` reads it from the status read the adapter already makes and holds it to the launch it was read under, so a session resumed in a new process under the same engine id never shows the previous process's mode (reviewer-medium's one P2, fixed in 7839ead63 with unit and integration cases that fail without it, re-reviewed clean). The Mode chip shows the engine's title for the mode (`default` is 'Manual'), its hover gives the report's age refreshed every 30 s, and its menu checks the mode with every choice still closed, since MAWS sets no mode on a terminal session; Session info shows the mode with its age, and a remote session's Session info gains a Permissions group holding the Mode row; 'Not reported by the terminal session' stays for a session with no report and for a bridge older than 1.27.14. Freshness: a local switch shows within one heartbeat (5 s), because the engine's `permission_mode_changed` record reports Shift+Tab as it happens; a remote one shows at the next status read (a watch opening, a model change, or a main-loop step-end at most every 10 s), so an idle remote session's switch waits for its next turn, recorded as a tracker row. 152. **2026-10-30, the architect, under O3, at the landing of PR #369 (merge 67f4baff4): every main-process, host and preload bundle is emitted as Latin-1 text, so V8 holds its source at a byte a character, about 6 MB off the daemon shape's idle footprint.** O3-D's executor traced the rise of `mawsd`'s idle reading on M5's mini run (33,457 KiB, against M2's 30,849 to 31,537 and `MAWSD_HEADROOM_KIB` 33,048) mostly to the scene (compiled code and zod's parsers left warm by an app's turn, about 1 to 1.5 MB, the object diff after release clean) and about 0.3 to 0.4 MB to code, and found a larger lever: Node keeps every loaded file's source in the heap, and one character above U+00FF anywhere in a file makes V8 hold the whole file at two bytes a character. `scripts/latin1-bundles.ts` is an output plugin on main's and the preloads' builds (the `?modulePath` hosts inherit it): each chunk is re-printed by esbuild with `charset: 'ascii'`, acorn places any leftover (comments and regular expression literals take `\uXXXX`; anything else, a tagged template's raw text among them, fails the build), the build fails if any emitted `.js` file still holds such a character, and turning source maps on is refused until they are chained. Measured on the MacBook by V8 heap after a forced GC: the engine host idle 4.24 MiB less, `mawsd` released 1.55 MiB less on O3-D's tip. An AST comparison of master's and the head's builds found 249 files byte-identical and 63 re-printed with the same tree, none different; functions shipped as source (`executeJavaScript`, the search worker) are byte-identical. Gates: `--gates all` 41037672797 and the hosted lane with `packaged` 41020368991, both on the head's tree bc155c253, no flaky line; reviewer-medium found nothing material; CI-opt reviewed the build hunk (the guard case runs in unit 4/4 after its build, no build cost). The prototype's leftover rewrite would have changed tagged templates' raw text and regular expressions' identity escapes; the landed plugin refuses the first and folds the second. O3-D's M7 measured series runs on a tree with this change, and its bound numbers cite the ASCII build's readings. Rejected: zod's jitless mode in `mawsd` (1.2 MiB, at about 30 percent on a warm 5,000-session `session.list`), and re-printing the renderer bundles and the hosts' `node_modules` here (tracker rows).

157. **2026-10-30, the architect, at the landing of PR #374 (merge b3fef978d): conversation search indexes a growing transcript from where it stopped, so an append no longer re-reads the whole file.** The long-transcript diagnosis (the owner's report that old sessions would not open) found the idle CPU of a large store in `fs.sessionText`, which re-read every transcript from byte 0 on each size change, 2.1 to 3.5 s of CPU per pass on the largest. The index now stores a checkpoint with each text (the byte offset after the last consumed line, the inode, digests of the first and last 4 KiB before the offset), and the host resumes there when the inode, the size and both digests agree, otherwise reading from byte 0; a line counts only once complete, a pass cut short clears the checkpoint, and a prefix rewritten during a resumed pass fails it into a full one. A transcript already indexed is re-indexed at most once per 30 s (`SEARCH_REINDEX_DEBOUNCE_MS`), the first index after a start at once. Measured on a 383 MB synthetic transcript on the loaded MacBook: about 1.0 s of CPU per append before, about 1 ms after (about 21 KB read). This adds index migration 15 (`session_text_state.resume`) and takes `WIRE_MINOR` 8; the long-transcript window (its own PR, still in review) takes the next. Gates: the head's runs 41130970504 and 41133629428, then the merge with master after #376 in run 41135433376 (class 1a, unit:darwin, which the migration and the fs schema reach, and the nine e2e groups the planner named plus e2e:desk-web), and the final merge after #261 in run 41138230305 (class 1a, e2e:desk-web, e2e:desk-web-hub), no flaky line in any. reviewer-medium's two findings: a prefix rewritten during a resumed pass, fixed with a test; a same-length rewrite in the file's middle, which the cheap check cannot see and the engine never makes, a tracker row.
