158. **2026-10-30, the architect, under O10, at the landing of PR #378 (merge e56f3f047): an endpoint that holds an event whose signer it does not know asks the hub for the org state, so a newly certified machine's first events no longer wait for the 10-minute read.** The finding came from the session sminos-message-delivery-audit, passed on by the owner and verified on master by the desk track: storing a certificate moves no org-state version, the hub sends `state` only on a new version, so an endpoint learned of a new machine's certificate only at its `SYNC_EVERY_MS` read or at an RL1 welcome, and `dc-linux171-1e68`'s first say after its cutover waited about 2 min on the MacBook. RL10's relay now asks for a read when it holds an event for an unknown signer or key, or for a certificate lapsed at the event's `created`: at most once per key per 60 s, coalesced, per key so that a second new machine inside the window is not starved (the hub's own re-read is one global 1 s window). Retirement stays bounded at the 10-minute read by design (Decision Logs 23 and 24: retirement is not revocation, and remote-architecture adds that a retired key is one destroyed or never at risk, so nothing signs with it during the window; such events never become a person's words either), a tracker row. Revocation: the architect's two tests pass, on master too (a `state` notice adopted with one read and no tick; a revoked key's events refused at once after it). The one path that still waited, a notice whose read fails, the architect and remote-architecture (its message 44) ruled endpoint-side: `#syncTo` retries on the link's backoff toward the highest version announced until the head reaches it, the tick the backstop. A hub resend was rejected (an acknowledgement on the relay and an owner's deploy for the same bound), and so was refusing a carrier's events while behind an announced version: notices are unsigned, so a buggy or hostile hub could stall every event on a machine by announcing a version it never serves, and the guarantee against a hostile hub comes from direct delivery. The lag is then the hub's read unavailability, the right bound for a path that carries availability with an honest hub, and until O8's RL4 delivery with an acknowledgement (the remote-layer spec's revocation of record), `admin remove-device`'s 'within 10 minutes at worst' reads 'within the retry's backoff'. It is built in its own PR, stacked on this one. Gates: run 41144016007 on the head (class 1a) and run 41145108191 on the merge with master (class 1a, e2e:desk-web, and e2e:desk-web-hub, which the planner names for the endpoint files), no flaky line; reviewer-medium twice.

161. **2026-10-30, the architect, under O2 and O14, at the landing of PR #381 (merge 3e94bde9f): a `mawsd` whose bundle was replaced under it launches nothing from the new bundle; a unit daemon restarts itself, and a private one refuses with the reason until the next app restarts it.** At O3-D's upgrade of both Macs (the MacBook at 20:53Z, the mini at 23:08Z, reported by the desk track) the old `mawsd`, running on across the app's quit and swap, launched each host three times from its own build's content-hashed entry chunk, which the swapped `app.asar` no longer held (`Cannot find module` on `out/main/index-<hash>.js` in both hosts' logs), and marked both hosts crashed until the new app's build check restarted it. With nothing live that cost about 3 s; a swap followed by any other client first (the sminos bridge, a remote request) would have left both hosts crashed indefinitely. Before each host spawn `mawsd` now reads its entry again (`currentBuildIdOf`, `src/engine-host/supervisor-files.ts`) and compares it with the build id it computed at start. On a mismatch the detached transport's new `beforeSpawn` ends the attempt as `notLaunched`, counting no exit: a `--unit` daemon stops with exit 75, so its unit restarts it from the new bundle and it adopts the running hosts; a `--private` daemon stays up, serves what runs, fails held requests with the reason and parks the host, so the next request checks again. Electron holds each archive it opened for the life of the process, so an ordinary read, even `existsSync`, still answers from the old archive after a swap; the check therefore parses the archive now at that path, with `process.noAsar` set inside one synchronous call (about 2 ms on a 100 MB archive). The same hold makes `mawsd`'s in-process deferred loads (the spine's services chunk, the endpoint chunk) read the old build, shown by a probe on the pinned Electron; from a checkout's `out/main` those loads go through the same check. Linux releases are immutable and the entry is resolved through `current`'s real path, so a flip never reads as a swap. No wire change. Proof: unit tests of both daemon kinds and a process test that swaps a built `out/main` under a running `mawsd`, each failing without its fix (the process scene then shows the original three exits). Gates: runs 41145053613 and 41145178351 (class 1a; e2e:supervisor-daemon, daemon-unit, machines, supervisor, default) and 41146823343 on the head, and run 41147865133 on the merge with master after #377 (`--gates all`), no flaky line; reviewer-medium's one finding, a private daemon left crashed with a stale refusal when the incompatible engine host it waited on exited after the swap, fixed with a test. The field check is the next upgrade of either Mac: no three-exit line in `mawsd.log`.
162. **2026-10-30, the architect, under O10, at the landing of PR #382 (merge 2d88524f1): an endpoint whose read fails after a `state` notice retries until its head reaches the announced version, so a revocation no longer waits for the 10-minute read.** Built as the architect and remote-architecture ruled at #378 (Decision Log 158): `#syncTo` keeps the highest version announced, and a chase retries through the link's `RecordRetries` under the key `sync` (about 1 s first, about 7 s after three failures) until the head reaches it, settling on any adoption that reaches it, on stop or at the link's end; the next welcome reads anyway, and the 10-minute read stays the backstop. Until O8's RL4 delivery with an acknowledgement, this is `admin remove-device`'s path, and its 'within 10 minutes at worst' reads 'within the retry's backoff'. A fail-first test: a revocation notice whose two reads both fail is adopted at the first retry, and the revoked key's event is refused at once. Gates: run 41146987836 on the head (static, unit, e2e:desk-web, e2e:desk-web-hub) and run 41148010890 on the merge with master after #377 and #381 (the same set with probes), no flaky line; reviewer-medium twice, its one finding (an adoption that reaches the version now settles the chase) fixed.
163. **2026-10-31, the architect, as P5's writer under O3 (its acceptance 5, raised by O3's recomposition audit): OX1's exception covers the larger residual O3-D built and measured, and the contract now bounds the residual by the landing that moves the state instead of limiting it to writes made by hand.** Decision Log 101 ruled on what a refused pre-O3-D app can write by hand beside a post-D `mawsd`. O3-D found two more ways such an app shares the profile. (1) Its `daemon.restart` is honoured (O3-D's D29): O2's build check makes it replace or re-install its own daemon, the post-D `mawsd` yields to it within seconds, and the old app then runs its spine alone, one writer; refusing it would leave that app with no engine host and no daemon of its own. (2) An app from before O3-A takes no `spine.lock`, so its main spine runs from its launch until `mawsd` yields on Electron's `SingletonLock` signal: in that window it writes the spine's non-index files (overlaps of 356 and 628 ms measured, bounded by `REFUSAL_TO_RELEASE_MS`, 5 s from the refusal), never `index.sqlite`, which it opens read-only at version 14 against its 13, and the Needs-you items raised while it was away never reach it (O3 Decision Log 19(4)). Ruled acceptable: only a downgrade or an old app left installed meets either case, the supported path is upgrade, both Macs already run post-D builds, and a shipped build cannot be changed to take the lock. O3's acceptance 5 is met with this residual, and OX1's sentence and O3 §2 now say so. Rejected: refusing `daemon.*` from a refused app, which strands it; holding the yield until the old app shows it has stopped writing, which a pre-A build has no way to signal.

171. **2026-10-31, the architect, under O4 and O5 and for the gates every child shares: a connector sign-in outlives the app that asked for it, an Electron root that fails before `ready` writes and exits instead of raising a dialog, and the enrollment prompt's echo race fixed in the product (PR #405, merge 58ebdfacc).** O4 M2: a Connect whose asking app leaves after the browser opened stays alive in `mawsd` until its 120 s deadline, and a consent the owner completes meanwhile seals the grant, because the grant is the machine's and the consent was given; cancelling at the asker's `clientGone` would discard a completed consent and leave a sign-in that silently did not take. Acceptance 1's `browser` failure covers only an asker gone before the hand-off. Two conditions bind it: an app that next connects sees the connector signed in through `connectors.signInChanged`, never a stuck connecting state, and the loopback redirect listener lives no longer than the flow's deadline. O5 M3 (advisory, for every Electron root): under Playwright's loader an exception thrown before `ready` shows only as an `electron.launch` timeout with nothing on stderr, because Electron's modal error dialog holds Chromium's start; it cost O5 two gate rounds (`require.main` is unset under the loader, so a relative read threw). The render host now writes its failure with `writeSync` and exits 70, which a windowless process under launchd needs anyway; any other root started without a window takes the same rule. PR #405: the `cli-prompt` ^C flake, nine sightings across children that never touched `src/mawsd/`, was a race in the product: `hiddenPrompt` wrote the question before `setRawMode(true)`, so input arriving the moment the prompt showed met a cooked terminal, echoed and with ^C turned into SIGINT. A person typing could not hit a window of two back-to-back calls, but a program answering the prompt (a script, an agent in a pty) could get the enrollment code echoed into the terminal and its scrollback. Raw mode now comes first, as getpass(3) orders it; the ^C case checks the terminal is raw at the prompt, and the test helper reports the output it actually timed out on. Reproduced by a 300 ms pause between the two calls (three of four tests failed) and passing in the new order; 30 of 30 local runs under load. Gates: class 1a (41215002392); reviewer-medium found nothing material. The tracker row of 2026-10-28 keeps only its two remaining items. 172. **2026-10-31, the architect, after O3: the renderer's other first reads survive an update's `mawsd` restart, and a snapshot not yet loaded never trims the restored layout (PR #406, merge 155eb0007).** Decision Log 170 found that the same restart window reaches the reads besides the launch mode. One helper (`src/renderer/src/ipc/until-answered.ts`, which #403's launch-mode ask now uses) asks a read again when main reports the local machine's `host.status`, and at once if one arrived while the read was out; a rejected call counts as unanswered. It now serves the local snapshot in `events.ts` (`session.list`, `session.projects.list`, `session.digests`, `attention.list`), `session.intent.list`, `session.deletions.list` and the local `laneA.list`, which buffers its change events again while a retry is out. The invariant: the local machine counts as loaded only once its records and the tab retention's intents are in, and until then its tabs are kept as a connecting remote machine's already are, so `repairMain` drops nothing and nothing trimmed is saved; `settleMachine` then drops what is really gone. A failed `session.intent.list` is now unknown, not empty, and a restored tab's empty state no longer closes it as gone while its machine is unloaded (the review's one finding, which also protects a connecting remote machine's tabs). Normal startup is unchanged: hydration waits for the first answers, not for success. Left as is: `attention.pendingNavigate` is main's and never crosses the restart; another machine's failed snapshot is retaken at its next link generation, since `host.status` speaks only for the local machine; and whether the sidebar's skeleton waits for the local machine to load is a UX call, registered as board #407 (deferred). Gates: all on the hosted lane (41211435139), its tree the PR head's, no flaky test, the cross-build scene (update, mawsd restart) now also writing a session tab into the profile's layout and reading it back whole; reviewer-medium's one P2 fixed and re-reviewed clean. It landed without a re-gate on the merge: master had moved by #404 (gate-plan) and #405 (the enrollment prompt), neither on its path. The wave 2 ledger gains O7 M2's history names (`remote-history.ts`, `remote.historyTooLarge`).
173. **2026-10-31, the architect, for O3-D's spine and O16's handover: a spine load caused only by the engine host's events no longer wakes an engine host that no one wants (PR #409, merge d3b3ea2c8), and a machine that another paired app is watching stays loaded.** O16 M2b found the race. After the engine host was asked to exit when idle, its link closed and the index source's `fs.bind` failed, but `sync()` still sent `fs.state` while the supervisor showed the host restarting. The supervisor held that request and, at the deliberate close, launched a successor, so a host that was parking came back. The fix: each spine load is marked wanted or not from its trigger. A command's or the endpoint's `ensure` makes it wanted, and so does a later need on a load the host's events started (`src/mawsd/spine-seam.ts`). Every spine request to the engine host passes through `SpineHostsAdapter.dispatch` (`src/mawsd/spine-hosts.ts`). For a load that is not wanted, while the host is not ready, it refuses the request with `host_unavailable` and never hands it to the supervisor. Nothing is lost: every part that talks to the engine host repeats its requests at each welcome. At the host's ready that means `engine.configure`, `engine.policy.set`, `host.configure` and `artifacts.setDefaults`; at its streaming, the index sync; at each barrier, the resync. Two alternatives were rejected. Skipping requests to a parking host fails because the spine cannot see that exit when idle is armed, and a ready but armed host may still serve live rows. Never sending anything from an event-caused load fails because a host welcomed while the spine was cold would never be configured. O16's handover must keep this invariant: a draining or exiting host, or a successor not yet welcomed, counts as not ready, and the successor must raise the link's ready and streaming transitions. Gates: static, unit, probes, `e2e:supervisor-daemon`, `e2e:supervisor`, `e2e:daemon-unit` and `e2e:settings` passed in run 41220396581, whose tree is the fix commit's, with no flaky test; `unit:darwin` is not reached. Master had moved only by #408, which changes CI only. Reviewer-medium found one real gap, and it predates this PR. On the daemon shape, a command whose handler waits for the session service to boot never answers while the spine is cold and the engine host parked, because only an app attaching or the discovery wake starts a parked host. That is P5's core scenario: a paired app opening a session on a machine whose own app is quit. So it goes to a follow-up PR on master now, not to the tracker row #409 added. The desk track's item 5 found the MacBook's spine still loaded three hours after its app quit at 14:02:42Z, with no quiet entry when the app left. The mini's app, paired minutes earlier, kept an endpoint channel open to the MacBook. Every open channel holds `mawsd` (`hold('channel')` in `src/endpoint/channels.ts`); the daemon's quiet requires no holds, and the spine is released only when the daemon is quiet. The engine host's park clock looks only at apps and interest, so the host parked as usual. Ruling: a machine that another paired app is watching stays loaded, so that app's reads answer without first waiting about 2.5 s for the spine to load. Item 5's cold reading will be taken while no other paired app has a channel open. The tech-debt tracker gains a row for `e2e:discover`, whose catalog-backed assertions use the default 5 s timeout; it missed twice today, and each time the rerun passed.
174. **2026-10-31, the architect, for O3-D's spine and every child that touches `mawsd`: a command that waits on the engine host now wakes a parked one (PR #411, merge 4118fa60a), and O4 M2 is accepted at its first stop.** #411 treats the wait itself as the need. Every session-service command waits in `SessionService.ready()`. While the boot has not finished waiting for the host's first streaming, that wait calls `host.need()`, which the spine root passes to its hosts. The adapter forwards the need only for a wanted load, so a load caused by an event still wakes nothing (Decision Log 173's rule). It keeps the need until the load streams, and tells it again if the link leaves ready first. That re-telling fixes reviewer-medium's one finding: a need told while the barrier resynced left the boot stranded when the host had been asked to exit when idle. `mawsd`'s `needEngine` does two things. It restarts the park clock from the need; without that, the woken host is asked to park the moment it is ready, and the next idle cycle's command never answers. It also sends `engine.ping` to a host that is not streaming, which the supervisor holds and launches a parked host for. A need that arrives during a discovery wake leaves that wake's park to the clock. Two alternatives were rejected. A list of the commands that need the host (`beforeSpineCommand`'s pattern) would have to be kept in step with the handlers. `hosts.wake` acts only on a host already parked, not on one still closing. Tests that fail on master: a daemon-in-process test in which a `machine.sock` `session.intent.list` and the endpoint's `session.create` and `session.acquire` answer within the discovery wake's 8 s bound across two idle cycles, each cycle ending parked and cold, while `attention.list` wakes nothing; four seam cases; two clock cases; and `e2e:supervisor-daemon`'s case (n), in which a parked host is woken over `machine.sock` and the private daemon then exits by itself. Gates: static, unit, probes, `e2e:supervisor-daemon`, `e2e:supervisor`, `e2e:daemon-unit` and `e2e:settings` passed in run 41225519070, on the PR head's tree, with no flaky test; `unit:darwin` is not reached. #411 removes the tracker row #409 added; the merge's conflict with Decision Log 173's new tracker row was resolved by hand. O16's handover must keep three rules: a request to a parked or closing host is still held and launches a host, the old host leaving ready reaches the spine's link, and a need restarts the park clock. O4 M2 (code head a1d727f76, wire 13 against master's 12) is accepted at stop (1). Its gates: class 1a (41226571943); cross-build against the 3.12 peer (41225432077); `--gates all` on the hosted lane (41215859564, 48 jobs green, its three reds fixed and rerun green); `e2e:discover` on the mini (41222546081); `e2e:dashboard-live-data` (41223857261); and `e2e:delivery` with `e2e:connectors` (41226572706). O4 also found that `e2e:dashboard-live-data`'s long case has been red on master since UX2-L removed the header's More actions button. Nobody saw it because this opt-in spec runs in no gate job. It is fixed on O4's branch and lands with it. O4 goes on to M3 after merging master; the load its dataset refresh triggers must be wanted under Decision Log 173's rule. `process-yield.test`'s design-system seed flake reached its fourth sighting (O4's class 1a run 41225432077), and a worker is fixing the test on master. The wave 2 ledger gains #409's and #411's spine names, O4 M2's delivery log line and the shared file `e2e/support/cross-build-samples.ts`.

176. **2026-10-31, the architect, for O3-D's yield test: the design-system seed flake in `src/mawsd/process-yield.test.ts` is explained and fixed in the test (PR #415, merge 999739301).** The engine host seeds its built-in design systems at its first start over an empty store. The test loaded the spine with `session.list`, a discovery read that wakes a parked engine host (the wake O3-D's M6 added after the test was written), so the spine's library sync (`src/services/library/sync.ts`) could land between the test's before-read and the refusal: the built-ins added, the fixture's own row dropped by the reconcile, the FTS shadow tables rewritten. The yield itself writes nothing: at the refusal, release, settle and `stopRoot` run within microtasks on an idle root, and the library's `stop()` drops any later answer. The test now loads the spine with `attention.list`, which takes the same load path (the lock, the index migration, the parts started) and wakes no engine host; it asserts after the release that the daemon log has no `spawned a supervisor` line, and keeps the whole-index comparison, whose failure message now carries the daemon log. Waiting for the sync to end was tried twice and dropped: reviewer-medium found that each wait could accept early when the park cuts off queued reads and a successor host starts, and a forced run confirmed it. Under load the old test failed 1 in 160 and the new one passed 200 of 200; a write injected into the yield's stop fails it 3 of 3. Two readings recorded, neither changed here. O3-D's D53 (a), that a yield's writes are only its stores' close, holds for an idle root: a busy root's release waits for it, and the library sync, outside `settling` by design, can still apply host answers meanwhile. And the discovery wake parks the engine host while the library's first sync may still be in flight, so after a cold `session.list` the library can stay unreconciled (seeded design systems missing, stale rows listed) until the next host connection; that is a tracker row and an input to O4's library reads. Gates: static, unit, unit:darwin and probes (41233634236), its tree the PR head's; master had moved by docs only. The friction entry is resolved.

An idle exit closes the host's connection, so `detached-transport.ts` reports it disconnected and the link reads restarting; one pid poll later (200 ms), with the pid gone and the exit asked as idle, it reports a deliberate loss and the supervisor parks the host, or launches a successor at once when a request is held. In run 41231090341 (the merge gate of #410) the command's own spine load (169 to 178 ms) held the daemon's event loop past that poll, the need landed inside the window, the supervisor held the wake's `engine.ping`, and the successor started 3 and 14 ms after the need: #411's held-ping path working as intended, with only the logged state word differing. The test now accepts `which is parked: waking it` or `which is restarting: waking it`, neither of which can match the app's earlier starting needs, and still asserts the answer within the bound, a new engine host under another pid, the park asked at least the idle time after the need, and the daemon's own idle exit. Waiting for the park was rejected: nothing in the e2e shows it except the daemon's status read, whose supervisor status wakes the host itself. Found by reading the code, not observed: an idle exit whose process outlives its socket by more than one pid poll reads as a quiet loss, and the host is relaunched after the restart backoff instead of parked; a tracker row, and an input to O16. Gates: on the head, three runs of static and both supervisor groups with no flaky case (41232524507, 41233417502, 41235130794); on the merge, since master had moved by #410 in `src/mawsd/daemon.ts` and `src/shared/host/host-supervisor.ts`, run 41236214928 (static, e2e:supervisor-daemon, e2e:supervisor). 182. **2026-10-31, the architect, at the fix for the engine host's idle CPU after the MacBook's swap (PR #419, merge 2d24e589b).** From 20:35Z to about 21:00Z the MacBook's engine host ran at 225 to 290% CPU with no session working. The desk track traced it to `src/engine-host/fs/service.ts`: every change to an agent sidecar file re-ran a full `SubagentReader.list` walk of that session's subagents, for every bound session, and main's `fs.scan` binds them all. One session had 4,233 subagent files and a workflow writing about 75 a minute, and one walk of that tree costs about 0.6 to 0.8 s of CPU. The fix: `SubagentReader.update` brings a listing up to date from only the changed files and answers null (a full walk) for anything it cannot place; one pass at a time per session, change-driven passes at most every 100 ms after the first and 5 s after a failed one; and change-driven listing only for sessions something reads (a follow, or the clients' `host.interest`, which mawsd's interest union already carries for the app and for the endpoint's remote viewers). Every follower poke waits until the agent changes reported so far are read, because the engine writes a child's meta before the parent's `Agent` result. A committed bench (`subagents-bench.test.ts`, run only with `MAWS_BENCH=1`) reproduces the incident on 4,040 files: before, 220 walks and 271% CPU at 10 writes a second, and 441 walks, 325% and a 23.6 s backlog at 40; after, no walks and 1 to 2%. Reviewer-medium ran three rounds: four P2s in the follow ordering, all fixed with tests that fail on the earlier versions, then none. Gates: the worker's run on the head's tree (41251802912: static, unit, `unit:darwin`, probes and eight e2e groups, no flaky test), then the architect's `--gates all` on the hosted lane on the merge commit, since an engine host change reaches every group through the build: run 41253276757. Left, as tracker rows the PR adds: the 30 s reconcile tick still walks a session held open (about 0.7 s CPU for 4,000 files, about 2% of a core), and a reconcile or rescan poke inside the watcher's 50 ms debounce of a child's meta write can still fold the parent's result first, a window older than this change. A client that never reports `host.interest` no longer hears `fs.subagentsChanged`; every current app does. The fix reaches the owner's Macs at their next build swap.

184. **2026-11-01, the architect, O12 gains M11, the spawner as owner (the desk track's PR #423, merge 8ad896717, docs; static 41266345501 on a tree equal to the merge's).** O7 M4d found that a lane B session spawned from machine A cannot be opened until B's sminos lists it, because B's endpoint ranks a lane B session only from its sminos record (`sessionRank`). O7 waits main-side, up to 60 s, for a session it spawned itself, saying it is waiting for the machine to list it rather than that the machine cannot place it. Routing ruled: the launch environment's `MAWS_LAUNCHED_BY` and its proof file ride in M11's own PR after O7 M4, not in O7 or O16, so the producer and consumer of that contract land together under one owner; O7 M4 pins `launchedBy` written once, by `session.spawn`'s handler alone, since it is now a trust input. Two adversarial review rounds changed the agreed shape in three places: the bridge keys on a durable handed-off mark (`handed-off.json` in the endpoint's state directory; once a record has been seen for a session, a missing record fails closed), /clear's successor takes its predecessor's record by appSessionId (an opId can recur after `KEPT_S`), and the proof is an envelope of member, device, opId and action.

O15 records any stage not landed as carried. The architect's rulings on its route list. R5: only headless tabs are fenced. An app's agent tab keeps today's release and stays in the panel as the person's tab, because fencing it would hold every laptop session whose agent left a tab open past the owner's 10-minute release. The `machine.browserEngine` welcome capability went with it. R2: `machine.browserControl {appSessionId, epoch}` is graded `approve`, not `converse`, because a person's input into the agent's tab bypasses the tool-permission prompt the agent's own call may meet. O17 M9 builds on it after O7 M5, with no joint landing; O7's plan had held no such reservation. R4: O16 confirmed that the engine host keeps the link's last snapshot per process, so a session adopted after the first snapshot takes its verdict. Nothing enters O16's checkpoint, mawsd sends the snapshot first on every fresh link, a successor's included, and the hold lives in `holdsWork` alone. R6: cua's Linux contract is cua #242, and the broker's protocol behaviours are cua #251's contract tests, each naming O17. The idle exception (D20): the line falls between the budget release, which can take a session one quiet sample after its turn and is fenced, and the idle release. The idle release comes only after the machine role's whole idle time (10 minutes on a laptop, 24 hours on a server) with no turn, interest or other work. At its due time a row held only by the browser hold waits with no timer and emits `host.browserIdle`, `mawsd` drops the session's headless tabs, and the row is reaped once the hold clears. O16's verified-empty release is a step of the reap, not a third path. M7 now also edits O16's `idle-release.ts` and `process-table.ts`, which O16 sees at its landing. Accepted by design (D12): P5 §2's rule that restarting `mawsd` never ends a session holds for the session but not its headless tabs. The render host is `mawsd`'s child and exits with its link. The section changes: purpose's estimate, acceptance 2's counter (O5 M5's `PENDING_BUDGET` extended, not a second constant), acceptance 4 (headless only, the idle exception, late adoptions, an app's tabs by rule), acceptance 9's stale-epoch reading, acceptance 10's grant, the edges and OX9 (the engines' commands in a new uniform-`internal` namespace `browserEngine`, the feed, `approve`, `browser.remote.*`), and the fence seam. It found one: the badge and the renderer disagreed about a pending permission between a feed's opening and its snapshot. The fix is one predicate, `fed(machine)`, that every reader goes through, pinned by both of the reviewer's repros. Known limits, named in the PR: with no feed (an endpoint older than M6) a fallback item counts only while a tab holds its session; a reopened feed shows its last cache until its new snapshot. Also on the branch: an attention event omitted as too large counts as a lost feed and gives the machine back to its streams, an ended feed's empty cache no longer erases the streams' fallback items, and the M3 forward test waits for the link's next channel after a forced drop, which closes that test's tracker row. Five Unresolved Review Findings stay in the PR (a delegation that expires by time alone does not recompute the feed's cut, as ruled; one `session.get` per named session; a channel within the link's grace that may not retry a `not-offered` feed; one leftover socket-style snapshot; `follows` unused). The residue, `Machines.onRemote` giving a listener no initial call, is a tracker row. O7's measured group and live bench stay with M7; the combined `mawsd` idle reading for O7, O16 and O17 is taken by O16's measured series against the 2390 KiB margin, since O16 lands after this and carries it. O7 goes on with M5a, then M5b; M7 and M5c last. The wave 2 ledger gains M6's names and the shared `address-paths.json` row.
199. **2026-11-02, the architect, at O7 M5a's landing (artifacts across machines, the rule and the reads; PR #441, merge 3a183be2f).** In a remote session's pane the Artifacts tool lists and opens the session's artifacts from their machine, a rename or a pin lands there, Save As exports on the machine and saves the file here, and Open externally opens a byte-exact copy on this Mac; the Library lists another machine's library from a machine menu, and the Browser tool's notice reads the machine's `session.capabilities`. `WIRE_MINOR` is 20, master's 19 plus one, and `RL2_PROTOCOL` is 1.4 (`rl2.artifacts`, `rl2.bytes`); O16 and O17 M7 each re-take master's minor plus one at their landing, O16 now 21. Rulings recorded here. First, an artifact write, which names no session, runs through a new engine-host command, `host.artifactOp`, limited to the 13 `ARTIFACT_OP_COMMANDS` under `host.op`'s ledger, rather than through `host.op` with its `context` made optional, which OX1 counts as breaking: `host.op` keeps a session context in its types, an older host answers `unknown_command` without ambiguity, and no contract changes mid-wave; counting an input field made optional as additive goes to the PR's Residue as a proposal for P5's contracts. Second, the session-safe projection: every answer that can name a session (artifact reads, `library.list` rows, act results, query outcomes, the `artifacts` feed) is cut at send time to the sessions the member views, leaving no count, placeholder or timestamp behind, and an id is never a capability; owners get no exemption, so a listing by session takes the session's path at `view` for them too. Third, the known limits, accepted under the owner's A12 note: opaque session ids inside CRDT and attribution bytes cross as they are (a member can tell that one hidden session edited two artifacts, never which), the `WITHHELD_SESSION` sentinel says a session was withheld, a `linked` event's timing can show that a hidden session touched an artifact, and a text file that is not valid UTF-8 is refused, never copied lossily, until a raw-bytes read exists. What the gates caught: the first hosted `e2e:remote` showed every remote artifact write refused, because the command's handler lookup saw only the handlers `registerEngineIpc` registers while the host registers the artifact writes before it; the unit tests had used a fake host. It was fixed with the command's own lookup and a test over the real registration, and the milestone review, briefed to look for any other path only a fake host had run, found none. Reviewer-high on the milestone found three Important defects, all fixed: an owner's session-filtered listing disclosed that a private session exists; an artifact act ran after its standing was revoked between the decision and the gate's handoff; Open externally on a remote html page always failed (a page is its one stored file, so its copy is whole). The standing race reached O10's code too: the machine, pty-host, endpoint and sminos branches handed an act on without asking whether its decision still stood, and lane A's admission wait reopened the window. At the desk track's choice M5a carries one check after the last shared await and before the executor branches, and lane A's check immediately before `rl10.rpc`, a moved decision returned as `DECISION_MOVED`; either way the act is decided again once through the gate, then answered `unavailable`. Each has a red test, and the desk track reviewed both hunks in the PR. Gates: `--gates all` on the hosted lane, inside O16's mini hold, on the head 7d5430461, which merges master bd84478e7, so the merge tree is the gated tree 919cc621 (run 41387919062, 55 jobs green and 2 skipped by the plan, default peer at 3.19 against 3.20, no flaky line). Reviews: five task reviews, reviewer-high on the milestone, a narrow re-check of its fix wave, and the controller's own check of the lane A fix, which ended the loop. M5a's endpoint and main code is not yet read at idle: O16's landing tree carries it once O16 merges master, and O16's next measured window reads `e2e:daemon-idle` on that tree, so the 2390 KiB margin's account for O7, O16 and O17 stays on one series. Residue routed: the input-direction rule as a contracts proposal; a raw-bytes read behind `rl2.bytes`; the browser socket's path length (O5's `browserCapability`) and the `source.test.ts` watcher flake as tracker rows; the calls' per-command budgets close the machine link's request-budget row. O7 goes on with M5b, cut from master; M7 and M5c last. The wave 2 ledger gains M5a's names.
200. **2026-11-02, the architect, at O16's landing (the holder: a session host under every live session, adoption across engine-host upgrades, and the verified-empty release; PR #442, merge 83984adb0).** Every live lane B launch on the detached and daemon shapes now runs under a session host, a small static Go program (`native/session-host/`, prebuilds for darwin-arm64, linux-x64 and linux-arm64) that keeps the output ring, the input offsets, the writer fence and its own roster. When the engine host is upgraded, restarted (`mawsd restart --engine-host`) or replaced after a crash, its successor adopts the running sessions instead of ending them; a planned update waits until no tool call is in flight, then freezes, drains, detaches and exits. On a server role the idle release is the verified-empty rule armed through OX10's gate. `WIRE_MINOR` is 21, master's 20 plus one, with the capability `host.holder`; O17 M7 and every later bump re-take master's minor plus one at their landing. Gates: `--gates all` with no lane on e9c1d0a78, the branch's merge of master 95050efd1 (run 41404958399, 56 jobs green and 2 skipped by the plan at attempt 1, no flaky line, `e2e:cross-build` pairing 3.21 with 3.20), and class 1a on the head 1f799e818 (run 41410004127); the landed tree differs from the gated one in `docs/` and `src/engine-host/ops.test.ts` alone. Measured by CI-opt on the mini on the gated tree: `e2e:daemon-idle-session-host` read 4,145 KiB twice against 5,120 (runs 41416322445 and 41417649315); `e2e:daemon-idle` a median 95,171 KiB against 99,145 over three valid runs of four (41421375802 at 95,875, 41423601805 at 94,163, 41425789190 at 95,171; 41419104126 void on macOS background load), cpu 0.12 to 0.13 s; `e2e:local-latency-daemon` (41428060325) delta-to-frame p50 25.1 and p95 37.2 ms against 26.45 and 42.56, round trip 1.3 and 1.8 ms, so two Ps cost no latency. Wave 1's tree ba1a3c4d4 had read `e2e:daemon-idle` at a median 94,771 KiB, the baseline floor at 60,321 KiB (+1,552 on O4's), and `e2e:daemon-idle-session-host` red, 4,673 KiB against 4,096. The final median is 1,456 above O5 M9's 93,715 with O7 M5a's code merged in, so of Decision Log 193's 2,390 KiB render margin O17 keeps about 0.9 MiB if the render case grew alike; a recording `e2e:daemon-idle-render` on the same tree settles the figure before O17 M7 resumes. D67 re-derives that bound at 5,120 KiB. D3 set 4,096 from a prototype that ran none of the real host's goroutines; a vmmap breakdown puts the gap in the Go runtime's own memory (1,312 KiB of VM_ALLOCATE for the prototype, 2,280 for the host, 1,752 on two Ps); and the host sits outside `DAEMON_IDLE_BOUND` and the render margin, a small cost beside the engine it holds. Keeping 4,096 lost: a pass near 4,050 would fail on noise, and a miss would cost a second window. The host runs on two Ps (`runtime.GOMAXPROCS(2)`), the one setting that reliably made it smaller; GC targets, a memory limit and forced release all raised the idle figure. Reviews: the xhigh panel at landing (w9n8bvrs2) found 18 (8 P1, 10 P2), 17 fixed and one dismissed as shape B's conditional path; reviewer-high on wave 1 found four defects, its re-verification one P1 (a failed adoption's record dropped while its engine still ran, so a second launch could run beside it; a record now goes only when its host and engine are verified gone), and the re-check was clean; an earlier delta review's P1, a held stop-all forwarded to the successor before adoption reserved the table, was fixed by reverting that branch. Rulings recorded here: an update's handover waits for quiet, since freezing first would hold every session; a failed adoption's host is an unattached record, not a live one, so a launch stops it and then launches, stop-all stops it, forget stops and then forgets, and its liveness is read when asked (keeping the hold lost: it broke the idle park and blocked a reopen); a dataset refresh in flight at a handover fails retryably (ruling B); `host.op` opens its ledger with `orphanAtOpen`; at the second merge `settled()` waits for `host.artifactOp`'s runs too, since both claim through one ledger. A Go test race, the test client discarding an `out` that arrived before its `applied`, was fixed in the test (red 8 times in 4,000 local runs, then none). Unproven at landing: a handover between two builds that both list `host.holder`, since ci-gate pairs a cross-build only with master; the next landing that bumps `WIRE_MINOR` pairs 3.22 with 3.21 and proves it, and its record says so. Residue routed as tracker rows: `host.op`'s late outcome, D62's drain across a handover with its interim copy, shape B's three rows in O3-D's front (row 2 by a follow-up PR), the stop-all a draining host's loss drops, the measured case's selector and wording, the session host's heap after traffic, re-adoption on demand, the D58a scene at unit level, and the holder-capable crossing; O4's park-need row stays open for the next editor of the park rules; the row on a lingering idle exit (#413) closes, fixed by `EXIT_WAIT_MS`. CI-opt reviewed the `gate.yml` hunks (the prebuild check, `MEASURED_E2E`'s session host group, and the Go tests in `static` and `unit:darwin`). The wave 2 ledger gains O16's names from M3b onward.
201. **2026-11-02, the architect, two fixes after O16's landing (PR #443, merge 338ddbd23; PR #444, merge 25cd9cd60), and O16's render reading.** #443, in P5 O5's code: `mawsd`'s `forward` restarts the park clock before it lowers the in-flight count, so a request that outlives `idleExitMs` (a `connectors.call` of up to 180 s) no longer lets `DaemonClocks.evaluate()` arm a park on the old `parkSince` the moment it ends; O7 M5b found the order while fixing the same one for its document hold. #444, shape B's row 2 from Decision Log 200 in O3-D's front: a stream whose catch-up failed restarts it at a live status of its own launch, as a relink does, at most `CATCHUP_RESTARTS` (3) times in a row, the count reset once the stream is live; past the cap it waits for a relink or a new launch as before. A new launch whose opening keeps failing still retries at every status of that launch, uncapped, which was outside the row. Gates: class 1a and `--gates all` on each head, each containing master 6354cffd1. #443: 41430316584, and 41431471621 red only on `e2e:discover`'s `:584` (the tracker's catalog family), green on rerun 41444935390, with one flaky pass in `maws-tools.spec.ts:218` on an EC2 overflow Mac, recorded as the hosted row's signature (23). #444: 41430704577, and 41431857531 with 55 jobs green and no flaky line. 202. **2026-11-02, the architect, at O7 M5b's landing (pages and documents on the read open; PR #445, merge 62a1dec05).** In a remote session's pane an html page, a PDF, an image or a media artifact now loads from its machine into the pane's frame, ranges included, and a document opens in the editor with every edit landing in the machine's own store; a member whose grade is `view` gets the read-only editor, and Download saves any machine's asset here. `RL2_PROTOCOL` is 1.5 (`rl2.read-open`: a `streamOpen` with no `op` for the kinds `http` and `document`, authorized at its row's grade and re-decided whenever what the decision read moves); `WIRE_MINOR` stays 21, so no cross-build pair ran, and the protocol tests and the pinned handshake vectors carry RL2's compatibility. Gates: the PR's landing gate `--gates all` on the hosted lane on its merge of master 6354cffd1 (run 41429534777, 54 jobs green and 2 skipped by the plan, no flaky line), then, since #443 had moved `src/mawsd/engine-requests.ts` under M5b's own edit there, `--gates all` with no lane on the merge with master itself (run 41448288798). Reviews: five task reviews, reviewer-high on the milestone with six Important findings, and a narrow pass on that fix wave with three more, every one fixed with a test red before it; the loop stopped there. Rulings recorded here. The scheme's remote responder never writes `ArtifactMachines`, and the local store is read before referrer routing, reversing the task's first choice: a referrer-routed answer let a remote machine claim a local artifact's id and redirect later local loads. An http read of a dataset snapshot holds it through `artifacts.holdAssets` from before the fetch to the stream's end, so a retention sweep in that window still serves the bytes. An agent's provisional document frame names its session as `withheld-session` for every member, under Decision Log 199's projection. A paused media stream idle 2 s or more gives way to a queued request with a normal end and resumes through Range; a download is never cut, and a short body fails it. Memory: a link retains at most 64 MiB of document payload, counting each queued frame whole until its last byte drains, and one oversized frame per stream (to the host's 64 MiB) passes its own stream's checks, as the engine host's queue does; main's bridge bounds its outbound queue at 8 MiB behind the frame it is draining. M5a's residue is settled. The input-direction rule is accepted into OX1: making an input field optional is additive when a caller omits it only toward a peer whose minor made it optional. The raw-bytes read behind `rl2.bytes` is board #446, deferred. Routed as tracker rows: the link's worst case of one oversized frame per otherwise-empty stream (about 2 GiB at 32 document streams), the window's MessagePort queue, and a certificate change that re-checks streams but not the feeds' subscriptions. A download whose answer has no `Content-Length` cannot prove it is whole, a known limit in the PR. O7 goes on with M5c, then M7, whose acceptance covers M5c; O17's remote stage now has its read open. The wave 2 ledger gains M5b's names.
203. **2026-11-02, the architect, at O12 M4's re-plan (PR #447, merge ddce29a4f), with three O16 rulings it and O17 M7 raised.** O12 M4, the reaping gate (sminos's observer on O16's `gate.sock`), is re-planned against O16's Interfaces §E as built and split in two: M4a the observer (a hello naming `childPid`, a 5 s heartbeat and reconnect, the fence in `turn.step`, `stop` and `proceed`, `launch_env` dropping `MAWS_HOLDER_SOCKET`), then M4b the receipts. Neither merges before the O16 fix wave below lands and a joint race proof has run against its head. Rulings as O16's architect. §E's settlement sentence is amended: every log-backed inbox delivery to a process whose mode record is `gate` is prepared as a receipt before handoff and settled at its first admitted model request, stays owed on `stop` with at-least-once recovery correlated by a text marker, and a `legacy` process keeps today's handoff settlement; settling only while a fence is armed loses a delivery handed off before the arm and dequeued after it. The observer answers every arm, renewals included, with one `armed` in arrival order, a turn that meets an arm included. An O16 fix wave (branch `o16-gate-reap`) takes three defects of the landed gate and reap: observer health judged silence on the wall clock and never healed on the same connection, so a sleeping Mac or a stalled loop would leave observed rows unreleasable until a reconnect (now a monotonic silence clock and `hb` healing, the silent span still unhealthy and nothing re-armed); the idle reap sent `end_session` and its SIGTERM with no lease check (now no signal without a confirmed live lease, live only while both the wall and the monotonic clock say so); renewals while signalled went unacknowledged (now each awaits its `armed`, paired first-in first-out on its connection). A budget reap's later rungs stay a forced release by design. Separately, O17 M7 found that O16's descendant veto leaves real MCP servers unregistered (cua's bare `node` command after any adoption, and ptc's launcher, which execs into a venv's python, everywhere), and the sminos bridge too, since sminos 1.31.0 sends no hello yet, so on the owner's setup every session would hold itself unreleasable. A probe on a devcloud VM (engines 2.1.283 and 2.1.316) showed `mcp_status` does carry each plugin server's config, so the owner fixture read earlier was curated, and that it keeps a killed server `connected` without a respawn. The fix (branch `o16-veto-mcp`) binds servers by process identity within 60 s of `claude`'s start and persists the bindings for adopters; the bridge's registration by its own argv and the row's session id is permanent for every process whose bridge never says hello. Master goes to the owner's machines only after that fix lands. Nothing on master changes with this record.

205. **2026-11-02, the architect, at the landing of O16's two post-landing fixes (PR #448, the descendant veto, and PR #449, the gate and reap wave, which carries #448; merge 627190848).** #448 (O16's D69): the descendant veto now recognises an MCP server that `mcp_status` names with no `config`, and a launcher that execs into another program (the owner's cua `node` shim, ptc's launcher into its venv's python), so a real plugin session is released again; before it, every such session was held from idle and budget release (Decision Log 203). A process is bound by pid, start time and its parent `claude`, its subtree covered: by argv where `mcp_status` carries the server's command; by count otherwise, after a fresh `mcp_status` with none pending, when the unbound direct children that are neither wrappers nor zombies, with the live bound ones, equal the connected stdio count, each born within 60 s of `claude`'s start and in a second before the launch's first turn; and the sminos bridge permanently by its argv (`bridge --session` naming the row's session, under a plugin root from the init list). Bindings persist per launch (`infrastructure {launch, processes}`) and adopters restore them; `caffeinate` stays counted as work. A VM probe recorded the owner's server shapes (fixtures `p5-o16-veto-mcp*.jsonl`): `mcp_status` carries each server's config, and keeps a killed stdio server `connected` with no respawn. #449 (O16's D70): the gate judges silence on a monotonic clock; an `hb` heals an unhealthy row on the same connection, and a reconnect still needs a fresh `hello`; each `armed` pairs with the oldest unanswered arm of its fence on its own connection and counts only while that connection is the observer's; no `end_session` and no SIGTERM goes out without a confirmed live lease (the latest arm acknowledged, both the wall and the monotonic elapsed under the lease less its margin). An unconfirmed lease at the SIGTERM re-arms and waits for `armed`, and withholds if none comes (journal phase `term_withheld`, the row idle); the host's own turn state is re-read right before the SIGTERM through `turnUnderWay`, the idle step's predicate. A budget reap's later rungs stay ungated by design. No wire field or frame changes. Gates: #448's head by `--gates all` (41472964171, one flaky pass explained), #449's head before it carried #448 by `--gates all` (41477297736, no flaky line), the combined head by class 1a (41498674129), and the merge with master 5482e42d0, which had moved in `src/main` under both, by `--gates all` on the hosted lane (run 41535634826). The desk track's joint race proof passed 7/7 on the combined head: MAWS's real gate and reap, assembled as `attachReap` does, driving a real engine session over a real socket against sminos's real bridge and mod in one `claude -p` on the stub provider, at production timings except the SIGTERM case. It also settled whether the turn re-read sees an engine-initiated turn: task, peer and cron turns show `session_state_changed: running` before `status: requesting` and before any output. Surprises: under MAWS's launch the engine runs `Agent` in the background even with `run_in_background: false`, and stamps a stdin prompt's origin `composer`, not `sdk` (O12 M4a maps it to `query`). Routed as tracker rows: the respawn window after `mcp_reconnect`, `mcp_toggle` or `reload_plugins`; an async SessionStart hook bound by count in a dead server's place; the veto's conservative misses; `mcp_status`'s `connected` for a dead server, for its other readers; the veto reading the status before its fresh tree read; a row not verified-empty for about 4 s after each turn; the session host's Go test racing the reap record; the secret scan's planted AWS key once not reported (the merge gate's only red, green on the job's rerun); a hosted signature (25). With the veto fixed on master, Decision Log 203's hold is lifted: master goes to the owner's machines, first as the MacBook's swap before O7's live sitting at 2026-11-03 08:00Z (the owner's answers: that time, and 'Swap before'). The landing order changed for it: O17 lands after the sitting, with one confirmation render and idle run on its merge tree, since its series ran on a tree without these engine host changes. 206. **2026-11-03, the architect, the index source's failed first scan (PR #452, merge 71969abd8).** When the MacBook's app went to master 75944744d for the owner's sitting, the index source's first `fs.scan` of a 7,750-session store answered in about 13.6 s, past the supervisor's 10 s default. The mini's smaller store scanned in time. #452: `fs.scan` takes a 120 s budget on both supervisors the index source scans through (mawsd's own and main's embedded spec), about nine times the slowest scan seen; a sync whose scan answered null is asked again after 2, 10 and 30 s within its host generation, never past an answered scan or the source's stop, and then logs that discovery waits for the next host start; failed text reads are retried only at a host start's sync. `fs.bind` keeps the default, since it does not grow with the store. 207. **2026-11-03, the architect, at O7 M5c's landing (O4's surfaces on another machine; PR #454, merge a4655841a).** A dashboard on a remote session refreshes its connector widgets here under the grade the owner answers, with its snapshots held on the machine while shown; Send to exports and delivers on the machine; Connect and Sign in complete through this Mac's loopback, the consent relayed over RL2 to the device that asked; and another machine's Connectors sheet asks for Touch ID only at the owner's Show or Refresh. `RL2_PROTOCOL` is 1.7 (`rl2.client-port` and `rl2.asset-holds`, after M7's `rl2.pong-clock`) and `WIRE_MINOR` 22, whose entries are real: `delivery.send` retagged machine, the `callback` field on `connectors.connect`, `reconnect` and `signIn`, `connectors.callback`, `machine.delivery` and `connectors.serversChanged`'s `ended`; this corrects the architect's earlier note that M5c leaves the wire at 21. O17's #450 also claimed 22, so it takes 23 at its landing. Rulings recorded here. The remote asset holds live on the endpoint per channel under the artifact rule (`artifacts.holdAssets` at `view`, `releaseAssets` at `list` and only for the asking channel's own hold, 256 a channel refused `busy`), and main holds a remote dashboard's snapshot on the router's target rather than through `ArtifactMachines`, which can lack the id at a dashboard's first hold. An acquisition on the endpoint is bounded at 8 s from its arrival, 2 s under the device's 10 s call deadline, judged on the clock at the answer as well as by a timer, and a late answer is let go there. A `delivery.send` is pending on its channel from receipt, so a cancel sent while it is decided waits on it. Once shown, another machine's Connectors rows are marked stale at every change and read again only through Refresh, each read its own Touch ID; a step-up window per open sheet, one Touch ID for background re-reads, would be a new time-boxed admin grant and is board ticket #455 for the owner's UX wave. `dashboard-live-data.spec.ts:526`'s flaky line was a product race older than O7: the owner's Start answers `ready` before the engine's MCP servers connect, so a refresh found no route and waited out its 30 s interval; the broker now wakes a routeless watch at a grant of its tool (two tests red first). Gates: class 1a on each head and on the master merge (41541843450, 41552593862, 41558751875, 41559318685), the six hosted groups twice with no flaky line (41543075306, 41553653036), and the landing gate `--gates all` on its head (41560844213), whose `e2e:cross-build` paired 3.22 with 3.21; its first attempt's two refusals at runner setup came from a TCC dialog on the mini raised by an unrelated Claude session reaching into Documents at 01:41:03Z, which CI-opt closed at 02:43Z. Reviews: reviewer-high on the milestone with three Important findings (the early cancel, a late acquisition's leak, the unasked Touch ID prompts), all fixed with tests red first, then a narrow pass whose one Important (the bound read only from its timer) was fixed the same way; the loop stopped there. Two Minors and six residue items are tracker rows here, and the `notOffered` pause's missing detail line is board ticket #456. O7 goes on with M7's measured group, red on its direct resume (a fix in review), and the sitting's live numbers. The wave 2 ledger gains M5c's names.
208. **2026-11-03, the architect, O7 M7's direct resume (PR #457, merge da7b8f1f6).** M7's measured group `e2e:remote-latency` on master 75944744d, run by CI-opt on the mini, missed one bound twice: the direct carrier's resume after a forced drop took 7,441.9 ms (41557747690, void on load) and 7,647.8 ms (41558674583, valid) against about 175 ms, while the relay resumed in about 70 ms and every other figure held. The cause was in main's remote link: `viaDirect` took a token from the direct carrier's bucket (burst 3, five a minute) before every dial, so the reconnect attempts of an outage longer than about 3 s, each refused or timed out, emptied it, while the listener charges only for an upgrade it accepts; when the network came back the probe's attempt skipped direct and the link waited out its backoff (1, 2 and 4 s) until a token refilled. Real cost: with the hub unreachable (a LAN without internet, a hub outage) a returning network stalled 0 to 12 s; with the hub reachable the session fell to the relay for up to 60 s. #457: `TokenBucket.holds()` checks for a whole token without taking it; a direct dial is gated on it, and the token is taken only once the socket opens, on both the attempt's dial and the catch-up re-dial, so the client's count matches the listener's and the limit still holds (three opened handshakes that fail spend the burst). Tests red first: resumes after 3.5, 6.9 and 15 s outages come up direct in the probe's own attempt, and a refused catch-up spends nothing. Gates: `--gates all` on its head before the master merge (41561328996: attempt 1 red only on `dashboard.spec.ts:548`, the hosted row's signature (22), in packaged 1/2, and two jobs refused by the mini's TCC dialog, all green on rerun), then on the merge with M5c's master static, unit, probes, `e2e:remote` and `e2e:machines` (41571726165), no flaky line in any. Reviewer-medium found nothing. CI-opt reruns the measured group on master after semis' run on 11-03, with O17's confirmation set; M7's measured acceptance waits for it. The fix is not on the sitting's machines; it reaches the MacBook and the VM with M5c for the S8 follow-up.
209. **2026-11-03, the architect, at the landing of O17's local stage (the agent's browser with no app; PR #450, merge 1aab503c3).** A session with no app attached now drives a real browser: `mawsd` starts the render host as its tab engine, tabs run offscreen at 1280x800 under Chromium's sandbox, a viewer watches and takes over through the producer's screencast, the engine host's fence keeps a session that holds a headless tab out of every release, and D20's idle exception closes an idle session's tabs. The merge kept both sides in seven files: the engine host's spawn carries O17's spawner variable beside #452's `requestBudgetsMs`, the endpoint services carry O17's `browser` beside M5c's `clientPort`, and O17's test takes master's `stdioServers` (O16's D69). Rulings recorded here. `mawsd`'s hello client capability gates the engine host's `unknown` default, since a rollback from master to a pre-O17 app under the replace build policy would otherwise never release or replace a session. A session that an engine host started by `mawsd` adopts is tagged `lost` rather than `adopted`, through R4's one scope exception: `MAWS_ENGINE_HOST_SPAWNER`, set by `mawsd`'s spawn and read and deleted at the host's start. Acceptance 14 at O17, the measured series on 97779bfe3 (CI-opt, on the mini): `e2e:daemon-idle` median 94,547 KiB (3 valid of 4); `e2e:daemon-idle-render` plain median 97,619 and browser warm-to-idle median 97,875, all valid and within; `e2e:local-latency-daemon` delta-to-frame p50 25.6 ms and p95 37.1. The four-kept case read 101,299 and 100,450, valid and over 99,145, from the pre-O17 cost of three more live sessions (about 0.95 MiB of Private memory each, measured with O17's pieces off). This amends how Decision Log 128 applies to a scene with more than one session, not its value: the case is judged on the browser case's median plus three times O17's own per-session share, which the Linux A-B (4 x 4 legs, all valid) put below its resolution (summed Private -697 KiB per session, standard error about 550), so the criterion holds on every metric's median (95,783, 97,409 and 98,395). The confirmation set on the merge tree, which carries #448's and #449's engine-host changes: render 41580742976 and daemon-idle 41584903115, each valid on its first run: plain 98,739 KiB (`mawsd` 37,665, the engine host 61,073; 406 under the bound), browser warm-to-idle 98,370 (36,913 and 61,505; 775 under), four-kept 100,835 (37,569 and 63,025), between the series' two valid kept readings, and daemon-idle 93,475 (33,617 and 59,857; 0.12 s of CPU; 5,670 under). Plain and kept read inside the series' range, daemon-idle 960 KiB below its lowest and the browser case 64 KiB above its highest, well inside the 1 to 2 MiB by which launches of one scene differ, so the merge moved nothing and the series carries; on the browser case's four readings (median 98,090) the kept criterion holds on every metric (95,999, 97,625 and 98,611 on Private, Pss and Rss). At one session the render scene's margin is close to spent: the plain case's second run read 22 KiB under the bound, its +1,504 over the first almost all the engine host's drift between jobs. The +1.5 MiB `mawsd` step between O5's landings (Decision Log 193) stays unattributed: it is in neither the idle heap nor the bare footprint. Routed as tracker rows: a live session's idle cost with no bound for N sessions, a heap-level reading for costs below a whole process's resolution, the engine host's drift between jobs, Linux pass 3 run by hand, the popup's sizing through a deprecated CDP method, the auto-attach provenance and the keys on a non-US layout; `document-perf.test.ts`'s CPU ratio as a new unit flake. 210. **2026-11-03, the architect, at the landing of O17's remote stage (the agent's browser seen and driven from another machine; PR #459, merge 7764f9b36), so O17 has landed whole.** A person on another machine opens a session whose machine runs no MAWS app and sees the agent's tab live in an "Agent on <machine>" group beside their own tabs; after Take control their clicks, scrolls, keys, IME text and Enter reach the page as a person's input, and the tab keeps running on the session's machine against its `localhost`. Where that machine's app is attached, the app is the tab engine of its agent tabs and serves them the same way. M9a carries it on RL2 1.8: the capability `rl2.browser-screencast`, the read kind `browser.screencast` on O7 M5b's read open (Decision Log 195), opened at `view`, or at `approve` by effect for control, a controlling open naming a done `machine.browserControl` act that the endpoint checks against its own ledger and binds to the member, device, session and epoch, a frame envelope with a backlog of one frame plus 4 KiB, and the session feed `browser.inventory`. M9b adds the producers and the app's bridge `browser.remote.*`; M10 the renderer's view. `WIRE_MINOR` is 24: the manifest gains exactly `daemon.e2eBrowserView` and `prepare`'s `socketPath`, regenerated from master's 3.23. `daemon.e2eBrowserView` follows the `daemon.e2eSeam` and `daemon.e2eRender` precedent: a main-only machine command that `mawsd` refuses unless it runs with `MAWS_E2E=1`, with a unit test of the refusal. Two defects on master since #450 were fixed in this stage. `mawsd`'s session sockets overflowed sun_path on a Mac for any user name of five or more characters, so the headless browser was dead for those users while reading available until first use; no deployed build carries it, since the owner's builds predate #450. `mawsd` now names its sockets by a 16-hex digest of the session id, the render host's directory is `rcua`, and `mawsd` passes the render host its path in `prepare`. The shared producer's Enter did nothing on a form or a textarea, in the local stage's takeover too; it now sends Chromium's native pair (a raw key-down and a char). No flaky line. The PR's head `c6b072d7b` adds only docs; master then moved only by #461's test, so the merge ran `e2e:default` and `packaged` (run 41695883847). Reviews: every task review clean (M9a on sol, the rest on opus while sol and astra were at their usage limit); the whole-branch xhigh panel `wf_67e76ba4-e20` ran on fable lanes for the same reason, and returned two P3s and nothing above (an app engine with no inventory push on a selection-only change; the renderer's draw acknowledging a replaced stream's frame, one family with M9b's stale acknowledgement), both debt, so the loop stopped there. At one session the margin is now about 400 to 1,200 KiB in plain and browser, one reading over in each: the engine host carries the drift (60,625 to 62,449 KiB across these runs), `mawsd` stays flat against the local stage's confirmation, and the stage changes no engine-host code, only shared IPC schemas and the socket protocol it bundles. Five of M10's scenes are held below e2e: the view-only member, the non-owner's step-up and the revocation (the fake sminos has one member), the slow carrier (no carrier throttling in the scene) and the peer without `rl2.browser-screencast` (no cross-build pairing of the device).
