211. **2026-11-04, the architect, at the landing of O7 M7's live record (PR #463, merge 086bc4f38): the live sitting ran from the MacBook against the mini and a devcloud VM, two of O7's own acceptance lines failed, and O7 gains M8 to M10.** The sitting ran from 04:01Z to about 06:30Z, 2 h 46 min after its slot: the MacBook had hibernated on an empty battery, and both target machines lacked what a remote spawn needs (the MAWS project on the mini and on the VM, the mini's trust for its folder, a route the VM could launch on), each set up during the sitting with the owner. Passed, on both machines unless named: streaming with token deltas and Working clearing (the VM), send, steer, interrupt, approve and deny, an image, a file edited from either side, the remote terminal (Linux on the VM), a dev server's link opened in the in-app browser, the attention badge with Needs you and its count falling, and the carrier hover. Failed: (1) a session the MacBook spawned on the mini never appears under its project in the MacBook's sidebar, and nothing reopens it once its tab closes (acceptance 5); (2) no question can be read or answered from another machine, since `maws.asks.list` and `maws.ask.answer` have no RL2 offer rows and launch disallows the engine's own AskUserQuestion (acceptance 4); (3) the side panels are refused remotely (`fs.subagents`, `fs.workflows` and `fs.tasks` not offered, `session.reloadPlugins` refused `act-required`), and `files.read` answers `internal` for the VM's scheduled jobs; (4) after a tool-using turn the VM's own status kept `activity: requesting`, so the app showed Working; (5) a remote `/btw` never answers; (6) an attachment reaches the remote machine but not as an `@` reference; (7) the MCP panel's Reconnect on a needs-auth remote server restarts it and cannot sign in, and points nowhere else, so M5c's remote sign-in in Settings › Connectors was not reached; (8) an untracked file shows no diff. Every A11 session's owner attestation also read `not-owner` on the receiving machine, both ways, so owners went down unverified (the desk track's O12). The owner skipped the Wi-Fi drop and the lid ("we should skip that because we need continuous wifi for other sessions to run. same for lid"), so acceptance 7's app half rests on M7's measured forced-drop case. The live bench was voided: the mini attempt's clock spread failed and the MacBook's Rule B load read 97.7 from another project's test runs, so vm-bench was dropped; M7's measured acceptance is CI's rl-4 (run 41573992251), every bound held. The owner's ruling on inference, given when a VM spawn was refused because MAWS's default `gateway` route found no ClaudeUsage relay on that machine, verbatim: "Wait, so we don't use the existing machine's inference? We shouldn't be using ClaudeUsage, We should be just using what machine uses as its default inference." It amends D-19's default for a session MAWS starts on another machine: that machine's own Claude Code setup decides (its login, settings and managed settings), and MAWS injects no gateway and reports no mismatch for it; whether the app's own machine follows the same rule is put to the owner. O7's document now plans M8 (the sitting's failures, each proved red first, with a contract test that fails on any session-surface command neither offered over RL2 nor greyed for a remote session with its reason), M9 (a `machine` route and remote setup from the spawning app) and M10 (the bench's four fixes and a short live re-check); O7 closes at M10. For the runbook: `admin pair` and `admin enroll` hold the org lock until their device answers, so a second code waits for the first; a declined enroll leaves a hub row that needs `--replace`; a scratch daemon exits after 10 idle minutes. The PR adds only O7's document and its report; static (run 41786841360).
212. **2026-11-04, the architect, at the landing of the desk track's PR #464 (merge 6b53fca62), O10's verifier: the owners of a session the app spawns on another machine now verify on every machine that receives its events.** At O7 M7's live sitting every A11 session's owners went down unverified (`not-owner`) on the receiving machine, both ways. The event's attest was complete; its one proof was the owner's step-up-signed `session.spawn`. But `apps/org-protocol/src/attest.ts`'s `SPAWN_ACTIONS` listed only `sminos.spawn` and `session.launch` (O10 M1), so `verifyOwnersProof` read the proof as an owner-add and refused it. The list gains `session.spawn`; the action keeps its name, since it is the endpoint's offer row and is in every machine's ledger. Five `session.spawn` vectors: the owner proven (`not-owner` before the fix), another member's spawn, another machine's, a revoked device's, and a signer the org never listed. A receiver verifies with its own build, so owners verify where this build runs, and an older receiver keeps writing them down unverified, as before. The ruling that a spawn proof bind to the session it attests could not be met here: neither spawn act signs an id of the session it creates (DX6's strict spawn fields; the machine picks the ids after the act), so a machine can reuse one owner's spawn proof for another session it lists. It is board ticket #465 (design first: a signed, app-chosen session id the receiver checks, against a receiver-side one-proof-one-session rule). Gates on the head `37b3a2788`: static, probes, `e2e:desk-web` and unit (runs 41788418951 and 41789480412, 0 flaky). The first run's one red was `test/support/remote-bench.test.ts`, which builds its bench with the real clock and refuses inside the mini's 06:45 to 07:00Z quiet window, so it fails any gate in that window; O7's M8 pins its clock. Review: reviewer-medium with a security lens, correct, no material findings.
213. **2026-11-04, the architect, at the landing of O7's re-plan (PR #467, merge e1f778be5): M8 to M10 are planned, and the owner answered both of M9's forks.** Asked whether MAWS should follow each machine's own Claude Code setup on every machine, the MacBook included, the owner answered "Every machine (Recommended)"; asked what the spawning Mac should do when the target machine lacks the project or its folder's trust, "Set it up from here (Recommended)". The charter's D-19 records the first as its amended default. M8 fixes the sitting's failures, each red first. The code corrected the architect's brief in four places: `session.reloadPlugins` has no offer row, and the device refuses it before any round trip; a local PDF is sent as a `document` block, so the remote send turns an uploaded PDF into one (a typed `uploads` reference behind `rl2.upload-blocks`, expanded by the files service's confined resolver); `activity: requesting` after a turn is every turn's state, local too, so the stuck Working is most likely a `turn.ended` the device dropped as unreadable across a version skew, and an unreadable event now marks a gap and re-reads the status; and lane B's stream carries no `session.operation`, so `/btw` and every remote operation could never complete, which an `operation` part and a per-opId `session.operations` read fix. Every session-addressed command is offered at its registry grade or declared in a remote-unavailable registry with its reason and greyed, held by a contract test; `fs.tasks` is offered under a new `task-list` rule, so M8 takes `WIRE_MINOR` at its landing. The architect's corrections: the Tasks panel is offered rather than greyed (§5), the remote folder picker never reads inside a Mac's privacy-protected folders unless an owner opens one, and Show's Touch ID is stated. M9's `machine` route injects nothing and records what the engine reports. An enum member or a settings `version` literal would be a wire major that cross-build refuses inside a major, so the route crosses as additive `route` and `observed` properties beside the legacy values, the choice lives in its own `machine-route.json` (an older build strict-parses `machine-settings.json`), and an older engine host is given the legacy route only where it is equivalent, else the start refuses `route-unsupported`, so inference never changes silently. The relay preflight is advisory, with Start anyway. Remote setup adds owner-only reads (`machine.folders.list`, `session.trust.check`) and an owner-only `session.projects.addFolder` act apart from the spawn guard; `trust: 'accepted'` is honoured only for a current owner and goes through the attested bootstrap launch, so MAWS still never writes trust. M10 fixes the bench (its key read expected a nested shape `mawsd keys --json` never prints, so the sitting's first attempt could never pass; a `mawsd endpoint sync` verb; one watching status link; the mini's scratch supervisor reaped; a load precheck refusing at 5 or more) and runs a short live re-check on the mini and a fresh VM, after the desk track's fix for managed-scope plugins on Linux hosts. The adversarial review ran two rounds (six findings, two blocking, then four on the narrow re-check), all folded in; the loop stopped there. Static (run 41793109590).
214. **2026-11-04, the architect, at the landing of the desk track's PR #466 (merge e007a4ab3), O7-adjacent: a lane B session on a Linux host keeps the owner's message tool after the engine's first plugin update.** On devcloud hosts the engine's startup plugin sync rewrites `installed_plugins.json` so that the plugins the host's `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` requires carry scope `managed`. The owner channel's resolver (`src/services/owner/resolve-style.ts`) took only the user, project and local scopes, and read only macOS's managed path, so it found no to-human install and launched sessions without the message tool (seen on dc-acceptance-bd31 from 06:01Z, after one session at 05:26Z still had it). The resolver now reads the machine's setup as the engine does, under the owner's rule that MAWS follows what the machine uses (Decision Log 211): an install of scope `managed` counts as `user` does (the engine's own test), managed settings come from the per-OS directory plus its `managed-settings.d` drop-ins in name order (the engine host's walk in `session-start-hooks.ts`, now shared), and managed settings take precedence for `outputStyle` and `enabledPlugins`. Red first: 5 of 28 resolver cases failed on the old code, the devcloud case exactly as on the host; 28 of 28 after. Gates on the head `c1df712e2`: class 1a (run 41792090294, the resolver's unit on the Linux pool) and `e2e:to-human` (run 41793534699), 0 flaky. The engine-host file changes only by an export, and no Mac in the org has a managed directory, so the architect judged the head's gates sufficient for this tree, with master having moved since by #464's verifier and docs only. Review: reviewer-medium, correct. Not modelled, a tracker row: the engine's exemption for an org's own mod under `allowManagedHooksOnly` (the resolver turns the tool off, the safe side), and server-managed settings, the macOS MDM profile and WSL's inherited directory. P2-C's spec states the scope rule as amended.
215. **2026-11-04, the architect, at the landing of the desk track's O12 M4 record (PR #468, merge 8f2d8e67a): the reaping gate's sminos side has landed, as M4a (the observer, doperpowers #456, merge `5f5b5468`, sminos 1.32.0) and M4b (the receipts, doperpowers #457, merge `76132b84`, sminos 1.33.0), so O12 is done again.** A marked lane B process with sminos loaded now sits inside O16's release fence, and a peer delivery to a `gate` process settles only at its first admitted model request, so a turn the gate stops leaves its delivery owed. A process already running when its plugin updates stays `legacy` for settlement until it relaunches, observed if it had M4a's observer. M4a converged under reviewer-high in three rounds and M4b in four; on claude 2.1.316 with the stub provider and real sminos delivery, M4a's live observer check passed 21 of 21 and M4b's live receipts check 17 of 17. The architect reviewed C5, the activation grace, at this landing and accepts it. It revises M4b's rule that a deliverer finding no mode record records `legacy` and hands off: for a process under 30 s old of a session a `gate` process once served, the deliverer answers `wait`, the delivery stays owed, no delivery attempt is spent and the deliverer goes on to other work rather than blocking (`sminos_holder_deliveries.py`), and after the grace the record alone decides. It serves the section's own rule that a resume under a new process is a new choice, since a deliverer meets a resumed process before its bridge can activate, and `ACTIVATION_GRACE_S=0` restores the literal rule. C7: only a classic SessionStart handled at a process age under 60 s counts as a fresh process that may enable receipts, and a fresh process whose mode record cannot be written never says hello (fail-closed). C6, that the receipt's marker line survives into the observed row, was shown live. M4's residue is on the doperpowers board (#168 to #170). Also recorded: the O7 M7 sitting's VM, dc-acceptance-bd31, was deleted at 12:01Z once the owner's optional Notion sign-in window (until 04:00 PST) had passed without a try, so M5c's remote sign-in stays not reached until M10's re-check on a fresh VM; the production hub still lists its machine, which only the owner's signing verb drops. The PR changes only O12's document; static (run 41826247269).
216. **2026-11-04, the architect, at the landing of O7 M8 (PR #470, merge 689701fec): the live sitting's failures are fixed, each proved red first, and every session command now has exactly one answer for a remote session.** Corrections to Decision Log 211 and PR #463: the spawned session missing from the MacBook's sidebar was not an id mismatch but the group's cap. A group shows 5 rows (8 after Show more), and a listed remote row ranked only by when it was issued, behind busy local records, so it fell under the cap; the app id and the engine id were joined correctly. The fix is parity: Resume… lists listed rows (another member's only under People), and a listed row ranks by its own state and the attention feed. With it, a rule the architect made in the owner's place and told them they can overrule: a session spawned from this app, on this Mac or another machine, leads its group, under pinned rows and shown past the cap, until its first turn ends, then ranks by its state. The stale Working's cause stays unsettled (the older-shape theory was ruled out over 15,144 events, and the app keeps no main log on disk, since main logs to stdout, which a Dock launch loses); the fix is the gap rule: an unreadable event is a visible gap and a generation-guarded status and record re-read that closes only the turn never seen to end. `/btw` and every remote operation could never complete because lane B's stream carried no `session.operation`: the stream now carries operation parts, outcomes are kept per opId and asked by `session.operations` at every reopen, and a stream ended `behind` reopens from its cursor, backing off when it makes no progress and showing a gap after three such tries. Engine 2.1.312 expands an `@` reference to a PDF in stream-json to a size note only, so `session.send` gains typed `uploads` behind `rl2.upload-blocks`, expanded at the endpoint by a confined resolver into document blocks (a refused open fails closed, an upload expires after a day, a message carries at most 100); a send refused while it carries uploads shows one plain line, and a PDF sent during a running turn is queued ("Sends after this turn"). `files.read`'s `internal` for the VM's scheduled jobs was the endpoint dropping the service's message. The MCP widget's Sign in runs `connectors.signInServer`, an additive command, since making `connectors.signIn`'s `path` optional would break an older receiver; Review shows untracked files. The M8 case's first run also found a refused send leaving the keyboard on the page body, local sends included (the editor goes read-only while a send is in flight, and only a send that went through took the focus back); a refused send now returns the focus to the box with its text and caret. The offer audit: the MAWS ask (`maws.asks.list`, and `maws.ask.answer` graded `converse`), `fs.subagents`, `fs.workflows`, `fs.tasks` (under a `task-list` rule, bound to the engine session it was placed on), `session.reloadPlugins`, `side.question.clear`, `session.operations` and `fs.unfollow` are offered; 54 commands, `mods.list` among them, are declared remote-unavailable and drawn greyed with their reason; 25 are answered in main; and `offer-coverage.test.ts` fails on any session command in none or more than one of the three. A subagent follow is owned by the client that started it (per-client holds on the engine host, `host.followOwners`); `authorize.ts` now applies a registry row's `accepts`, which only `fs.unfollow` uses. e2e:remote's M8 case runs a second app attached at the same time and proves the listed row and Resume… on the client without the mark, the ask settling across clients, and one client's unfollow and quit leaving the other's follow held (the spawned row was within the visible rows 1,062 ms after B's engine host started it); acceptance 5's 5 s is measured live at M10. WIRE 24 to 26 and RL2 1.8 to 1.9, additive only; two `maxItems` in manifest entries that exist only on the branch were edited by hand, since the generator classes a raised bound as breaking, and regenerating leaves the manifest unchanged. Each of its seven tasks was reviewed until clean, with the fixes red first, then reviewer-high over the whole range found two Important defects (the re-read's record unguarded, and a message's upload bound taken from the per-call batch size), both fixed, and nothing in authorization or the wire. Full gate: run 41902970356, 0 flaky, its one red job (e2e:c7-utility) rerun once after the Electron download in its setup failed before any test ran. Eight tracker rows carry the residue.
217. **2026-11-05, the architect, at the landing of O7 M9 (PR #472, merge b26b72488): a session follows its machine's own Claude Code setup by default, on every machine, and a project missing on the target machine is set up there from the spawning app.** This builds the owner's two answers of 2026-11-04 ("Every machine (Recommended)" and "Set it up from here (Recommended)") and D-19's amended default. A launch on the `machine` route injects nothing, records what the engine's status reports as `observed`, and names it (ClaudeUsage when the base URL is that machine's relay, the Anthropic login, or the URL's host), never as a mismatch; usage, accounts and gateway models follow the observed route, a live status before a record's earlier one. `gateway` and `direct` stay per-session choices, and Session info's switch now reaches all three routes; switching back to the machine's own runs the launch's relay check, whose warning comes after the old launch has stopped (a tracker row). The default lives in its own `machine-route.json`, since an older build strict-parses `machine-settings.json`, and an older build's `gateway.defaultRoute` patch writes it only on a change. The route crosses as additive `route` and `observed` properties behind the engine host's `engine.route-machine` hello capability (WIRE 26 to 27, RL2 unchanged at 1.9). An older engine host is given the legacy direct route only when none of `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` or `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` resolves from the machine's environment or settings; otherwise, or where the machine uses a settings source the resolver does not model (server-managed remote settings, a policy helper, the cowork settings switch), the start refuses `route-unsupported`, and a dead relay there refuses even Start anyway, so inference never changes silently. Remote setup adds owner-only reads (`machine.folders.list`, `session.trust.check`) and the owner-only act `session.projects.addFolder`: the spawn sheet browses that machine's folders (a private folder opens only after its own confirmation), shows that machine's trust card, and a trusted spawn carries `trust: 'accepted'` through the attested bootstrap launch, so MAWS still never writes trust. Reading a folder that is not yet trusted runs none of its code. The setup reads resolve `.git` pointers, `commondir` and links segment by segment, case-insensitively, through the checked resolver before any read, parse config without following includes, and read the index with a bounded reader (4,096 bytes a name, 16 MiB in all, mandatory extensions refused); a 630-line object reader written on the way was dropped as more untrusted parsing than the question needed. One git process remains on that path, the org derivation's config read, run outside the folder with `--no-includes`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, since a fake-home probe showed git otherwise reads the global config's includes; the trust and start guards keep master's hardened git runner (a tracker row). e2e:remote's M9 case sets up an unregistered checkout on B, trusts it there and spawns on B's own route; cross-build reports no breaking change against wires 3.26 and 3.20, and against the 3.20 host (run 41966308441) proves the legacy route and both refusals. Live specs pin the gateway route, since their scenes' homes hold no machine login, and the e2e launch drops the runner's `ANTHROPIC_*` variables. Each of its five tasks was reviewed until clean (the setup reads in four rounds). Reviewer-high over the whole range found six Important defects at the seams between tasks (one owner's protected-folder consent serving another, an older daemon dropping a route choice, the model list ignoring the route, a discovered session's relay card moving its route, the switch back with no compatible model, and the custom sign-in's config file unread), all fixed red first; one bounded re-review closed them but for one shared seam (the default route read from this Mac for another machine's session), fixed, and its one unresolved finding is a tracker row. Full gate: run 41960398446, with one flaky pass in a case M9 does not reach: O16's handover exit counted as a crash while main had a call in flight to the old host (board #473). The machine route's live proof is M10's sitting.
218. **2026-11-05, the architect, at the landing of the desk track's PR #475 (merge 91ca5d02e), O10: the RL10 directory tells sminos when a machine has retired.** A deleted acceptance VM's sessions stayed in `sminos list --org` indefinitely, as did every other retired `dc-` VM's, because RL10's `org` frame listed every machine certificate under a live delegation as a live machine with a live machine project and sent every project `retired: false` (board #474, found by the desk track after the owner's drop of dc-acceptance-bd31 did nothing, the VM's certificate having been retired at its deletion). A machine is now retired at `now` when the head enrolls it under no name, former names included, and no certificate stands for it (authentic, its key unrevoked, its `notAfter` ahead); its machine project goes out `retired: true` and its `machines` entry carries an optional `retired: true`, which an older sminos ignores. Every name bound to an enrolled ref is exempt, so a forged certificate bearing a former name cannot retire it, and restatements now run one at a time, so an older one can no longer finish last. Deploying it makes each cut-over sminos uplink end its stream once and reconnect at the first frame carrying a retirement, the path DX2 used for a revoked machine (16 retirements gave one reconnect against sminos 1.33.0). The sminos half, a retired machine's rows reading `gone`, is doperpowers board #171. Gates: static, unit and `e2e:desk-web` on the head (run 41970740033, 0 flaky) and on the merge tree over master's M9 (run 41977503371); red first twice. Review: reviewer-medium, two P2s fixed red first, then correct.
219. **2026-11-05, the architect, the owner's rulings and directions at O7 M10's live sitting.** Touch ID: asked whether a spawn on another machine should still ask for it, given that opening a remote terminal on a machine one owns asks nothing and this MacBook reaches the mini by ssh without it, the owner answered "Not on my machines (Recommended)". A `session.spawn` on a machine whose owners include the signed-in member is signed with the same step-up key without a confirmation prompt, so the receiving machines and the owners' proof are unchanged; Touch ID stays for a spawn on another person's machine, an approval on a session the member does not own, owner and share changes, machine admin, the org state's signing and a non-owner's shell. *2026-11-13 (UTC):* Amended by the UX wave (U9; O-48; `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-12-ux-wave-U9-other-machines.md`), on the owner's words: the observation behind O-48 ("I cannot create a fork session from the remote machine …") and "Approve and start", 2026-11-12; this ruling's own reason extends to a fork on a machine the member owns. A `session.fork` on a machine the member owns is signed unasked, as `session.spawn` is; nothing else is. Built in O7 M10. Message reach, a direction amending section 7's "No new delivery policy (owner, 2026-10-26)", in the owner's words: "the regular UX I'd expect within org is that people will mostly keep their own machine private, mostly not allow other people to spawn agents on their own machine or control artifacts, terminals, browsers, editors, or more. At best they'll share the session transcript read-only with minimal sharing. for agent-to-agent cooperation, I can imagine that people either list their session address so that other people can talk to their agent, or optionally not even list and go 'unlisted' where no one can message that session either, or open receiving messages only from selected individuals." Machines private by default and read-only transcript sharing are already the design; who may message a session (listed, unlisted, or selected people) is board #477, to be designed with the desk track before a second member joins. Connectors switchable per machine and per session, so a connector like Notion (72 tools) costs no context where it is not wanted, is board #476, in the owner's words.
220. **2026-11-05, the architect, at the landing of O7 M10's code (PR #478, merge 6f9dd7af0): the live bench is fit for a live run, and the sitting's three defects are fixed, one of them only in part.** The bench reads `mawsd keys --json`'s flat `keyId` against fakes rendered by the endpoint's own answers. A new `mawsd endpoint sync` runs an org-state read that begins after the call (exit 0 listed, 4 hub-confirmed, 1 none). A run holds one status client, the mini's teardown waits for its scratch mawsd to be gone, and a precheck refuses a run while this Mac's or the mini's load1 is 5 or more, or the mini's cannot be read. reviewer-medium found one Important (a sync held across an unenroll answered 4), fixed red first. A: a remote session ranks by its latest activity, the newest of the record's `updatedAt`, the listing's `issued` and the turns seen on its stream; an unread remote session had ranked last among the unread. e2e:remote's M8 case now seeds nine unread idle sessions where it pinned nine, and was red on the old ranking (run 41980345401) and green on the fix. That covers a session whose turn ends off screen. A session read on screen still ranks behind every unread one under the default priority order, local and remote alike, and the owner's MAWS group holds more unread sessions than the group's eight rows, so the sitting's failure most likely stands for a session the owner watches; the order is put to the owner. B, the owner's ruling (Decision Log 219): main's signer skips the confirmation for exactly `session.spawn` when the held org head lists this device and key under the member and the member is among the machine's owners, trust acceptance included (the architect's ruling: the trust card is itself the confirmation, as it is locally, and an owner's own terminal on that machine already asks nothing); the renderer has no lever over it. reviewer-high, with a security lens: correct. C: a terminal-held session's thinking row ticks on a wall clock and leaves at close when it has no text (W1, unchanged); the composer's placeholder and the label's missing effort show the screenshot's session was terminal-held. reviewer-medium: correct. No wire or RL2 change. Gates: `--gates all` on the branch with master bf2dce3f4 merged (run 41981616511, 55 jobs, 0 flaky), static and unit on the docs-only head (run 41989347023). The live bench stays carried; the owner's live re-check and O7's close follow.
221. **2026-11-05, the architect, the owner's ruling on the sidebar's order and its landing in O7 M10's follow-up (PR #480, merge b8af88890): an unread session ranks ahead only while its unread turn is fresh.** At M10's landing the architect found why a session the owner had watched was missing from its group: nearly every row in the owner's MAWS group carried an unread dot, the 5-day to 2-week ones included, the default priority order ranked every unread session ahead of every read one, and a group draws at most eight rows, so a read session, local or remote, left the group once its first turn ended; Decision Log 220's fix covered only a session whose turn ends off screen. Asked which order the sidebar should use, the owner answered "Fresh unread first (Recommended)": an unread session ranks ahead only for 24 h after the turn that made it unread, and older unread rows keep their dot but rank by last activity with the rest, Needs-you and working still first. `priorityRank` (`src/renderer/src/overview/derive.ts`), which the sidebar and the Overview share, takes `now` as an argument and keeps an unread session in its tier only while `now` is under `UNREAD_FRESH_MS` (24 h) past its unread time: the digest's last turn end, or for a session with no digest its last activity with the seen activity; the sidebar reads a clock rounded to five minutes off the shared 30-second tick. e2e:remote's M8 case now follows the owner's flow: the first turn ends on screen, the row carries no dot, the tab closes, and past the stream's linger the row leads the five drawn rows above nine stale unread sessions; a probe with the 24-hour check reverted failed it and the unit cases (run 41994264716). Gates: `--gates all` on dabb67a04 (run 41995538110), with three jobs that died in setup on GitHub's Electron download relaunched green on the head (run 42005780355), and static and unit on the last code commit (run 41995935803). Three hosted specs passed on retry, none of them reached by a sort and a clock: connectors-direct.spec.ts:878 (a connector grant's count), supervisor.spec.ts:1270 (j) (the main process's quit guard) and :1603 (n) (a daemon's socket); they go to the hosted-Mac flake row. Review: reviewer-medium found one Important in the e2e (closing the tab before A's own status ended the turn), fixed by waiting for Send; the ranking itself sound.
222. **2026-11-05, the architect, O7 closes at M10's live re-check; its last follow-up, a thinking block's start (PR #482, merge 5c22d0ce6), landed.** The re-check ran on the MacBook swapped to master 01c0b239c at 10:13Z (the desk track, on the owner's "Swap when ready (Recommended)"), with the mini on 5f2bc8964. B: a "+ Session" on the mini asked no Touch ID (the owner: "yes, didn't ask for touchID"). A: the owner watched the session's first turn end, closed its tab, and "the row stayed under MAWS", under the fresh-unread order of Decision Log 221. C: the live label counted up, and closed blocks read "Thought for 5s", "4s" and "7s", but blocks whose text came in one burst read "Thought for 0s". Their cause was a thinking row starting at its first text delta: the normaliser emitted nothing at `content_block_start`, and on claude-opus-5-5 the text arrives 0.2 to 2.9 s after the start and the block stops within 1 to 23 ms of it in 10 of the fixtures' 15 blocks. The architect's ruling, built in #482: the engine host reports a thinking block's start as an `assistant.block.delta` with an empty `thinkingDelta`, the existing shape, so the wire and RL2 stay unchanged and the live label and the final figure run from the block's real start; and a figure under a second reads "Thought", on the threshold the turn divider shares. It reaches each machine with its next build. A terminal session's live label still starts at the bridge's first thinking chunk, because the engine's hook contract hands a hook a block's start as an untyped engine chunk (the desk track's reading of the sminos types; a tracker row). Gates for #482: `--gates all` on its last code commit (run 42013363487, 0 flaky; the snapshot its fixture moved regenerated after), and static, unit and both packaged shards on the head (runs 42022352182 and 42026197040), each packaged failure a known hosted flake in a spec it does not reach (presentations-comments.spec.ts:248, render-host.spec.ts:442). Review: reviewer-medium, correct. O7 is done: M1 to M10 landed and recorded in its Outcomes. The live remote bench, whose fixes landed in M10, was never run against a real machine and is carried as a tracker row.
223. **2026-11-05, the architect, under O18 (new), O8 and O12, at the owner's ruling on message reach (board #477): an agent may message a session exactly when its person can see it, a share gains "Can message", and `listed` stays the default.** The desk track's draft, on sminos 1.34.0 and master b7e7d9594, found three gaps. A peer `say` is never checked for reach. sminos sends no session's `access` or `shares`, so every session is listed in effect. And every org machine's logs carry every session's lifecycle events and the `say` events addressed to it, so only the hub listing honours `private`. Asked who may message a session's agent, the owner answered "Can message shares (Recommended)": a member's agents may message a session when the member's rank on it is at least `list`. A listed or org-wide session then takes messages from any member's agents, and a private one only from its owners and the people it is shared with. A new lowest share grade, `list` ("Can message"), lets a member's agents message a session the member cannot read. The architect added that grade to the draft's recommended rule: without it, letting someone's agents message a private session would have meant letting them read its transcript, against the owner's "minimal sharing". The owner declined two alternatives: the same rule with shares as they are, and a separate reach setting that would allow a session the org can read but few can message. Asked the default, the owner answered "Listed (Recommended)". This amends section 7's "No new delivery policy (owner, 2026-10-26)", and section 7 is revised. The architect accepted the draft's technical recommendations. The receiving machine's endpoint decides, through a `reach?` ask beside `words?`, failing closed with the delivery kept owed. The sender's check is resolution. A refusal returns as a signed `refused` event. Unlisted is met only when the hub filters each private session's events by a signed audience; at the architect's review, the desk track added an `unlisted` event for a removed share. O18 is opened for this work, owned by the desk track. By the architect's reading of Decision Log 219 it is not required for P5's close, because the owner tied it to a second member's joining, which P5 defers.
224. **2026-11-05, the architect, under O18, O8 and O11, at the landing of O18's document (PR #483, merge 95000f84a): message reach is built as audience streams the hub filters whole, and refused at the hub's upload before delivery.** The desk track wrote the document from its draft and Decision Log 223's ruling: design D1 to D10, the remote layer's amendments, and milestones M1 (the hub's policy registry, upload check and filter), M2 (the endpoint's `list` grade, `reach?`, audiences and policy cache), M2b (device and browser clients), M3 and M4 (sminos), M5 (O8's share picker) and M6 (live acceptance on the staging org with a second member). Its review was a security and a buildability adversarial review, then three rounds of revision and re-review, then a narrow check that approved with no material findings; each round confirmed the earlier fixes closed. Four means changed what section O18 said, each recorded in the document's Decision Log. First, the audience belongs to a writer stream, not an event: sminos imports each writer strictly in sequence, so a withheld event would leave a permanent gap. A private session's events therefore go to their own writer, named after the session and a digest of the audience, and the hub filters whole writers on every surface it reads through one check. Second, an event's audience is the intersection over every session it names, checked against an org-wide, revisioned policy registry at the hub. Third, the architect approved moving the refusal: a signed refusal written under the recipient's writer would have given away the hidden session's machine and answered a forger's guess. The hub instead refuses at upload any event naming a session it doesn't know or that doesn't admit it, with the same `not-reachable` for a hidden session as for a nonexistent one. The refusal returns over the sender's own upload path, and a content-free tombstone keeps the sender's writer contiguous. The hub can only refuse, never admit, and the receiving endpoint's `reach?` still authorizes each delivery. Fourth, sminos sends access, shares and the `list` grade only to an endpoint that advertises them, since an older endpoint rejects a member frame carrying an unknown grade. Three conditions the architect set are acceptance items. Widening reach (going listed or org-wide, adding a share, raising a grade) runs only as the operator at a terminal with a confirmation read from it, while narrowing runs from any shell. A private session's agent messages only sessions within its audience, and the CLI refuses any other before writing anything, so messaging out never opens reach back. And a message admitted while a share stood, or sent from a stale view, is never delivered after the share is removed. The reviews' further refinements are the document's own: binding a session to the machine that first exposed it, a validated `unlisted` notice, redacted spawner references, split messages' routing tags rewritten, the archive written only from what the hub admitted as public, and capability gates on RL1 and RL10 that hold private streams back from an older hub, endpoint or sminos. Section O18 is amended to match: its Repos line (the hub is in MAWS, `apps/desk-hub`), the refusal bullet, the stream bullet, and acceptance 3, which reads "refused before delivery: at the hub's upload, with the recipient's endpoint as defense in depth" in place of "refused at the recipient's machine". Acceptance items 7 and 8 are added. M5 is the MAWS architect's, built with O8 M6. Production's hub deploy is the owner's and waits for M6's proof. Gate: static on the head (run 42109806508), which equals the merge tree.
225. **2026-11-05, the architect, under O18 and O11, at the landing of O18 M1 (PR #484, merge ff1b9d581): the hub keeps the org's session policy registry, refuses at upload what it puts out of reach, and serves each audience stream only to its audience; no production hub deploy from this master until O18 M2 and M2b land.** The hub keeps one registry row per session: the machine that first exposed it, a binding that never moves, and its latest audience and `policyRev`, which only the session's own `joined` and `state` snapshots move. The registry is filled once, at the deploy that brings it, from every project's newest snapshot. One read check covers every hub surface that returns stored events, writer names, vectors, audit rows or session-plane rows, and a frame already queued is checked again against its reader when it leaves the queue. The protocol package gains the audience digest and stream names, and the remote layer's sections are amended in the same PR. Review: reviewer-high with a security lens, three passes (five findings with three P1, then one, then correct). The in-flight send window and the side channel in the offsets' volume are board #485 (P3, deferred). The new tests (`policies.test.ts`, `audience-reads.test.ts`) were each seen red first. Gates: static and unit on the code head (run 42120533710), then static, unit, probes, `e2e:remote` (its scene enrolls on the hub's local runner) and `e2e:desk-web` on the head (run 42122486308, 0 flaky); the head contains master, so its tree is the merge tree. No wire version moves: the endpoint hello's `capabilities` is optional, so no cross-build. The deploy rule, on the desk track's finding: a production hub built from a master that contains M1 makes each machine reconnect once and refuses, as `not-reachable`, any addressed event naming a session the registry does not know, and today's endpoints and desk-web read the resulting withheld tombstone as malformed until M2 and M2b land. The backfill makes that rare while the org is the owner alone, but not impossible, for instance for a session whose snapshot never reached the hub. Production's hub deploy already waits for M6's proof. Should a production hub deploy for anything else come due before M2 and M2b land, M1's backstop goes behind a flag first. Staging takes M1 now, from the desk track.
226. **2026-11-05, the architect, under O18, O10 and O11, at the landing of O18 M2 (PR #486, merge 617fde4b5): the endpoint answers `reach?`, signs each private session's events into its audience stream, and keeps the policy cache; M1's batch order is fixed.** The endpoint gains the `list` share grade. It decides a peer delivery through `reach?`: same owner first, then the sender's rank on the recipient. It signs a private session's events into the stream of their audience, and holds them back as owed from a hub that does not advertise `audience`. It keeps a policy cache built from verified snapshots, ordered by `policyRev`. M2 also fixes a fault in M1 that O18 M3's executor found. A `say` naming a seat that joined later in the same upload batch was withheld, about half the time when a session spawned a seat and messaged it at once. The hub now judges an event's references against the batch's own snapshots, and still judges an `unlisted` notice against the rows from before the batch; the tests were red first. Review: reviewer-high with a security lens, two rounds. Round 1 found two P1s and a P2, all fixed: a ledger resend of a private stream's event to a hub without `audience`, a device's stream writer that resolved to no member, and an `audience` hold left in place after a void. Round 2 came back correct. Gates: static, unit and probes on the code head (run 42135782716), then static, unit, probes, `e2e:remote`, `e2e:remote-latency-check`, `e2e:desk-web` and `e2e:packaged` on the head (run 42137314954, 0 flaky), the packaged group because the packaged `mawsd` loads the endpoint as a chunk. The head contains master, so its tree is the merge tree. No wire version moves. Residue: a machine of phase 1, held in the registry by its name, loses its binding when it is renamed, so its rejoin is withheld as another machine's; every machine enrolled through its endpoint keeps its ref and is unaffected (a tracker row, the fix proposed in the PR). An older desk-web hides a private session whose listing carries the new fields, which M2b fixes. Correcting Decision Log 225: the desk track holds M1 off staging until just before M6. Staging's machines run today's endpoints, so the hub's refusals would only reach the staging and phone runs, with nothing to test until M2 and M3 are in. The production rule stands: no production hub deploy from master until M2b lands as well. O18 M3, sminos, lands in doperpowers after this.
227. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O18 and O13, at the landing of O18 M2b (PR #487, merge 9a3848039): desk-web's frozen client stays frozen and is refused toward a private session; desk-web reads the hub's withheld tombstone; the production hub waits for M2's endpoint on every machine.** M2b was first built to let a browser message a private session: the hub would serve each browser the policy rows of the private sessions whose audience holds its member, and desk-web's `say` would seal each event with that audience. That `say` is in the DX6 client, which desk-web's CLAUDE.md freezes, to be fixed only when a moved machine's phone loop depends on it. The architect asked what leaked without the change. M1 already refuses, at upload, an unchanged browser's human or `say` naming a private session (`not-reachable`), storing and fanning out nothing, and an `@all` that resolves to a private member is refused whole. The executor checked that premise and found no path the hub misses. So the DX6 client stays frozen, and its refusal is its recorded behaviour. The `say` change, desk-web's audience planner and rows store, and the hub's policy frames were reverted, and are kept in O18's Decision Log as designed and set aside. What stays is desk-web's verdict on a withheld tombstone (`src/log/`, outside the frozen files) and the hub's proof that an unchanged browser is refused. The architect ruled that the audit row of a refused device human stays visible to the project's browsers: it is content-free, names no session, reads the same for a nonexistent one, and matches what any reader of a plain writer sees of its tombstones. Residue: a browser cannot message a private session until a desk-web client is unfrozen (board #488, P3, deferred). Review: reviewer-high with a security lens on the net diff, no material findings. Gates: static, unit and `e2e:desk-web` on the merged head before its last spec-only commit (run 42141731122, 0 flaky); the records commit's static gate covers the spec. Correcting Decision Logs 225 and 226 on the production hub: landing M2 and M2b on master is not enough. desk-web's half ships with the hub, since the production deploy builds desk-web from the same checkout. But a machine's endpoint is in its `mawsd` and gets M2 only from a MAWS swap, and the MacBook (01c0b239c), the mini (5f2bc8964) and the acceptance VM all run builds from before M2, so their endpoints would answer `rejected` to a tombstone. No production hub deploy from a master containing M1 until every enrolled machine's `mawsd` runs a build at or after 617fde4b5; any earlier one puts M1's backstop behind a flag first. The swaps that bring M2 come after the soak's build check ends (2026-11-06 22:18Z), at a time the owner gives.
228. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O18, at the landing of M2's follow-up for M4 (PR #489, merge a6bae52c0): the endpoint's `audience` frame for a local session names the members a change withdrew.** O18 M4's executor found that sminos could not name the stream for the `unlisted` notice when a listed session goes private, since it holds no member list. The `audience` frame now carries an optional `withdrawn`, the members the change at that `policyRev` withdrew. One shared `withdrawnBy()` computes it for both the frame and the signer's check of the notice, so sminos is told exactly the set the signer accepts. Interfaces §B is amended, and O18's Decision Log rejects reading `org.members` instead. Review: reviewer-high with a confidentiality lens, correct. Gates: static, unit and probes on the head (run 42146373571, 0 flaky), and `e2e:remote` and `e2e:desk-web` on the head (run 42147350130); the head contains master. The first attempt had one flaky pass, `e2e/remote.spec.ts:431`, where A's sidebar did not expand B's machine line within 30 s after A's relaunch. That step changes no session's policy, so it reaches no line of #489, and the rerun of `e2e:remote` passed with none. It reaches the machines with the swap build after the soak (owner, 2026-11-06: "Right after the soak (Recommended)").
229. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O18, at the landing of O18's records for M3 and M4 (PR #491, merge d85d2a42d): sminos's half is built, and widening a session's reach is closed to every agent only by the app's step-up, before a second member joins.** M3 (sminos 1.35.0, doperpowers PR #463) and M4 (sminos 1.36.0) are built and reviewed at reviewer-high. 1.35.0 sends today's endpoints what 1.34.0 does, except for the hello's optional capabilities, which no endpoint before M2 reads (a replay of the pre-M2 fixture, 107 ok and 0 failures). Both merge after the post-soak swaps, so the soak's last day runs on the sminos it started with. M4's review found that an agent's shell can widen a session's reach on its own: it unsets `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, runs the CLI under a pty it controls, and types the confirmation, which the reviewer reproduced for `sminos access <s> listed`. Nothing that runs as the same user can tell that apart from the person at a terminal, and an ancestry check would add code without closing it. The architect accepted the CLI gate as built, which stops an agent that follows its instructions, and moved the real fix into the app. Widening goes only through M5's `record.access` and `record.shares` offers, with step-up, and once they exist the CLI refuses to widen ("use MAWS") and keeps narrowing. That cutover is a precondition of a second member joining the production org, beside the production hub rule (Decision Log 227). Until then the org is the owner alone, so a session an agent widens is exposed to no one else. The owner was told. Section O18's acceptance 8 is reworded to match. One item stays open: D5's republication of a machine's own snapshot when the hub withholds it, which M4 did not build. The desk track tickets it, and unless it shows a bound for how long such a session stays unknown to the registry, it is a precondition of the production hub deploy, since an unknown session's messages, the owner's own included, would be refused. M5, the Share sheet, rides with O8 M6, whose re-plan is executing; M6, the live run, shares one staging run with O8 M6. Gate: static on the head (run 42160992535), whose tree is the merge tree.
230. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O8, at the landing of the hub's provisioning-key renewal (PR #493, merge 9964a7551): a provisioning key's expiry follows its delegation in the signed org state, moved by an admin route the app calls after submitting that state.** O8 M4c renews a provisioning key's delegation from the app. The desk track built the hub's half, on the architect's ruling for O8's re-plan: `POST /api/provisioning-keys/<id>/renew`, admin-only, with no body. It moves the hub's copy of the key's `expires` to the expiry of the delegation with that id in the org-state head the hub holds, so the key the hub redeems never outlives what the roots signed, and the app's order is fixed: submit the signed extension, then call the route. It answers the key as `GET /api/provisioning-keys` lists it, and refuses 403 `not-admin`, 404 `unknown-key`, 410 `key-revoked`, 410 `key-expired` (an elapsed key is never revived), 409 `not-renewed` (the head holds no later delegation of that id) and 409 `too-long` (past now plus `MAX_PROVISIONING_KEY_TTL_S`, the cap a new key has, which the app checks before signing). A delegation's expiry is always a date, so no key runs without one. Review: reviewer-medium, no findings. Gates: static and unit on the head (run 42163673652), and `e2e:remote`, `e2e:remote-latency-check`, `e2e:desk-web` and `e2e:desk-web-hub` on the head (run 42164697829); the head contains master. Production gets the route with the owner's next production hub deploy, staging with the desk track's deploy when O8's e2e needs it.
231. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O18 and O12, on widening after M5's cutover: a machine enrolled at no hub keeps the terminal gate, and the cutover closes widening to every agent that goes through sminos, not to a hostile process of the same user.** The desk track asked how the CLI should treat widening after the cutover on a machine enrolled at no hub. There is no org there, no second member, and no app path to widen through, so refusing would leave the owner no way back from private. The architect ruled that such a machine keeps the operator's terminal gate, on two conditions: enrollment is read as `hub.json` in the same sminos home as the records, never from an environment variable, a flag or a state directory pointing elsewhere; and enrolling changes no record, so the cutover applies from enrollment on. The exception is per machine. The desk track proposed per project, so that a session in a project with no hub could still be widened. On an enrolled machine, though, every directory without a `.smns` is in a hub project (its repository's, or the machine's own, which holds P5's sessions with no project), so the app can address it. The only projects with no hub are those a committed `.smns` declares without one, and sminos publishes nothing of them, so no member sees or reaches their sessions and their reach has no effect. A per-project exception would also open a path through sminos's own verbs: join a project with no hub, widen at a terminal, join back. Correcting Decision Log 229 and acceptance 8: neither the endpoint nor the hub checks a widening against an act. The endpoint takes a session's access and shares from sminos's member frame, and the hub's registry takes the machine-signed snapshot, checking only its binding and `policyRev` order. A process of the same user that writes sminos's record file, calls `set_visibility` itself, or moves `hub.json` aside to reach the terminal gate still widens. Such a process can already send its transcript anywhere, so a hub-checked act would close one path of many, and none is required. The cutover closes widening to every agent that goes through sminos, and the hubless gate holds against an agent that follows its instructions. The owner was told. O18's next document PR records both, after board #492.
232. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O8, O18 and O15, at the landing of O8's re-plan of the rest (PR #494, merge 5a5d2b22c): every remaining O8 milestone is executable, built in the order M7a, M7b, M6a, M6b, M4b, M4c, M5b, M8, and O8 builds its own pieces in the endpoint and the services.** O8's executor re-planned the advisory milestones on master 910539147, on the architect's rulings, after a `doperpowers:adversarial-reviewer` buildability review of three rounds (13, 3 and 0 findings, all adopted). M7 splits into M7a (the Channel tab: the project log read and verified, people's words sent v2-only, agent-to-agent traffic folded per exchange, migrated history labelled, waiting cards answered inline) and M7b (to-human entries across machines, from a per-project journal in the spine and a feed the endpoint serves). M6 splits into M6a (reader state and drafts per member) and M6b (the Share sheet, carrying O18's M5: the `record.access`, `record.shares` and `record.owners` offers gated on sminos's `record` capability, `record.policy`, and a local door to this Mac's own endpoint that takes only the three record acts, their signature verified every time). Scope: O8's Constraints are amended so that M4b, M4c, M6a, M6b and M7b change `src/endpoint/`, `src/services/` and `src/mawsd/endpoint-seam.ts`, and M4b and M4c move code into `apps/org-protocol`, each such hunk in commits of its own that the desk track reviews; the hub stays as landed. The desk track builds sminos's three executors, Decision Log 190 (b)'s join fix before M5b, and the CLI's widening cutover once M6b's offers are on master (Decision Log 231). Custody, the architect's ruling: M4c's new delegation code goes from main into Secret Manager through `gcloud secrets versions add` with the code on stdin alone, after the state is signed into a persisted slot that stays unsent until the version is confirmed. It is never logged, put on the clipboard, written to disk or shown, which keeps Decision Log 75 (1) once the app is the only signer. A renewal signs the state's extension first, then calls the hub's route (Decision Log 230). Correcting Decision Log 30: an in-place `--replace` keeps a machine's accepted grants (`src/endpoint/enroll.ts:208-225`); a machine that lost its ledger, or enrolled again with a new key, loses them, so M4b re-signs only the issued grants the machine's `endpoint.grants` lacks. M8 is owner steps and evidence: M3's root-add sitting first (skipped when `admin show` lists the app's device as a root), then O14 B4's steps 4 to 8, then the app revokes the deployment admin's device. The owner's week (acceptance 16) starts when M7b lands, the architect's recommendation, told to the owner. M6b's live proof is the staging run shared with O18 M6, which needs a second member the owner controls. Reviews: reviewer-high with a security lens on M4b, M4c, M6b and M8; reviewer-medium on M7a, M7b and M6a, with a confidentiality lens on M7b's feed and M6a's reader routing. Gate: static on the records commit, which covers the document.
233. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O12, O8 and O18, at the landing of Decision Log 190's residue (b) (the desk track's PR #498, merge b59428afd): the endpoint places a lane B record it can't join by the spawn it was launched for, so owners can move from O8's app without a window on the spawner's bridge.** A `/clear` can replace a lane B session's engine session before its first record arrives, or while the join reads `engine.sessions`, so the live rows never name the record's engine session and the spawner's bridge held on the new one. The join now falls back to the spawn: the `ownersProof` act whose action is `session.spawn`, matched against the spine's `launchedBy.opId` through `session.list` with the archived included. It is the spawn act and not the first entry, since once `record.owners` exists a record with no spawn act can hold an owner's act first. An opId two app sessions share places the record under neither and marks each handed off, so a spawner's bridge on either ends until that session's own record decides. A record joined by its spawn while the spine names another engine session for its app session never replaces that session's listing. A record with no spawn act, or one no spine record names, stays unplaced as before. Review: reviewer-medium over four rounds of fixes, the last correct; the P1 among them was a candidate's subscription left alive on a shared opId. Gates: static and unit on the head (run 42172404053), then static, unit, probes, `e2e:remote`, `e2e:remote-latency-check`, `e2e:desk-web` and `packaged` on the merge (run 42173503872). Cost: an unplaced spawned record reads `session.list` on each member frame, bounded later by a cache on the spine's revision if it matters. O8's M5b, Add owner, waited on this. With it landed the RL10 conversation fixture's sminos hellos carry the `record` capability (PR #496, merge bdd6cb7db), which sminos 1.38.0's conformance replay compares exactly; 1.38.0 ships the three `record.*` executors that M6b's and M5b's offers need on a target machine.
234. **2026-11-06, the architect, at the landing of the quit reaper (PR #502, merge fd33c5818): a quit on a saturated Mac ends within about 6 s, and the stall below it is reported to Electron.** The stall is Chromium's, after MAWS's own shutdown. Under one busy loop per core, Electron's `quit` fires and then the browser process waits in `TaskTracker::CompleteShutdown` for a shutdown-blocking task on a background-QoS worker that macOS does not schedule while default-QoS work fills every core. A MAWS-free Electron 44.4.5 app reproduces it (8 of 8 stalled, 12 of 12 exited with fewer busy loops than cores). The owner said "File it (Recommended)", and the architect filed electron/electron#55647 from the flake agent's draft, with its repro inline and no MAWS internals. The duplicates searched were #52582 (a macOS 26.4 hang without load, closed) and #55208 (a prevented `before-quit`); neither matches. The mitigation, `src/main/lifecycle/reaper.ts`, is the architect's design: at `quit`, after the shutdown list's steps on both paths (so it cannot cut a drain or the #473 handover), a detached `/bin/sh` sleeps 5 s and then, only while main is still its parent, SIGKILLs main. 5 s is about ten times the p99 from `quit` to exit with half the cores busy (78 quits, p99 530 ms, max 1.2 s). A new first step flushes every browser session's storage and cookies, so the kill loses no site data. One deviation, accepted: the reaper also kills main's own Chromium helpers, each a child of main whose executable is a `*Helper*` in the bundle's Frameworks, re-checking each one's parent and path just before its signal. Killing main alone left a helper alive for minutes holding its launcher's stdio, which made every Playwright teardown time out. It never signals a process group, so `mawsd` and the engine hosts, which outlive the app by design, are never matched. Reviews: reviewer-high with a lifecycle lens found one real issue, a parent check reused across the helper lookups, fixed with per-signal re-checks and a regression case. The architect's reviewer-medium on the kill path then found that a check and its `kill` are two steps: main can exit between them, and a helper is then signalled after reparenting, or in principle after its pid is reused. The architect accepted that as residue. A reparented helper is still MAWS's own helper, which the sweep exists to end. Pid reuse inside that window needs the pid space to wrap within milliseconds. And the same two-step gap is in any `kill(2)` by pid on macOS, which has no process handle to signal through, the main kill included. Gates: `--gates all` on the head, whose tree is the merge tree (run 42214107193), and the agent's `--gates all` on 2fa2dfebf (54 jobs, 0 flaky).
235. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O18 and O8, on the CLI's widening cutover: it follows each machine's own endpoint, and a second member waits for every machine to be cut over.** The desk track asked what turns on the CLI's refusal to widen (Decision Logs 229 and 231). The architect ruled it keys on the endpoint's RL10 welcome to sminos carrying the capability `record`, which an endpoint says exactly when its build serves the three `record.*` offers (O8 M6b adds it in the same commit as them, a headless machine's endpoint included). The refusal is sticky: sminos keeps the word in its `audiences.json`, so an endpoint that is away or stopped keeps the cutover. Only a later welcome without `record`, a MAWS rollback, lifts it. The desk track built it as sminos 1.39.0 (branch `o18-widening-cutover`): widening goes only through the app where sminos is cut over to an endpoint in its own sminos home whose kept capabilities carry `record`, and an unreadable `audiences.json` on a cut-over machine counts as `record`, failing closed. Decision Log 231's exception becomes "not cut over": a machine whose sminos has no such endpoint, such as a DX2-enrolled machine without MAWS, keeps the terminal gate. The architect accepted it. So the cutover no longer waits on M6b's landing date, and a second member joining the production org now waits on four things: O18 landed, the production hub rule (Decision Log 227), D5's republication (board #492, Decision Log 229), and every enrolled machine on sminos 1.39.0 or later with its endpoint's welcome carrying `record`. The sminos releases (1.37.0 D5, 1.38.0 the record executors, 1.39.0 the cutover) merge after the post-soak swaps, the mini's after its research run ends.
236. **2026-11-06, the architect, at the landing of the remote subagent drill-in (PR #501, merge c6c5a57e9): a subagent of a lane B session on another machine shows its own rows, live and after its end.** The owner reported, with a screenshot of the Subagents drill-in: "one report -- sesion on mini, (spawned from MAWS not cli,) the app doesn't show the subagent trasncript". The drill-in showed main's remote overlay, `REMOTE_GAPS['subagent-rows']` (`src/main/lane-b/remote-outcomes.ts`), carried over from O9's lane A gap when O7 M2b drew the overlay, and never lifted after O7 M4 offered `fs.agentEvents` and M8 gave the client its own follows. For a running agent of a live session the gap was all that was missing: its rows are journal events carrying its `agentId`, which plane B already carries, and its slice already crosses as `session.events`. Its end was not: the local rule switches an ended agent to its disk transcript, which over the link is one unbudgeted answer whose later lines never cross. So a child of another machine's session reads the session's stream whenever this launch started it, its end included, and reads disk only for an agent this launch never started; a resume or a `remote.gap` takes it back to the stream. Main pages an agent's slice of a live launch past the channel's bound, the two waits on `fs.followState` re-ask for a remote session instead of stalling, and a disk window too large for the link says so in words. Nothing under `src/endpoint/` changed, and the O7 plan's capability list now says the gap is lifted for lane B. A remote terminal (lane A) session's subagent keeps lane A's gap, as O9's Decision 12 ruled: the endpoint's plane A subscribes upstream at a session's main scope, which never carries an agent's rows, and nothing on the machine journals a terminal session's stream. Lifting it would take an optional `agentId` on RL2's lane A subscription behind a peer capability, plane A keyed by session and agent, and main's watch and the renderer's remote plane taking agent targets, a few hundred lines across four layers. The owner was told and asked whether to ticket it. Review: three rounds of reviewer-medium, each finding fixed with a test that reproduces it; a fourth reviewer-medium on the last commit, the round-3 fix, found it correct. Residue is in the tech-debt tracker (an agent read from disk across machines). Gates: red first (run 42193080250, the drill-in reading the gap's words), the agent's static, unit and `e2e:remote` (run 42198471280, 0 flaky), and `--gates all` on the composition of PRs #502, #501 and #503, whose tree is this landing set's final tree but for the records (run 42216010699). Its one red job, `e2e:browser-comment`'s case at spec line 414 (an app-style pane's scroll not seen within 5 s on the mini at load 6.4), is reached by none of the three PRs, and it passed on the job's rerun with 0 flaky at load 16.
237. **2026-11-06, the architect, at the landing of New chat's machine first (PR #503, merge 1dabeaccd): a chat opens on any machine without a project, in any folder there, git or not, and a remote caller never reaches a machine's home through a session rooted at it.** The owner said: "another friction I noticed is that you can't open new session on other machines unless I declare certain project on that machine. this is not needed -- let machine be chosable without choosing project. LIke if I just want to open session on mini, on certain directory with no git, I should be able to." The architect's design: New chat's machine chip opens with no project chosen, and the project chip then follows the chosen machine, offering its projects, New project (that machine's folder browser from O7 M9: any folder, trusted and registered there as a project) and Don't work in a project (the machine's home, whose trust is asked once). M9's "Set up this project there…" item is gone; its flow is now the machine, then New project. New project and the home are for the machine's owners only. On the endpoint, `session.spawn` takes `checkout: '~'` for the home rather than an omitted checkout, which would be a breaking wire change. It is owner-only, resolves through the same realpathed home helper as the rule below, and is offered as `rl2.spawn-home` at RL2 1.10, so a machine on an older build greys the item with "update MAWS on" and the machine's name. `git.worktree.list` answers git's own toplevel (WIRE_MINOR 28), and `MachineState` carries the machine's rights and capabilities to the renderer. The desk track's condition, widened by the architect to every member, owners included: for a remote caller, a session root at the machine's home or above it is no root. `git.*` answers no target, the checkout feed holds nothing, `files.*` narrows in the files service's own root computation through a remote flag the dispatch sets, and `scm.*` checks the checkout's git toplevel and trust key and fails closed. A repository is known through `git.worktree.list` and checked at request time on the requested directory, its two Unicode spellings compared by device and inode, with native real paths for every grant. This also closes the existing gap of a machine-started chat in no project, whose shared view would have shown the home. So a remote viewer of a chat in a home folder sees its conversation and none of its files, git or scm. Review: four rounds by the desk track on the endpoint and a read-through of the last two commits, converged; reviewer-high with an authorization lens in two passes, every finding fixed, and a follow-up pass over the last commits, correct. Residue, accepted, is in the tech-debt tracker (the home-root rule's edges, and the worktree service's NFC folding on Linux, which the desk track found). e2e does not drive a member without `spawn` or a non-owner's greyed items, since the scene's org has one member. Gates: red first (run 42197614854), the agent's full set on 40d5d5979 (run 42205374911, 55 of 56 jobs and 0 flaky; the one red, a unit test whose expectation the desk track's round 4 changed, is corrected by the test-only head), its static, unit, probes and `e2e:cross-build` on the head (run 42206657034, 0 flaky), and `--gates all` on the composition (run 42216010699). #501 and #503 both appended a case to `e2e/remote.spec.ts`; the architect kept both in this landing's merge. The mini runs the new endpoint only after its swap, which follows the semis research run's end; until then its home item is greyed.
238. **2026-11-06, the architect, under O8, at the landing of O8 M7a (PR #504, merge 10b840079): a project's Channel tab shows the project's signed log as main verified it, offline from a cache that is one reader's, and a person's words are signed and sent from it.** Built to O8's §M7a as re-planned (Decision Log 232). The tab opens from a project's menu for a project with a `smnsId` and draws exchanges between two sessions folded, broadcasts, people's words, earlier to-human entries, history attested at migration or marked legacy, and a count of what failed verification, none of which draws. Main is the trust boundary: it reads the hub's Project socket, verifies every event against the held head, and hands the renderer verified events with `mac` and `sig` stripped; the channel key never leaves main. Offline, the tab draws from the cache as of the last catch-up with the reason, and the composer is withdrawn. Two decisions the architect checked against the design. The cache and its resume cursor belong to one reader, the org and the member: a member switch resets the channel and reads from the start, since a held log is what the hub served that member and no other may see it; re-reading on each switch is the accepted cost. And "Not sent" holds within the app's life, so a relaunch keeps no copy of an unsent word, which replaced the spec's self-contradictory line. Review: a task reviewer at each of the three frontiers, then reviewer-medium on the branch with a confidentiality lens in three rounds (round 1 three P1s and a P2, round 2 a P1 and a P2, round 3 none), every finding fixed. No `src/endpoint` or `apps/org-protocol` hunk, so nothing for the desk track. Gates: `--gates all --evidence` on the product tree with master 18d73c708 merged (run 42259619702, 55 jobs green, packaged included); the commits after it touch only O8's spec and report. Its one flaky pass, `e2e/browser-cua.spec.ts:1028` (O17 scene 4), is in a headless scene that runs mawsd, the engine and cua and no app, so no M7a code; cua's first `windows.query` answered not connected before its bridge joined, the signature scene 3 showed on the previous head, now tracked in the friction tracker. Residue: three layout points of the tab (the offline footer's scrollbar gutter, the 24-character alias cap, long-named exchange rows in a column under about 500 px), registered for the UX return as board #505 on the owner's word for UX items. M7b, to-human messages across machines, is next, cut from master after this landing.

240. **2026-11-07, the architect, under O14, O18 and O8, at the desk track's report of the mini's swap: both Macs run master f857f0450 from one build.** The semis research run's evening ended at 23:58Z on 11-06, before its booked 01:30Z, so the mini's swap went early; CI-opt held the mini's runners from 00:05Z and mini-ops waited for the running `e2e:default` job to drain (00:05:57Z). 246. **2026-11-07, the architect, under O8, O10 and O17, at the landing of O8 M7b (PR #514, merge 9353f255b): a project's Channel tab shows what sessions sent their human, from every machine of the project, each reader seeing only their part; the owner's week (acceptance 16) starts from it.** Built to O8's §M7b as re-planned (Decision Log 232), on M7a (Decision Log 238). Beside M7a's signed log the tab draws each machine's to-human messages, permission requests and questions, with that machine's mark, in time order across machines, each machine's history paging back; a machine whose endpoint lacks `rl2.channel` is read once through `channel.history` and opens no feed. The architect's rulings, as built. One extraction of to-human items, `src/shared/owner/to-human-items.ts`, serves lane A's `#host` hand-off, lane B's owner-channel events and the transcript's own derivation. The spine's journal is `channel_entries`, an `ADDED_TABLES` entry under `user_version` 15 with AUTOINCREMENT ids, so a pruned id is never reused under a cursor and a downgraded build keeps its index. MAWS asks come from their own record (`maws.ask.opened` and `answered`, each part of `ask/<askId>/<qid>` encoded), a subagent's asks included. The start path holds 1,000 entries and answers wait 256 for their question; text is cut at 64 KiB and options at 512 B with 32 kept, in the journal's `record`. `channel.changed` is main-only, through a new `mainOnlyEvents` list. `MachineFeed` gains `channel` in `org-protocol`, offered as `rl2.channel`, and the feed is project-scoped (`{t:'sub', feed:'channel', project, cursor, credit}`); the hub relays it as an opaque channel frame, so no hub deploy is needed. The endpoint half went through six rounds of the desk track's review (581 to 601, the last READY), whose findings set its shape: opaque SIV cursors under a key of their own, refused uniformly and padded to 20 digits; reach checked on history and on the snapshot; a streamed scan bounded at 4,000 entries that answers `unavailable` past it; up to three snapshot restarts with a 2 s wait for a rights change, then `unavailable`; a queued snapshot checked again before it is sent; and overflow recovered from the list of let-go ids that were never ranked, never by a snapshot. The architect's round-4 ruling, a recovery position taken as one AUTOINCREMENT id, was wrong and is overturned: snapshot pages are ordered by `(at, id)`, so no single id bounds what has been accounted for, and the desk track's round 5 found two MUSTs from it. Round 6's convergence rule was agreed in advance: after it, only a content leak or a silent loss of a viewable entry reopens review. The architect accepted one cost: after a burst of more than 1,000 hidden entries, a viewable lane A event waits behind the catch-up's placement reads. Main subscribes each roster machine's feed where `rl2.channel` is offered and falls back to history otherwise. It keeps a cache per machine that belongs to one reader (500 entries, 4 MiB, read strictly at load), dedupes on the machine and the entry's id, and orders by `at`, then the machine's journal order, since ids are opaque (the executor's refinement of the architect's `(at, id)`, accepted). A whole read replaces a machine's entries, so an entry a reach change takes out of the member's view leaves it. Machines read `live`, `read` (an older peer, read once), `offline` or `unavailable`, with as-of times. The renderer draws them as their own kind, `to-human`, through an additive main-to-renderer change. #506 took RL2 1.11 and `WIRE_MINOR` 29 first, so M7b renumbered to RL2 1.12 and `WIRE_MINOR` 30 in new commits. Reviews: the task reviewer at each frontier; the desk track's six rounds on every `src/endpoint` and `apps/org-protocol` commit; reviewer-high on the branch with a confidentiality lens, one Minor (the journal's order across history pages), fixed, and no confidentiality defect. Gates: `--gates all --evidence` at 4e35fb15c (run 42344559621, 53 jobs green), which caught one real regression, `library.spec`'s version-9 template lacking the journal the opener creates at any version, fixed in 9bb79f34e; on 9bb79f34e static, unit, `e2e:library`, `e2e:cross-build` and both packaged shards (runs 42357252517 and 42359252532), `e2e:settings` with the to-human entries photographed (run 42346311257, two layout faults the photographs caught fixed before it), and `e2e:discover` (run 42359252532, attempt 3: 9 passed, the spike skipped by design, no flaky line), after four attempts failed in job setup on the mini, before any step, unable to fetch action archives from codeload while the mini's whole network was degraded; CI-opt cleared it by seeding the runner's action-archive cache with all seven setup actions. The PR's head moved after that only in O8's own record. Master had moved meanwhile in O18 M6's staging harness, which imports `apps/org-protocol`, where M7b adds the channel feed, so the merge itself took static, unit and probes (run 42373049418). One packaged 2/2 failure at `render-host.spec.ts:442`, the tracked pre-move flake, passed on rerun; M7b touches no render-host code. Acceptance 16, the owner's week, starts from this landing, and §M7b's live check (the owner's MacBook showing a mini session's to-human message and a devcloud VM session's question in one project's channel) needs a build carrying M7b on each of those machines. M6a is next, cut from M7b's head and opened only after this landing. A layout point of M7a's say rows (a long title cuts the machine chip, where M7b's headers keep it) is on board #505 for the UX return. Residue is in the tech-debt tracker.
