248. **2026-11-07, the architect, under O8, O18 and O10, at the landing of O8 M6a (PR #518, merge d1d0eecc7): each reader's pin, read marks and draft on a remote session are their own, kept by the session's machine; Decision Log 235's second-member precondition gains every enrolled machine on a build with M6a's endpoint reader rule.** Built to O8's §M6a, cut from M7b's head and opened after #514 landed (Decision Log 246). The spine gains `reader_state` and `member_drafts` as `ADDED_TABLES` entries and `HandlerContext.reader`, which only `mawsd`'s front writes, from the endpoint's authorization decision and never from a request. `session.reader.update` (a patch of pinned, last seen and unread, answering a `ReaderState`) is new at `WIRE_MINOR` 31, graded `list`, and the endpoint offers it in place of `session.update`, which stays local and refuses any reader. `session.drafts.get` is graded `view`, since a member only ever reads their own draft (`converse` was rejected: it would make a read a ledgered act), and `session.drafts.set` is a `converse` act kept out of the ledger's stored documents. The architect's rulings, as built: co-owners share the owners' row, and a non-owner writes only their own; every record the endpoint sends a member, on every surface (answers, `session.changed`, snapshots, history pages, remote rows), carries that member's own reader fields or none, each surface proven by a behavioural test over real channels; on an older machine every reader keeps today's in-run fallback, and the rows replace it where offered; a member's own change reaches only that member's other devices, on their checkout feeds. The desk track's four rounds on the endpoint found, and the fixes closed: a stale owner reader at dispatch (the decision is now checked again where the write is dispatched, retried once, then `unavailable`); an unbounded projection queue that ran on after close; an owner's queued record sent after its member lost ownership (now checked at dequeue, the feed ending `behind`); and a reopened checkout feed that never read the record again (the client now re-reads under its member, fenced by a generation and retried on the feed's schedule). The architect's premise that a reopen after `behind` loses nothing was wrong, and O8's Decision Log replaces it. A certificates parity fix the desk track asked for rides in its own commit (65e0532b1), outside the reader work: a certificate set changed under the same head now rechecks every subscription and resumable cursor, as a new head does. The draft path took most of the review. The desk track's reviewer-high on the renderer and reviewer-medium's four passes over the branch found six ways to lose a draft or an attachment (a timed-out save releasing the next one, a clear leaving no revision, a fitted read deleting a withheld attachment, two timeouts with the older act landing last, an edit pushing a stored attachment out of the transport budget, attachments past RL2's 4 MiB taking the text with them) and one reply that overwrote a newer reading. The fixes: a compare-and-set on the writer's stamp, compared for identity and never for order (the architect accepted its same-millisecond hole over a machine-assigned number, which would need a new output or a read after every save); a tombstone the machine stamps on clear; a withheld attachment never deleted by being left out; the text always kept; a bounded resend; a generation fence on replies. The architect asked for one enumerating test over the draft path's events, against the real `DraftStore` and the renderer's queue: it failed 361 of 1,650 orders per reader before the fixes, found one loss of its own, and now runs every order of up to 5 of 7 events (5,094 per reader) clean. O8's retrospective keeps the lesson: write the enumeration first, with every event that changes what crosses the link in its alphabet. No review found a disclosure across readers. Gates: `--gates all --evidence --lane hosted` on the product tree b90a20de5 (run 42395007929, 55 jobs green, no flaky line in any job's log), placed off the mini while `dasd` looped there; e2e:remote's read cases green as well on d7d6e4ffc (run 42386897329). The head after b90a20de5 changes O8's records only, and master's move since, #516 (`machine-counts.py` reading `identity.json`, merge 5fefe80c4, its tracker row closed), shares no file with it. Decision Log 235's second-member precondition gains an item: every enrolled machine runs a build carrying M6a's endpoint reader rule, since an older endpoint sends the owners' reader fields to any device. The home-root rule's edges (the tracker row from PR #503) are re-reviewed at the same point, before a second member joins. M6b is next, cut from master. Residue is in the tech-debt tracker.

CI-opt's run 42411142643 on 6f89f9d11 read the daemon shape after a render at 100,979 KiB (`daemon-idle.spec.ts:354`), 101,170 (`:444`, over in both of its valid readings) and 105,347 (`:474`), all valid by load and over `DAEMON_IDLE_BOUND`, 99,145; plain idle (`:304`) read 96,851, within. The architect's investigation found two parts: about 0.7 MiB of heap from always-loaded IPC zod schemas added since 11-03 (O8 M7a and M7b, O7 M9 and M8 the largest), with no leak and the endpoint not loaded in these scenes; and about 1 to 1.5 MiB from memory pressure on the mini that day, Electron processes' footprints stepping up at zero CPU while their RSS fell, which the load rule cannot see. The architect chose a code change over a re-baseline: the bound's own rule, 1.5 times the detached supervisor on the same tree, now gives about 93,889, below today's bound, so a higher number would restate acceptance 14. The fix, on branch `fix/idle-footprint`, makes the wire fingerprint a build-time constant (about 340 KiB per process) and loads zod's 62 non-English locales on first use through the repository's first pnpm patch (about 1,158 KiB per process), and records, without changing any rule, each sample's CPU ticks, wakeups, pageins and pressure level and each window's swapouts and pageouts. Agreed with CI-opt: once those fields have recorded a few runs, a window in which a measured process's RSS falls by more than 10% is void like one under sustained load, a pressure level of 2 or more a backstop. CI-opt measures the fix from 01:05Z on 11-08. The mini's resident co-tenants (a Chrome open for three days among them) page the measured processes out; what to do about it is the owner's, and asked.

The wire fingerprint is computed when the wire changes, not in each process at its start: `src/shared/ipc/wire-fingerprint.json` holds `fingerprintOf` over the real registry, `wireFingerprint()` returns it, `wire-fingerprint.test.ts` holds the file equal to the live schemas, and `pnpm wire:manifest` rewrites it beside the wire manifest; its value, `659d7538a26c8a3f`, is unchanged, so peers of other builds see the same fingerprint. zod's locales load at their first read: the repository's first pnpm patch, `patches/zod@4.6.5.patch` (recorded in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, where pnpm 11 keeps `patchedDependencies`), makes the `locales` export of zod's CommonJS core, classic and mini entries a non-configurable getter, the mini entry because the MCP SDK requires `zod/v4-mini` itself; English messages are unchanged; `test/zod-locales.test.ts` holds the installed copy to the patch and a packaged check holds the copy inside `app.asar`, so a zod upgrade that drops the patch fails both. Against run 42411142643's readings that would bring `:354` and `:444` within the bound and leave `:474` over, if the footprint falls as far as the heap; the measured runs decide, and if `:474`'s engine host stays about 2.7 MiB above the 11-03 lows the architect asks CI-opt for an A/B against c6b072d7b. The patched dependency means a machine's next build takes the fix only through `pnpm install`, which every swap path runs. 252. **2026-11-08, the architect, under O8, O18 and O10, at the landing of the channel-reach fix (PR #524, merge 2600f3732): a member reads any project's channel on another machine, and each entry is still cut to the reader's view of its session; O8 M6b's shared staging run passed, three reader-side defects it showed are board #525 and join the second-member precondition, and its sign-in pitfall is corrected.** The fix as built overturns what Decision Log 251 said it would be. The endpoint no longer gates a channel feed or `channel.history` on the project at all: any project id is admitted, and each entry's rank against the reader's view of its session is the only gate. The worker's evidence, which the architect accepted: an id the machine doesn't know has no entries, so refusing it acts only as an oracle; main treats a refusal as final, so a project the machine learned later would stay refused; and the index and the journal are written asynchronously, so a 'machine knows it' check races them. Feeds per channel stay bounded by `MAX_SUBS` (64). `orgProject`, the feed's and history's `reaches`, the `removed` ending and the authorize refusal are gone; the one reader of the head's `projects` left is RL10's org directory (doperpowers #181, MAWS #521). channel-feed's tests fail 11 of 15 on the unfixed code and pass 15 of 15 fixed, and the scene's three M7b cases fail unfixed and pass. reviewer-medium, with a confidentiality lens, found nothing material, and the desk track found it READY (657 and 658) after one correction to its header. That is org-internal metadata, not content; remote-derived ids are guessable, and M7b already scanned the same way. A per-member viewable index or constant-work paging would close it, weighed once orgs have more than one person. 253. **2026-11-08, the architect, under O9 and the desk track, at the landing of PR #527 (merge fe15386a0): MAWS reads every task notification a transcript row carries, and the engine's queued notices, so a lane A session's tasks stop reading as running for good.** The defect was in two readings of DX4's jobs contract, which MAWS shares with sminos. First, the engine joins the notifications it delivers at once into one row (from 2.1.257), each `<task-notification>` block after a blank line. MAWS read only the first block and searched elements over the whole text, so the row's other tasks read as running for good, and a monitor's statusless event could take a later block's status. Every joined row the desk track's survey found (26) is in the preamble form of a subagent's transcript, which backfill did not read at all. Second, the engine enqueues every notification in the main loop's transcript as a `queue-operation` record without a uuid before handing it to its loop, and one for an agent that handed back, or for a session whose process ended first, is never handed over, so the enqueue is the only word that the task ended (sminos 1.40.6, DX4 case 18). The contract now reads: each block is one notification, in the plain row, the `queued_command` prompt and the `queue-operation` enqueue whose content starts with a block, and a `remove`, a `dequeue` or an enqueue of other content is not read; a monitor's expiry is judged by the folding loop's own monitors, as `sminos_jobs.py` 1.40.6 has it. Backfill gives one outcome per block, and reads the preamble form (`[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION - NOT USER INPUT]`, a blank line, the blocks) only on a row the engine marked `origin.kind: task-notification`. Those rows' origin moves from null to `task-notification` and their delivery class from tail to interleave. They were `isMeta` rows and were never drawn, so what changes on screen is that the nested agent such a row names now finishes in a drill-in, where it read started for good. The fake machine's `LoopIndex` reads the same three forms, and `loopJobs` folds the main loop's words (`also`) only while the loop's own rows leave a task running, as sminos 400632ce does. The queued-notice rule holds in the contract's readers alone, the lane A ledger and the fake machine: no MAWS view shows a subagent's own shell or monitor as running. Both vendored copies of the vectors are doperpowers `sminos/tests/dx4-jobs.json` blob a414fd58d, which sminos merged unchanged as b0db15af (doperpowers #478). Reviews: the desk track found each fake-machine commit READY (dce803f6f, 39b8ceff6, fc48476a5 and the README's 70114acf1, its 672, 675 and 677), and sminos's own reviewer-medium found the fold order that fc48476a5 mirrors. reviewer-medium, on the MAWS-side readers (`src/services/lane-a/jobs.ts` and `src/engine-host/fs/backfill.ts`) and their consumers, found nothing material. Gates: `--gates all --lane hosted` on 6dcbb774a (run 42453368238) had 43 jobs green and 2 placeholders with no flaky line on its first attempt; 10 groups lost setup to GitHub's release-download 504s before any test ran, and `e2e:connectors-oauth`, cancelled at its job timeout on the mini's slow uplink, passed on the second attempt. The head after it differs only in the PROVENANCE and README. On the merged head 6ba25abef, which takes master's #534 (Decision Log 252) cleanly, the 10 groups passed (run 42474104352), and static, unit, `e2e:desk-web` and `e2e:desk-web-hub` passed (run 42474148718), the last two because master's #524 reaches them as this change does; neither run had a flaky line. Master's moves after it are records, #535, #536 and #537, none in this change's files, so those gates serve. Three CI-opt and O8 pulls landed as their merge commits without records. #535 (merge 4f383a654, CI only, run 42474110066) lets a gate's hosted-eligible e2e groups overflow onto the mini when they would start sooner there, only on a lane the mini publishes as free in `MAC_MINI_LANES`, and a missing, stale, future or unreadable publication holds both lanes. #536 (merge cda04be85, test only, run 42475987888) makes O8 M6b's `ShareSheet.test.tsx` await the sheet's render after each policy reply at seven sites; one case queried a radio before the sheet drew the read, which failed unit 4/4 on two runs on loaded runners. #537 (merge de59012ad, CI only, run 42477848701) hands curl #534's API headers through a here-string rather than a pipe, so a curl that exits without reading stdin, as `mawsd-pack.test.mjs`'s fake does, can no longer fail the fallback through printf's SIGPIPE; that test failed static once on O8's M4b run 42475844068. CI-opt's #533, which had Linux static and unit skip Electron's binary, was closed unlanded: a gate composing it with #535 showed that `src/main/lane-b/remote-port.test.ts` reaches Electron's path, so unit's result would have depended on what the cache restored, and #534's fallback already covers the outage it was for. Of the one swap's three fixes, #524 and this one have landed, and #525's (PR #530) is in its final gate. Residue: the desk's fake machine replays only rows with a uuid, so its live path never reads a queue record; that is a tracker row for the desk track.
254. **2026-11-08, the architect, under O8 and the desk track, at the landing of the desk track's PR #538 (merge d76c27400): an admin's removal of a machine's hub row can be bound to the machine's key, so a drop that settles late never removes a machine enrolled anew under the same name.** O8 M4b's drop deletes the machine's hub row when the signer settles the drop, which can be well after it was signed. Its Task 3 review found that `DELETE /api/machines/<name>` revokes whatever holds the name when it lands, so another root's new enrollment of that name in between would lose its row and its live credential. O8's guard against the app's held head (its D23) narrows that but is check-then-act against a head that can be older than the hub. The route now takes `?ref=<MachineRef>&pub=<key>`: the Registry removes the row only while its enrollment's posted key is that ref and pub, checked in `revokeMachine`'s first synchronous step, before the fence at the Machine object. A row whose enrollment posted another key, or none, answers `200 {revoked}` and is left alone, as an enrollment mismatch already answers. Half the pair or a bad shape answers `400 bad-shape`, and with neither the route is the CLI's removal as before. The key, not the enrollment id, names the machine: the state never makes a dropped key current again, `keepMachineKey` posts no key another ref holds or that is retired or revoked, and an enrollment keeps its first key. A certificate machine posts no key, and M4b refuses its drop. reviewer-medium found the rolling deploy's gap: the Durable Objects update minutes after the Worker, and an older Registry would ignore an extra argument and remove whatever holds the name. So the key-bound form is its own method, `revokeKeyed`, which an older Registry lacks, and the Worker answers `503 hub-updating`, failing closed; a delta review found nothing. Gates: static, unit, `e2e:desk-web` and `e2e:desk-web-hub` on the head (run 42479261252), and `e2e:remote` and `e2e:remote-latency-check` on the merge (run 42480041736), which run desk-hub's local runner. M4b's app sends the pair and reads `503 hub-updating` as retryable and `404 unknown-machine` as gone. Until a hub runs this change it ignores the pair and answers the same, so D23 is the only guard there. The staging deploy is the desk track's; the production hub deploy is the owner's, with M4b's landing.
255. **2026-11-08, the architect, under O8, at the landing of PR #530 (merge 3527b7248, board #525): a reader of another member's shared session sees what their grade lets them do, whose words they are reading, and why a send did not go.** The owner found three defects in O8 M6b's real-member sitting on staging, as the second member reading sessions the harness member shared (Decision Log 252). At Can message the pane read "Reading the session from its machine" for good; the owner's prompts read "Your message" to the reader; and at View the composer was enabled and a send did nothing visible, though nothing reached the machine. Now, at Can message the pane says the reader's agents can message the session and the reader can't read it, and names whom to ask for View, without attempting a read. A prompt is labelled "you" only when the reader owns the session or this app sent it, and otherwise with its owners' names ("its owner" when they are unknown), on lane A and lane B alike. Below Converse, once the grade is learnt, the composer is disabled with a line naming the grade needed and whom to ask, and a refused send says "Not sent:" with its refusal. Lane A's pane decides from the reader's view in the renderer; lane B answers `READ_REFUSED` only when the org's rows list the session as another member's, so the owner's own sessions are never refused, and the 0.5, 1, 2 and 4 s ladder covers the window before the grade is known. The grade is read again on reopen, on the session's revision or `policyRev`, and on a member change. Submit's `records.has` gate is fixed. What the endpoint admits at each grade is unchanged and was proven live in the sitting. Reviews: reviewer-medium found four P2s, fixed in 9a53e0441; its review of the fix wave closed its one finding and named three label-timing edges (a member switch, a queued prompt whose pane closed, a queue event before its reply), which converged with no fix and went to board #526, the reader's rank and each prompt's author on the wire, which starts after O8 M4b. Until then, below Converse the composer stays enabled until the first refused send, and three author labels can be wrong (a Converse sharee's prompts read as the owner's to a reader, the reader's own sends after a reload read as the owner's, and the owner sees a Converse sharee's prompts as theirs), all in #526. 258. **2026-11-08, the architect, under O8, at the landing of O8 M4b (PR #539, merge 0a1fa8e83): the Org page's Machines group is no longer read-only. An admin whose Mac may sign enrolls or re-enrolls a machine, changes its owners or drops it behind one Touch ID, and an owner, or an admin for an `'org'` machine, grants members rights on it, each grant delivered only once the machine has seen the signing head, and each verdict final.** Enrollment shows the code once with its expiry, polls for the machine's key (MAC-checked, frozen at its first good answer) and signs `machine.add` or `machine.replace`; the sheet owns exactly the run its start's answer names by `generation`, and a cancel names that run or, with null, only the sender's own start. A drop deletes the machine's hub row when the drop settles, bound to the dropped key (#538, Decision Log 254), and never while the held head lists the name again. Each row lists the members holding a grant, read from the machine's own ledger through `endpoint.grants`, marks pending, refused and missing grants, and shows a failed read's reason with Retry; Re-grant offers what this Mac issued that the ledger no longer holds. The architect's rulings, recorded in O8's Decision Log: a grant to another machine goes push, then grant. The app pushes its held head through M3's planner, coalesced per ref, and sends the grant only when the machine answers at or past the signing head on the app's current line, showing "Bringing <machine> up to date…" meanwhile; when the machine's ledger lacks the member, the app pushes and reads again before signing. A grant to this Mac's own machine goes through the local door at once, on the grant's own signature verified against the held head with no act step-up, which the desk track accepted as an amendment to M6b's rule (desk 683). Every verdict is final: a write-ahead `sending` precedes each act; outcomes belong to the issued record across sign-out and close, since grants.json is per account and a profile cannot switch members; a failed outcome write is held and retried, never resent; and an entry left `sending` across a restart reads outcome-unknown and asks the person to sign again. Grants don't lapse with their signer's standing, by design (RL §6.3, RL DL24): a machine verifies a grant once against the head it holds, so a demotion, or an owner change with no key revocation, leaves that person's grants in force. The branch review's loop was bounded: one re-review round per fix wave on its own diff, after which only findings that break finality, authority or secrets, or cause a silent wrong action, were fixed, and the rest went to residue or the UX ticket. Reviews: each task at its frontier; the desk track on every `apps/` and `src/endpoint` hunk (desk 674, 683 and 684, and the org-protocol move's differential over 14 grant vectors); reviewer-high (production code, security lens) and reviewer-medium (renderer and tests) on the branch, both closed after one re-review round. Gates, on 01450ac1c (tree cd2e48b63; the head 2971fb93a adds only O8's spec and report): `--gates all` run 42526195244, 56 jobs and no flaky lines, `e2e:desk-web` red once on `command.spec.ts:66`, the known A2 rename-locator flake, which M4b does not reach, and green on its rerun; `e2e:desk-web-hub` and `e2e:remote-latency-check` run 42526210394, green; `org-page.local.test.ts` 12 of 12 on three CI attempts. Master had moved only in P5's records since the branch's merge of d7586dada, so the head gate serves the merge. The architect fixes the A2 locator in a test-only PR. Live use: mawsd's `endpoint.local.grants` is at `WIRE_MINOR` 33, so the Org page's rights need the next swap of all three machines, and a drop's key-bound delete needs a production hub carrying #538, the owner's deploy; until then the app's guard against its held head is the only protection for a drop's hub row. Residue: seven tracker rows; the 14 copy lines that name a condition without a next step, and the no-recipients sheet's hidden read failure, go to board 540, deferred with the paused UX wave. O8's Outcomes record M7b's live check (Decision Log 257). M4c is next.

260. **2026-11-09, the architect, under O8, at the landing of O8 M4c (PR #543, merge e57f69c63): the Org page's Delegations group. While this Mac may sign, an admin creates a delegation behind one Touch ID, renews or drops one, revokes stray hub keys, and sets where the provisioning code goes; the code goes from the hub's mint into Secret Manager on a `gcloud` child's stdin and is never shown, copied, written to disk, logged or sent to the renderer.** A create mints the hub's key, makes the delegation's key pair in main, signs the delegation into the pending slot unsent, writes the code and confirms it with `describe`, and only then publishes; a renew's or drop's hub half follows from the signed head with no stored continuation, and Retry calls only the route. `prefixProblem` and `mintDisagrees` move into `org-protocol`, whose `successorProblems` gains directional prefix codes; the hub's acceptance is unchanged, and the desk track found the move correct (desk 701). The architect's rulings, recorded in O8's Decision Log: the code's destination lives in main's own `<profile>/provisioning-secret.json` (version 1, a damaged file never overwritten), not in machine-settings.json, which by O7 M9's rule gains no property, overturning the refresh plan's item 13; it has a Reset (`org.provisioningSecret.reset`, confirmed), safe because the file names a destination and holds no authority state. `gcloud` runs with HTTP logging and file logging off, a `versions add` whose answer is lost reads may-have-succeeded, and hub error codes pass through an allowlist with a drift test against desk-hub's exported unions. A failed sign names `secret-store` or `unsent`; strays show their key's age, under 15 minutes with a stronger warning; a dry composition check runs before the mint; sign-out never calls the hub. One custody owner per run: the holder drops on every exit and an abort kills `gcloud`; the running create's reservation (its prefix held from 60 seconds before the mint to the run's end) is excluded from strays and revokes; hub halves are bound to the sign-in epoch. Answers carry the run's generation, the delegation id and the held hash, and a status revision, since a status push and an invoke's answer travel on separate Electron pipes; a row discards only its own slot and latches a published or discarded answer by hash until the revision moves; publication is judged by positive provenance. From the branch review: a mint disagreement names fields and never the hub's values, since the token could otherwise reach the renderer; a marker write that commits and then throws is read back; a lost 2xx mint body reads may-have-minted; a Discard during a running create is recorded before the run ends; a drop answering stale says the delegation may have certified a machine since it was opened and asks for a new preview. Reviews: each task at its frontier, with fix waves; the desk track on the `org-protocol` move; reviewer-high (security lens) and reviewer-medium on the branch, one fix wave (eight findings) and one re-review round, after which the loop bound of Decision Log 258 applied. Gates, on 4d2b45261 (tree 211a00287, master bdd178dd8 merged in; the head a72346675 adds only O8's spec and report): `--gates all --evidence` run 42594055817, attempt 1 with 53 green and two red, `unit:darwin` on the `sessions.test.ts` 4,000-upsert CPU bound (599 ms against 500) and `e2e:connectors` on the mini's overflow (`:168` and `:272` timing out before main reached a supervisor, `:304` the known flake), under a mini 15-minute load of about 19, both green on attempt 2 with no flaky line; attempt 1's flaky passes were `supervisor.spec.ts:847` (row 29) and `:1442`, case (m), O2 M7's idle release, whose first try waited past 2 s for the engine host's arm frame under that load, a first sighting, in code M4c does not reach; `e2e:desk-web-hub` and `e2e:remote-latency-check` run 42609058582, green; `org-page.local.test.ts` 15 of 15 on three CI attempts (run 42584197185). Both runs' trees equal the gated commit's. Live use: no hub deploy is needed, since the renew route (#493) runs on staging and on production's hub@14ec1177, and `WIRE_MINOR` stays 33; the Org page's Delegations need the next swap of the machines' apps to this master. The Worker bundle changes by the moved `org-protocol` code, so the desk track deploys this master to staging before any production deploy. Board 512 (a CLI `delegation extend`) stays open until the production delegation's first renewal shows whether the Org page's renew replaces it. Residue: one tracker row, the hub not announcing new certificates. M5b, Add owner, is next.

264. **2026-11-10, the architect, at the landing of PR #549 (merge 3d8ce1403, deploy tooling only; board 547, the desk track's): `deployment admin`'s waiting verbs sign on the head as it stands when they sign, a `409` is never reported as a fork, and a refused confirmation says what it read. The two facts O8's record left pending at Decision Log 263 are filled (master 1a0ce4c5f).** The change serves `staging-run.sh` and `production-run.sh` alike, since the code is shared; it follows the two tooling faults of M8's sitting (Decision Log 263, deviations a and c). A verb that waits for a confirmation re-reads the head before it signs; when the head moved it signs the same change on the new head with every confirmed object pinned (the device entry and its keys, the root, the delegation, the machine), and refuses "changed while waiting" when one of them changed; enroll's guards run inside the edit. A `409` `not-head` or `rollback` is checked against the verified chain: an adopted proposal settles, and a superseded one is dropped with the anchor advanced in the same write. Every read that sees a newer head writes it to the anchor before any decision, the second review round's finding, so a device revoked while a verb waits cannot be replayed into signing. A confirmation that reads neither an answer nor an empty line writes what it read to the terminal in hex; standard error and the logs get the count and the kind alone, the first round's finding, since a pasted key or password would otherwise reach the runner's logs. Reviews: three rounds at reviewer-medium with a security lens, the third with no finding. Gate: `static,unit` hosted, run 42771026070, on 00c76691c with the run's tree equal to the head's; desk-hub's `admin.test.ts` (47 tests) and `admin-verbs.test.ts` (40) ran in it. No Worker or app change, so no hub deploy. On production the verbs no longer act, the deployment admin's device being gone since version 24; the change matters for staging and for any later deployment admin. The cause of the refused `y` at M8 stays unknown; a repeat would now show its bytes. O8's pending facts, in O8's spec and report: `dc-linux171-1e68` adopted version 24 at 05:38:54.091Z by its `mawsd.log`, read by the desk track at 06:12:31Z (`orgHighWater` 24, `org-listed`), so all three machines are on it; the owner's fresh look at acceptance item 7, a project's Channel tab across machines, was not recorded at the sitting, the item stands on Decision Log 257, and O15 re-checks it. The owner's project registry design for board 521 is drafted on the branch `registry-design` and awaits the owner's approval; it gets its own entry then.
265. **2026-11-10, the owner and the architect: the owner approved the org's project registry, the design for board 521 (`docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-11-10-project-registry-design.md`, revision 6, merged at 32f258b53). It amends OX3 and is built as a follow-up outside P5's acceptance.** The owner's proposal at M8's sitting (Decision Log 263) and their answers since: every Mac lists the same projects; a `.smns` in a folder declares it; the command is `maws project`; and, to the design as presented, "Approve" and "Spec now, build beside O15": the execution spec is written now, the desk track starts its half at once, and the app-side changes reach master only after O15 has fixed the build P5 is verified on. The shape: each machine signs one record of its own project folders, ordered by a revision counter; the hub keeps the newest of each machine and hands the records to devices and machines; every reader verifies. A project is one id, sminos's; a declared folder that names no project gets an id minted by its machine; a link is a machine's stored statement that a folder is in another project; stored statements beat the folder's files and change only by an act. One rule decides a directory's project in sminos and in MAWS, pinned by shared vectors. Rejected: projects in the signed head, which would need the root's signature per folder; a table in the hub that devices edit; a project record signed by a member's device, deferred. What changes in this document: OX3's org project is the registry's, grouped by its id, and its checkouts are declared locations, read from the machines' records for machines reached over the network; Decision Log 27's row key becomes the folder's label on its machine, with a row's layout key assigned once per device; the head's `projects` list stays unwritten and reserved. The remote layer's account of what the hub reads (RL §5.9) gains declared paths and observed remote keys, which the owner was shown as a choice. The endpoint's org directory is built from the records, which answers board 521's three options and closes the desk board's 181. Reviews before the approval: the desk track read the desk-side sections three times, and a critic debated it in four rounds, each narrower, the last leaving notes for the spec; the design's own Decision Log 3 to 8 records what each changed, the largest being that the first draft's walk would have moved several hundred sessions on the owner's MacBook into one project after a single chat in the home directory. Route: doperpowers:execspec on two tracks, the desk track's (the record, the hub, sminos) and the architect's (the index and endpoint, main, the page, the command, the live check).
266. **2026-11-10, the architect, under O15, at its start and the landing of its execution document (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p5-o15-recomposition.md`, docs only): O15 is planned as five milestones, and the owner answered its two questions: when `mawsd` cannot start MAWS opens a repair screen and runs nothing of the machine's inside the app, and the embedded root's deletion is built now and reaches the three machines before the verdict.** Two research inventories from master a839e6bb2 are committed beside the document. The first finds the embedded root live: `src/main/app.ts` composes the services' library in main for a saved `supervisor.shape: embedded`, for `MAWS_HOST_MODE=detached` or `utility`, and, unasked, whenever the main profile's LaunchAgent is absent, not loaded, refused or silent (`planShape` and `prepareShape` in `src/main/hosts/daemon.ts`); what O3-D deleted was O6's stand-in server, not this root. The second splits the 16 acceptance items into 67 claims: 31 have evidence seen on production machines, 3 of them in full (4g, 4j and the admin device's end), and 36 have none (11 a gate or a bench only, 10 staging only, 15 neither). The largest gaps: item 7 was never run, the lid and Wi-Fi tests skipped at O7 M7 (Decision Log 211); the remote bench has never been valid on a real network (the tracker's row of 2026-11-05 on the live remote bench); item 14 has no reading on the MacBook (deferred at Decision Logs 168, 173 and 180); a spawn on the VM failed at O7 M7 and M10 and was not re-checked; remote artifacts were never used live. The architect's decisions under those answers, each with its reason in the document: the repair screen is a window with a page and a preload of its own, not a native alert; the main profile's machine runs under its unit or not at all, so a development build with no unit shows the same window and names a new `pnpm dev:scratch`; the client settings version does not move, since a build that meets a newer version reads defaults and a rolled-back machine would lose its settings, so `supervisor.shape` and `keepRunningDefault` stay in the schema as retired fields, `shape` always read and stored as `daemon`; `MAWS_HOST_MODE` is retired; the embedded sentinels and baselines leave the gate (`e2e:c7-utility`, `e2e:supervisor`, `e2e:supervisor:packaged`, `e2e:local-latency`, `e2e:daemon-idle-baseline`, `e2e:daemon-idle-aba`, `e2e:keep-quit-live`) with no measured limit changed, the idle bound's constants staying as Decision Log 128 set them; main keeps its answers for an older `mawsd` without `machine.render` or `machine.delivery`, which are OX1's and not the embedded shape, their retirement a tracker row at O15's record; `detached` stays as the transport of `mawsd` and of the app's relay links. M1 takes the suites off the embedded shape and M2 deletes it and builds the window, on one branch by a plan executor. M3 gates the resulting master, has the owner deploy production's hub (which brings PR #538) and swaps the three machines with the owner's approval, the swap staged as the live check of items 1 and 13. M4 checks every claim on that build by gate, by the swap, live through an agent's use of the apps, on staging, by the real-network bench, or by the owner's hands; a failed claim is fixed or put to the owner as a ruling, and the architect waives nothing. M5 writes Outcomes and asks the verdict from 2026-11-14. A finding for the risk register: both of a native Mac's signing keys, the channel key and the step-up key that has been production's only root since Decision Log 263, are plaintext P-256 private JWKs in `<userData>/device/keys.json` (directory 0700, file 0600), and Touch ID is a step in the app's flow that binds nothing to the key, so the risk row "Production's org root is a software key until O8" is wrong about its end; M5 corrects it and files a tracker row, and Secure Enclave keys stay deferred with the Developer ID. The owner then answered three questions on the closing run: an agent drives the live checks on the MacBook with the owner giving Touch ID, one 45-minute quiet window serves item 14's reading and the real-network bench, and item 7's hour asleep is run in full. 267. **2026-11-10, the architect, under O15, at the landing of its document's amendment (docs only): O15 gains a milestone, M2b, between the deletion and the final build, because a read of what the closing run can observe found acceptance 7's sentence on wake not delivered by the code's design; the final build and the three machines' swap move about a day, to 2026-11-12 or 11-13, and the date from which the verdict is asked does not move.** Before fixing the final build the architect had a research agent read, at master e2ade600c, whose app code equals the machines' cf30dafdd, what each of the 67 claims can be observed with on that build (the report is kept outside the repository with O15's evidence). Its first finding is on acceptance 7, "Within 1 s of the network being reachable after wake, every subscription resumes from its cursor". Nothing in the app reacts to the network's return: the only near trigger is a 2 s poll of the interface addresses, which fires only when an address changes. A link whose channel died during the sleep learns it at a failed write or a missed ping and then follows its backoff, up to 5 s between attempts and 30 s after two minutes; it reuses a cached relay socket that may be dead; the org feed's wake probe does nothing while a socket object exists and the feed has no liveness check, so a half-open feed can stay dead. Main's log lines carry no time and go only to stdout, so the time to a resume could not have been read on the day either. The gate's tens of milliseconds (`e2e:remote-latency`) measure a probe fired at the instant of the restore with every old socket already destroyed, and a real wake provides neither. The architect ruled the fix into O15 before the final build and did not spend the owner's hour asleep on a known failure: the owner's answer of Decision Log 266 puts the verification on one final build, and a fix after it would move that build anyway. The owner was told the same hour. M2b as designed (the document's sections D to K and its Decision Logs 6 and 8 to 11): one module owns the wake notice and reports the sleep's length; a sleep of 10 s or more, twice the endpoint's shortest ping period, drops every socket from before it at once, since a dead connection that is only read from reports nothing and resuming from a cursor is the designed path; for 60 s after a wake or an address change the app learns that a path is reachable from bare TCP connects every 100 ms, which cost the endpoint no handshake token and the hub no Access check, registry read or snapshot, and each link runs its direct and its relay carrier as two lanes, each attempting when its own path answers, the first completed handshake adopted; a link that has been 10 s in its lanes with no channel lets a running attempt finish, starts no more for a path that has already answered and then runs today's attempts alone, the two never at once, while a carrier whose path has not answered yet is still waited for and gets its lane the moment it does; outside a window a link behaves as today. The org feed and each project socket take the same drop, reopen with one open in flight, and are checked for life at all times by a WebSocket protocol ping. Main writes every line with its time to `<profile>/logs/main.log`, which closes the tracker's row on the main process's log, and the lines the closing run reads for a resume exist there and in `mawsd.log`. The 1 s is claimed with its six conditions stated: a handshake with no catch-up page; at most six subscriptions on the link; round trips, and the endpoint's own work between a `sub` and its `subbed`, inside the stated budget; the adopted lane's first attempt succeeding; two tokens in the direct listener's bucket where the direct lane is adopted; and at most 4 KiB written on the new channel ahead of the subscriptions, since the link sends its pending acts and its queries about older ones first. None is about when the network returns inside the 60 s. A wake outside them is recorded with the condition it missed, the hour is run once more, and a second such wake goes to the owner. A new case in the measured group resembles a wake: the old sockets stay open and silent, the wake is injected, the path returns at a time the app is not told, the cursors have really left the ring, and the app's own log is read. Measured for the design: the hub's own time on a WebSocket upgrade is 220 to 240 ms on staging, most of it the Access check and the registry round trip every request pays (the desk track; 300 ms is allowed, the largest share of the relay carrier's second); the hub's runtime answers a protocol ping in 8 to 64 ms without waking the object (the desk track, staging); the endpoint's work to resume a session stream whose cursor the ring has evicted is a read of the launch's journal from its first row, about 35 ms for each 10,000 rows before the cursor, 16 ms at 10,000 rows, 157 ms at 50,000, 670 ms at 200,000 and 1,714 ms at 500,000 (a one-file bench at master caaef871c on the MacBook under load, warm file cache), against 0.07 ms while the ring holds the cursor; the largest journal on the MacBook is 23.5 MiB, about 55,000 rows, and on the mini 36.6 MiB, about 89,000 rows (mini-ops), so about 0.2 s and 0.3 s of that work today. The design that would bound that work is checkpoints in the session stream (the document's section L). The owner is asked whether it is built in M2b or after the verdict; the question is open at this entry, and without the ruling it is not built and the limit is recorded. From the same read: a refused or silent start of the unit is one launch outcome with four cases, built by M2 (the document's section A), and macOS's Login Items was not tested on a machine, the rule being written so that it does not depend on the answer; a peer message in the session pane shows its sender's owners under Decision Log 111's rule, read from sminos's frame with no wire change; `host.log` says when a launch's ring first evicted and when a read was served from the journal, so that 2c's record can say where a replay came from; `artifact_export`'s description no longer says it needs the app; a failed hub connection of `mawsd` logs its error code and addresses, for the 71 s of Decision Log 262. M4's rows are amended where the read found a claim unobservable or misdescribed: 7e is read from the app's log against an outside probe of the network, 11c is restated under Decision Log 111, 14c is read before 14a and 14b, 15 names the tailnet listener's port, and item 10 is a real-model lane B session through production's unit on `dc-linux171-1e68` with no app subscribed. Left out of M2b and owed as tracker rows at M5: `mawsd`'s own hub link after a wake, a `missing` answer still not drawn, the direct race's losers charged by the listener and not by the app's bucket, the project socket's wake path with no case in the built app, the hub's 190 ms per request, and an alias in sminos's frame that can imitate the owner label. Verification: the amendment went to the adversarial reviewer in three rounds, each answered rework, and the architect's own read between the second and the third found two more defects. Round 1: a probe's answer could hold the working carrier behind a hung one, the handshake's catch-up redials were dropped, and the endpoint's work before `subbed` was unnamed and unbounded; taken as two lanes that race, catch-up kept as it is, and that work measured and stated. Round 2: the claim's conditions were not stated, and today's attempts running beside the lanes put two diallers on one token bucket with no rule for which channel wins; taken as the stated conditions and as three modes for a link in which the lanes and today's loop never run at once. The architect's read: the fallback to today's loop cut a lane attempt begun in its last second, and ended the lanes for a path that had not yet returned. Round 3: that fix stopped looking for the other carrier while an attempt ran out, and the subscriptions are written behind any act a new channel resends; taken as a lane that keeps waiting for its first answer and as the sixth condition. The reviewer's last verdict was to review the implementation next and not the specification again; asked only whether its two cases were closed, it found the first closed and the second open for many small queries, which the sixth condition now covers. Three of the four texts of the fallback were the architect's own and were wrong, and the document's Decision Logs 8 to 11 keep each with what replaced it. State at this entry: M1 is built and reviewed on its branch and its full gate is running; M2 starts from this amendment's section A; M2b is built on its own branch by a second plan executor. Landed without records since Decision Log 266, all CI-opt's but the third: PR #550 (merge e2ade600c), the static gate's checks as parallel jobs on hosted Linux; PR #551 (merge e8be3867e), a docs-only tree runs a short checked gate in place of static; PR #553 (merge 7f3694f10, test only), a unit case of `org-session.test.ts` that read the root anchor while its write was in flight and reddened #550's first gate, no product defect; PR #554 (merge 14a2dd1ea), `scripts/ci-gate.sh` waits out an exhausted API budget, polls a run conditionally and keeps its ref when it ends with no verdict, after the account's shared budget ran out at 11:02Z and a landing gate gave up on a run that was green. The registry's M1 and M2 are on master (82f706120 and caaef871c) and its M3 is merged in doperpowers (sminos 1.42.0); they are recorded in its own document, whose Progress lines for M2 and M3 land with this entry.
268. **2026-11-10, the architect, under O15, at the landing of M1 and M2 (PR #562, merge 10e30f17f): the app composes none of the machine's services. No setting, environment variable or failure runs them inside the app, and a launch ends on `mawsd` or on the repair window, the owner's answer of Decision Log 266.** M1 took the embedded shape out of every suite: the scripts `e2e:c7-utility`, `e2e:supervisor`, `e2e:supervisor:packaged`, `e2e:local-latency`, `e2e:daemon-idle-baseline`, `e2e:daemon-idle-aba` and `e2e:keep-quit-live` are deleted, every case both shapes had runs on `mawsd`, and the builder's report (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p5-o15-report.md`) lists each deleted case with what it pinned and where that is covered now. Two cases the first draft could no longer read end to end were ported to `mawsd` by the architect's ruling: a kill -9 of an engine host holding a session nothing can adopt, and a relaunched app reading an idle release. M2 removed main's composition root: main opens no spine data, starts no spine, takes no `spine.lock` and owns no engine host or pty host; a boundary rule and a bundle guard assert that main's graph holds none of `spine-root.ts`, `entry.ts` and `checkouts-root.ts`. `resolveLaunch` answers one launch outcome; a packaged start that ends without a welcome is classified by the document's four cases, and main then opens the repair window, which says why, offers the repair, retries one at a time and continues into the app in the same process, and quits MAWS when closed. Nothing on the way to that window throws past it. `supervisor.shape` reads and is stored as `daemon` with no settings version bump, Settings loses the shape switch and the quit default, `MAWS_HOST_MODE` is read by nothing and logs once that it is retired, and `host.status` keeps every schema member, so the wire does not change and `WIRE_MINOR` stays 33. Acceptance 3's proof is cross-build's scene (embedded profile, mawsd): the last embedded build, 63443a3bd, makes a session, an artifact, a draft and a two-tab layout, and this build opens that profile on `mawsd` and reads each, with no index rebuild. The ack bound: M1's port of `e2e:artifacts-perf` to `mawsd` read a keystroke-to-durable-ack p95 of 10.6 ms against the 10 ms bound in a valid window (run 42837591610), with the utility shape at 9.5 ms the same day; the architect loosened nothing and took it to the owner, who answered "Re-derive it: 13 ms (Recommended)". The bound is 13 ms, and a measured window with a 1-minute load of 5 or more at either end is void and says so. The verdict on the landed tree's measured code, run 42867217013: first sync 140.8 ms of 150, ack p95 12.0 ms of 13 at loads 2.94 and 2.33, and no acknowledged edit lost over three kills. The margins are 9 ms and 1 ms. The tracker row the branch adds gives the path's stage timings, in which the relay through main and `mawsd` is about 0.85 ms and nearly all of that is four socket hops and main's loop, and names two small gains in the engine host that were left out of this build. One measured run before it (42857861414) was void by load, started by the architect before the mini had settled; since then a measured run starts only on the mini coordinator's go. Review and gates. The branch was reviewed at each task and whole at reviewer-high, as its document named; before it landed the architect also ran a review panel on it, six reviewers under five lenses and one verifier, because the registry branch's panel had found on the same day what single reviewers had passed. It confirmed three findings, all fixed on the branch at aa10461f8 with a test that failed first: reopening MAWS from the Dock or Finder while the repair window is up now retries, as a second launch does, since macOS sends a running app `activate` and starts no second process; a stale `mawsd.lock` whose pid another process holds is no longer read as a live `mawsd`, which had hidden the Login Items action after a restart of the Mac; and a ported test asserts again that a new session's mode comes from the captured defaults. Class 1a on aa10461f8 (run 42902397703) and the landing gate on the merge's tree (run 42907474173). Every Mac group with the packaged jobs on 372cd6dd6 (run 42871682868): 63 jobs green and one red, `e2e:supervisor-daemon`, whose case (h) read `1 busy terminal` where it expects two, at a 1-minute load of 10 to 15 on the mini's overflow; that is the tracker row's signature of Decision Log 262, its fourth sighting, on a count the branch does not change, and the group alone was green on f4dd650ad (run 42899608186) and again on aa10461f8 with the repair cases (run 42904757874). `e2e:cross-build` against the peer 63443a3bd on f4dd650ad (run 42897731007); its first Mac run had failed (run 42869002645) because the new scene never sent its turn, a defect of the scene and not of the build, fixed in one line. The builder had chained three gates in one command and that red was read two hours late, so every gate's end is now reported by itself and a new scene runs alone on a Mac before a final gate. Between 372cd6dd6 and aa10461f8 the code changes are that one scene line and the three fixes. Landed without records since Decision Log 267: PR #555 (merge a2624903f), the live bench's clock voids on exchanges that cannot share one offset, with bounded waits and the owner's sheets in the operator's words; PR #556 (merge f62734932), another machine's lane B record with no digest reads its listing's state, so such a row no longer reads Idle while it runs; PR #557 (merge 37f68b4db), PR #558 (merge 67e104ba4) and PR #560 (merge 0195cdee4), the desk track's deploy tooling for the closing run's rows, the RL10 fixture's hello with locations, and two fixes from the rows' rehearsals; e79947863, a race in `pairing.test.ts` that had failed #558's landing gate; and the registry plan's Progress lines for M4 to M7 (0e1718bbd, 5d7145b8c, d2b69fe64, 0ac951eaa), each a doperpowers landing, sminos 1.43.0 to 1.46.0, which complete the desk track's sminos milestones. Next under O15: M2b, the resume after a wake, in its gates on its own branch; then the final build, the three machines' swap on the owner's approval, the closing run and the owner's verdict from 2026-11-14.
269. **2026-11-11, the architect, under O15, at the landing of M2b (PR #567, merge f0ff96253): the resume after a wake is built, and the measured group's wake case holds acceptance 7's second on the mini over both carriers. In valid windows on the mini the direct carrier had its link up 83 ms after the restore and the last of six subscriptions answered at 200 ms, at 1-minute loads of 2.1 to 2.5, and the relay 107 ms and 457 ms at a load of 3.9, against 1,000 ms; no session was read whole on the remote machine in either window. The final build carries three known gaps at a link's return, kept out by the architect and registered as board 565.** What was built, as Decision Log 267 designed it and the builder's report lists by task (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p5-o15-m2b-report.md`): main's log file with a time on every line and the error words; the wake source and the reach; the link's window with its two lanes, the floor and the relay socket's rules; the org feed and the project socket after a wake; the endpoint's `subbed` line with its time from the `sub`; the peer message's owner label, `host.log`'s eviction and journal lines and `artifact_export`'s description; the 7e probe; and the gate's wake case. The owner answered Decision Log 267's open question with "Build it now (Recommended)", so the session stream's checkpoints are built too, every 10,000 rows, with a detector that stops a launch's live checkpoints when the journal would write an engine line otherwise than the stream said it. Beside log lines (the endpoint's `subbed` line, `host.log`'s two and the hub link's error words), that is the one change of behaviour on `mawsd`'s side, and the wire does not change. The wake case and its rule, fixed by the architect before any number was read: per carrier the old sockets stay open and silent, the wake is injected no sooner than 158 s after the stall so that the remote machine is past its own resume window, the machine settles immediately before the timed window, and the path returns at a time the app is not told. The link and each of six subscriptions must be answered within 1,000 ms of the restore and the feed within 2,000 ms. A 1-minute load of 5 or more on any of five readings per carrier voids the run; a miss with one of the six stated conditions is recorded as outside the claim; a read of a session's whole launch on the remote machine between the restore and that session's answer fails the case. What the case found. Its first readings with the remote machine past its own resume window, on hosted Macs, were 1.4 s on the direct carrier and 3.6 s on the relay (run 42920990691). The cause was in the app: a remote session's tab draws "is offline" in place of the pane while the link is down, the pane's unmount freed the transcript, and its mount at the return read the whole launch over the link inside the window in which the stream resumed. The fix keeps the transcript through the placeholder (`src/renderer/src/lane-b/transcript-hold.ts`) and main says how each subscription was answered: `remote.session` carries a count of answers and the last one's mode, a tab and its source arm at main's word of the loss, and the first answer after it decides, a replay keeping the rows and any other mode causing one re-read. This is inside the app; the wire and `mawsd` are as they were. Four of that fix's five review rounds found a defect, and the record keeps them because one was of the architect's own proposal and another was found only by the panel the architect added. A reviewer found that the first version kept the rows after any return; a second round found two defects in inferring the answer from the machine list, which the count replaced; the architect then had main also answer a return with no subscription, whose review reproduced an overlap with a session's opening, and the architect had it reverted the same night; a panel of eight on the whole change found that the hold lost a loss said in the call in which it first held, with tests that sent main's two messages in the order main never sends. Each is fixed with a test that failed first, and the last fix was reviewed again alone. The second finding is not fixed and is stated: with the fix in, hosted Macs still read the relay's last answer at 1,039 to 1,289 ms in valid windows (runs 42936316081, 42936315294 and 42942499138). The remote machine answers both sessions within about 20 ms of each other, and the second answer reaches the app 610 to 680 ms after the first, behind up to 256 KiB of the first session's replay in the channel's send window, and the same journal read costs 2.3 to 4.1 times its warm self after the remote service's idle collection, with either kind of collection. On the mini that wait read 262 ms, and the bound holds there with the relay's last answer at 457 ms. An app-side order for it, each resumed subscription asking one message of credit until all are answered, is designed and not built. The three gaps: a tab first shown while its machine is offline reads its whole launch as soon as the link is up; a visible tab of an idle remote session reads its whole launch at each return; and after a return that nothing subscribes after, a held transcript stays undecided until that stream's next answer, so a load that failed in the outage is not retried by itself. The first two are as before M2b; the third is new with the fix and narrow, and its repair is the round that was reverted. Board 563, found by the same reading, is the reader's place in the transcript lost at every reconnect of a remote tab; it is outside P5's acceptance and was kept out of the final build, the owner told. 273. **2026-11-13, the architect, under O15 and acceptance 14, at the margin work's landing (merge 4109f5e25 of `p5-idle-margin`'s head ef0e009e4 onto master d6f8c10de): Decision Log 124's idle collection gains a quiet entry, which amends its "no timer"; the two cases the owner's ruling of 2026-11-12 asked recovered read within the bound, and the four-kept case, 4.1 MiB lower, still reads over by 602 KiB.** The quiet entry (`src/shared/host/idle-collect.ts`): a process that opts in also collects after a quiet period of 30 s (ACTIVITY_QUIET_MS) with no activity told, whatever its own idle predicate reads, so `mawsd` collects with clients other than the app connected and the engine host after work its quiet held through (in run 42986183954 the supervisor's last collection came before the render). Its clock is armed by activity alone and runs out once per period; GROWTH_BYTES still guards the collection, with one floor shared with the idle entry; the next period is armed only by the next activity, so a process is woken at most once after its last activity and never periodically. `mawsd` and the engine host opt in; the pty host does not, since its idle is no live pty and it is outside the bound. Activity is what starts or carries work, told where it comes in: a request or command received, a work lifetime's begin and end, a host event that is news, a change the store's watcher hears, a chunk on a cua pipe, a document port or a tcp forward. What a process writes out is not activity, nor is a periodic sample: `eventNews` treats an event as news unless its only change since the last of its subject is telemetry (a session status's `memory`, the budget's `totalKiB` and `sampledAt`), so the engine host's 10 s memory sample keeps no period from running out; it lets a subject go at the session's exit or removal and every subject at a host link's end, and holds 1,024 subjects at most. The moment of collecting asks the process's live `settled` again, and also for no work lifetime open, no activity since the period ran out and no I/O of the process's own in flight (`ioInFlight`: libuv's file, socket-write and DNS requests); held off by I/O alone it is asked again 1 s later, five times at most. A request's lifetime is its runtime's, from receipt until its answer settles, its client gone or not. An operation that answers before it ends holds a lifetime until it settles, is dropped or is cancelled: the connectors' starts, route D's operations, a dataset's publication, an artifact fetch's body, an artifacts.sock response, an endpoint dispatch; `mawsd`'s loaded spine with work in flight is not settled, and a hub sync is held as an operation. A standing hold (an open remote channel's, an endpoint document channel's park need) guards the exit and the park as before but is no operation in flight: the clocks are told the operations apart, and the collection's `settled` reads only those. What remains: a collection can still land on work no hook names (an async step parked on a timer, a CPU pass between macrotasks; the document search indexer is the one such loop the sweep left, a tracker row). The cost is one pause of that work per quiet period, about 50 ms (48 to 54 ms measured for the engine host's quiet collections on the mini, run 43310045595), behind the growth guard, never a lost or wrong result; a new kind of long-running work takes a work lifetime where it is created. The daemon-idle harness now records each measured process's memory by category (`footprint`, or `vmmap --summary` for a pid it cannot read) before the window's CPU is first read and after its last sample, as the records' `categories` and one printed line, counting swapped inside dirty. Reviews: three reviewer-high rounds, each finding fixed, closed after the third with the residual pause above named rather than chased; a diagnostic measured run on e3a9d9e1e (43310045595) read the render case within (97,763 KiB) with the supervisor's quiet collection firing twice, and showed the browser and kept cases never firing, the periodic sample's fault, fixed in round 2. The measured readings on ef0e009e4, on the mini under CI-opt's holds, against DAEMON_IDLE_BOUND (99,145 KiB, 1.12 s), in KiB of `mawsd` and the supervisor: `e2e:daemon-idle` (43342345313) 95,843 (35,729 + 60,305), CPU 0.11 s, load 4.2 (pair 4.4), valid, within (96,579 at eb17e5459); `e2e:daemon-idle-render` (43344708307) 98,675 after a render host has ended, valid, within by 470; 96,402 with the browser idle, void by the load rule (5.25, pair 5.89, `spotlightknowledged` at the top); 99,747 with four kept sessions (38,817 + 61,233), valid, over by 602; and again (43350172010) 98,179 after a render host has ended (37,857 + 60,321), valid, within by 966; 97,523 with the browser idle (37,841 + 59,537), valid, within by 1,622; 102,466 with four kept sessions as read, void by load (9.33, pair 10.26). At eb17e5459 the three read 100,339, 99,522 and 103,874. The supervisor's quiet collection shows in the render case's log (43344708307: "idle: collected in 45 ms, heap 36.7 to 33.2 MiB (used 33.1 to 32.0), after 30 s with no activity"; 44 ms in 43350172010's, and 44 ms in `e2e:daemon-idle`'s); the browser and kept cases' artifacts carry no daemon log, so whether it ran there is not in the records (a tracker row). The verdict: the cases the ruling of 2026-11-12 ("머지 후 바로 여유 회복 (Recommended)", Decision Log 272) asked recovered, the render host ended and the browser idle, read within on valid readings; the four-kept case, over by construction before the registry landed, is 4.1 MiB lower and still over by 602, and goes to M5's verdict on 2026-11-14 as it stands, the wire registry's cut (the tracker's row of 2026-11-12) its candidate. The records now place what lies outside the V8 heap: `mawsd`'s native allocations sit in the region under Chromium's PartitionAlloc page tag (`footprint`'s App-Specific Tag 14, VM tag 253; the system malloc zones hold about 1 MiB in every case), 7,344 KiB in `e2e:daemon-idle` and 7,824 to 8,336 in the cases with clients other than the app, 0.5 to 1.0 MiB more, beside its V8 region (Tag 16, VM tag 255) of 22.6 to 26.5 MiB. Gates: before landing, `--gates all` on ef0e009e4 (43320203632; 64 jobs green, 4 skipped); the merge's class 1a (43355351589), whose one red, unit 1/4 at `src/engine-host/documents/kinds/document-perf.test.ts:122` (cpuMs 44.7 where 51.24 was asked, a CPU-timing case on a hosted runner, in a file the change does not touch, the tracker's row of 2026-11-03), was rerun with `--failed`; master moved to be663d261 in one push with #607 (1d1db65f0) and #604 (be663d261) on top, and the gate on that chain (43355342867, class 1a with `e2e:layout`, `e2e:settings`, `e2e:presentations`, `e2e:design` and `e2e:document`) is green. The tracker gains the search indexer's row, the residual pause's, and the measured browser and kept cases' missing daemon logs; the wire registry's row gains the kept case's reading; the 2026-11-11 `mawsd` row gains this outcome and the placement by category; the document-perf row gains its third sighting.

275. **2026-11-14, the architect, under the UX wave's U9 and the project registry (board #644, PR #647, master 5002c670a): on this Mac another machine's session is filed by its listing whether or not its project is known here. It amends O7 M2's "one row per address" and the registry's M11.** Found by U9's scene `e2e/remote.spec.ts:3272` (A8 to A10, the fork step), red on the mini's host (run 43523605515) and green on hosted Macs. A person opened another machine's session from that machine's row; once this Mac held the session's record, the row left the machine's row for a project row of the Projects list, collapsed and so not drawn, and the machine's row went too when nothing was left in it. The cause was on master, not in U9: `rowOfListedRecord` (`src/renderer/src/stores/org-projects.ts`) let the record's project decide whenever that project was known here, on O7 M2's premise that "RL2 machines send no projects". The registry's M11 (1ba432458) ended that premise: `registryRecords` (`stores/registry.ts`) places every registry location on another machine among this Mac's projects under the address the record carries, so the same address was filed under the machine's row before its record arrived and under the project's row after. The mini met it because its registry read arrived before the record; hosted runs did not. The rule, the landing's three lines: (1) another machine's session is filed by its listing whether or not the registry knows its project, because that row is the one the person clicked, and one address is not filed in two places depending on whether a record is held; (2) the registry places other machines' locations among this Mac's projects under the address the record carries, so "its project is known here" no longer means "this Mac's project"; (3) this Mac's records keep the old rule, and the tab set, the resume picker and the channel's waiting filter follow the sidebar, so each shows the session where the sidebar does. The other option, filing the listing where the record would go, lost: a listing does not carry the record's project, so it would need a read the click does not have, and it is the larger change. Tests first (the registry-placed project with a listing whose key names no row: the row stays the machine's when the record arrives; and the case where listing and registry agree on the key). Runs: class 1a 43547613057, unit:darwin 43547639326, `e2e:remote` on a hosted Mac 43543938420 and on the mini's host 43544485448, `default` on the mini's host 43543894146. What is left, in the tracker: while a record is held, a listing that goes away (a gone at a higher revision, a failed re-verification, a hub snapshot without the key, the machine leaving the roster) files the row under the registry's project without a click; reachable today, traced from code, not reproduced.

The UX wave runs as its own initiative; the desk-web deploy the owner asked for once its last link lands is not P5's.

## Outcomes and retrospective

Written on 2026-11-13 by the architect under O15 M5 (Decision Log 274), before the owner's verdict. The final build, **F**, is master f1852de3a: fixed at the first machine's swap on 2026-11-11 21:39Z, on the MacBook, the mini and `dc-linux171-1e68`, with production's hub deployed from it (Decision Log 270). F's gates ran on f8f1d41b0, whose tree differs from F's only under `docs/` (O15's Decision Log 43 (m)). Times are UTC. The readings behind each line are in O15's Progress and its Decision Logs 44 and 45 (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p5-o15-recomposition.md`). Evidence that shows the owner's sessions stays on the architect's disk under `~/.local/share/maws-architect/o15-evidence/`, and the folders named below are relative to it; a run number is a GitHub Actions run.

The claim ids are the evidence inventory's (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p5-o15-inventory-evidence.md`). The result words are those of O15's Progress: pass, finding, void, refused, not run, and cited for the one claim taken from the record. A finding is a check that ran and showed something the owner judges; no claim is recorded as failed, and the architect waives nothing. Of the 67: 46 pass; 5 are findings (2b, 3b, and 15a to 15c under one verdict); 4q passes for one machine and is a finding and not run for the other; 4 are refused (4m to 4p); 5 are not run (7a to 7e); 2 are void (14a, 14b); 1 is cited (11a); and 3 are the verdict (16a to 16c), given on 2026-11-14.

**O15's own acceptance 2, the repair window, live: finding** (2026-11-12 06:57:30Z to 07:21:52Z, F; `quiet-window/`). With MAWS turned off under Login Items, opening MAWS at 06:59:53Z drew no repair window: the launch bootstrapped the unit, `mawsd` started at 06:59:54.955Z and adopted both hosts, the outcome was `unit`, and the app opened with its sessions. No host was a child of the app. The return half of the row was not exercised, and the owner turned the switch on again at about 07:01Z. At F a packaged launch whose unit is not loaded bootstraps it and nothing reads the switch, so the app ran `mawsd` while the owner's switch was off (O15 Decision Log 45 (c)).

**Acceptance 14 on the mini, as Decision Logs 270, 272 and 273 left it.** On F's code `e2e:daemon-idle` read 97,715 KiB and 0.13 s against 99,145 KiB and 1.12 s, and the session host 4,353 KiB of 5,120 (run 42948213134). In `e2e:daemon-idle-render` on F the case after a render host has ended was within (a median of 99,107, within by 38 KiB), the browser-idle case read over in two valid windows (99,938 and 99,586) and is recorded over by the owner's ruling, "초과로 기록, 진행 (Recommended)", and the four-kept case read over the one-session bound as it is built. After F, the registry's app side read over in all three cases, and the owner ruled "머지 후 바로 여유 회복 (Recommended)" (Decision Log 272). The margin work that followed (merge 4109f5e25, Decision Log 273) put the two cases the ruling named back within the bound on valid readings: 98,675 and 98,179 KiB after a render host has ended, 97,523 with the browser idle, and `e2e:daemon-idle` at 95,843. The four-kept case, 4.1 MiB lower, still reads over by 602 KiB on its valid reading (99,747) and goes to the verdict as it stands. No bound was moved.

### What each child delivered

P5's purpose is one MAWS, signed in as one person of an org, that shows every machine that person may see, the org's projects and the sessions in each, and drives each session at MAWS's own depth on whatever machine it runs. Each child against it, with the claims above that rest on it:

- **O1, wire compatibility, seams and the bench** (PR #213, master 368ede2cf; Decision Log 43). Main's services became movable without being moved, with a protocol major and minor in every hello (OX1) and the local-latency budget (OX7). 1c read inside OX7 on F's code, and 13b crossed a wire minor in the gate.
- **O2, `mawsd`'s lifecycle, packaging and the pty host** (PR #296, master 7a5893b2c; Decision Log 107). Every machine got its daemon: a unit for the main profile, a private daemon for every other, and terminals that outlive the app. At F the MacBook's pty host of 2026-10-30 was still running, kept across the swaps, and 1a ran on a private daemon. Acceptance 14's bound is its measurement.
- **O3, the services host and the spine, then git, scm and files** (closed, Decision Log 168). The spine, the index, attention and the session service run in `mawsd`. That is what 2a to 2c read with the app quit, and 1d's index was the same file after the swap.
- **O4, library, connectors, delivery and catalog** (PRs #399 and #424; Decision Logs 169 and 183). The rest of main's plain-Node services moved, with the custody of the connectors' grants. 2a's dataset refresh went through a connector with the app quit. The record of the Keychain adoption on the owner's Macs is still not taken (below).
- **O5, Chromium on the machine** (PRs #418 and #428, closed by #432; Decision Logs 181, 191 and 192). The render service, the artifact endpoint and the daemon-owned cua socket. 10a and 10b returned a PNG and a PDF on the VM with no app, and 4l's screenshots came from each machine's own render host.
- **O6, the renderer's machine dimension and org projects** (PR #268, master d7ea2f657; Decision Log 88). Addresses, stores and layout keys by machine, and the sidebar's machine chips: 3a and 3c.
- **O7, remote lane B at full depth** (M1 to M10, closed at Decision Log 222). Lane B sessions on other machines in the app, with the remote surfaces: the 24 cells of 4a to 4l, 5a and 5b, 8a and 8b. Its remote bounds are held on one host; across the owner's network they have no valid run (4m to 4p).
- **O8, the org interface** (M1 to M8, done at Decision Log 263). Sign-in, the Org page, owners on rows, visibility and shares, the project channel, and the app as the org's signer: 9x, 11a, 11c's Channel tab, and 12 with O18.
- **O9, lane A in MAWS** (Decision Logs 131, 143, 149 and 241). Terminal sessions controlled through the bridge on this Mac and on another, with what the bridge cannot reach disabled and named: 6a to 6d.
- **O10, the endpoint module and the protocol package** (the desk track; M1 to M11, every acceptance clause met, Decision Log 159). Each machine's one authenticated, encrypted way in, with the policy and the grades enforced on the machine: 9a to 9c and 12c's refusals.
- **O11, hub v2** (the desk track; M1 to M8, PR #212). The hub as a relay and a store of signed things: it refused 9a's forged uploads and carried the VM's link in 4q while the direct path was down.
- **O12, sminos under the endpoint** (the desk track; done, Decision Log 215). Owners on every session record, the opId ledger and the lane B observer: 11b's `verified` line with its owner.
- **O13, `web-cards`, the phone** (the desk track; M1 to M7, production's org rooted at Decision Log 119). The phone's cards over the new protocol, kept through the cutover. The closing run did not reach it, since 7c and 7d are not run; its last live record is B2's, a blocking card answered from the phone on production on 2026-10-29 (Decision Log 146).
- **O14, cutover** (the desk track; done, Decision Log 242). Each machine on `mawsd` and RL1 for good, and the shared org key of desk phase 1 gone from every place that kept it.
- **O16, the holder** (PR #442, Decision Log 200; PRs #448 and #449, Decision Log 205). A session host per lane B process, so an engine-host upgrade ends nothing: at the mini's swap six live sessions were handed to the new supervisor (13c).
- **O17, the agent's browser with no app, and its remote view** (PRs #450 and #459; Decision Logs 209 and 210). Not required for the acceptance; landed whole. On Linux the agent's browser still reads unavailable with its reason (10c).
- **O18, message reach** (the desk track; every milestone landed, Decision Logs 244 and 251). Who may message a session's agent. Not required for P5's close; 12a's listings by visibility ran on it.
- **O15, recomposition and the owner's week** (Decision Logs 266 to 274). The embedded root deleted (PR #562), the resume after a wake built and held in the gate on both carriers (PR #567), one final build on the three machines and production's hub, the closing run, and this record.

### Items handed to O15 by name

- **The migrated-history label** (Decision Logs 155 and 242): closed. On F the MAWS project's Channel tab read "attested at migration by" each of the three machines on events from before the move (11c's production half).
- **RL10's projects** (Decision Log 242): closed into the project registry. The owner approved its design (Decision Log 265); the desk track's half landed (the record and the hub on master, then sminos 1.42.0 to 1.46.0), and the app's half is on master since 2026-11-12 (PR #571, Decision Log 272). What is left is the registry's own M13, the live check on three machines, on its track. 3a's carried case, a remote session in a project this Mac does not hold, closes there.
- **O8's acceptance item 7**, a project's Channel tab across machines (Decision Log 264): closed by 11c's production half, read by the architect's driver on F, not by the owner's own look.
- **The deployment admin device** (Decision Log 69): closed. 9x read one root on F, this Mac's step-up key.

- **The MacBook's cold idle reading** (Decision Logs 168, 173 and 180): taken once, as 14a and 14b, and void by load. Not closed: as read it is about 8 times the footprint bound. Carried by the tracker's row of 2026-11-12 on the MacBook's idle cost.

- **Decision Log 161's field check**, no three-exit line in `mawsd.log` at the next upgrade: closed. The MacBook's swap of 2026-11-06 counted no missing module, no replaced bundle, no unexpected host exit and no `error:` line (Decision Log 239), and at the swaps to F the old `mawsd` wrote its planned `bundle was replaced` lines on the MacBook and restarted itself with code 75 on the mini (O15 Decision Log 44 (c)).
