The first run's failure, a command that gave up at 10 s while the daemon ran on, was fixed by PR #343 (Decision Log 136), and the single run after the mini's rebuild read 392 writers verified with no mismatch (Decision Log 141). O15's M5 text names #345 for the fix; #345 fixed step 7. The same text says the logs are past retention; no other record here says so, and the question said that it was not confirmed. The owner closed the item.

### What is carried

Each line names its tracker row by its date and subject (`docs/tech-debt-tracker.md`) or its board ticket.

*The closing run's exceptions, which the owner judges at the verdict.*
- Row 7 (7a to 7e), not run, deferred by the owner with no date: the row of 2026-11-13 on acceptance 7. Its folders, its probe and its runbook are kept.
- 4m to 4p, refused at the bench's precheck: the row of 2026-11-05 on the live remote bench, which now names the mini as the runner.
- 14a and 14b, void, and the MacBook's footprint as read: the row of 2026-11-12 on the MacBook's idle cost.
- Acceptance 14's four-kept case, over by 602 KiB: the row of 2026-11-12 on the daemon processes' wire registry, its candidate. Beside it, the rows of 2026-11-11 on `mawsd`'s step and on the harness's load rule, whose change is the owner's, and the three rows of 2026-11-13 from the margin work.
- The repair window: the row of 2026-11-12 on the launch outcome. What a switched-off unit should do is the owner's ruling.
- 15 and 4q's half on the mini: the row of 2026-11-12 on the direct listener's addresses (the tailnet found by address range), and the row of 2026-11-12 on acceptance 15 (the engine's loopback listener, and the mini's inventory not taken).
- 3b: the row of 2026-11-12 on sminos's org listing and MAWS's remote row. The desk track's three options for the listing, none built: as now (5.3 to 10.9 s); waiting published at its first tick (0.3 to 5.9 s); or that plus a nudge from the uplink's plane (about 0.7 to 1.6 s), the only one within 2 s and the desk track's recommendation.
- 2b: the row of 2026-11-12 on the remote row's state in the mini's window.
- 1d's Connectors read and O4's adoption record: the row of 2026-11-13.
- 9a's event half, no check from the hub's own position, and 11c's drawing with two members: two rows of 2026-11-13.

*Other findings of the closing run* (O15 Decision Log 45 (g)), each a row of 2026-11-12: a failed engine-configuration push kept until an engine-host restart, which refused every local lane B start on the MacBook for about four hours after the swap; a Stop whose interrupt fails showing nothing; the 4-mini project's sessions missing from the mini's own window; a window off screen answering late; the Channel tab reading "reading channel" through a network loss; the late `[reach]` line; and the unit tests' leaked `gcloud` double.

*From M1 and M2, the deletion.* The row of 2026-11-10 on the document store's ack path, and rows of 2026-11-13: main's answers for an older `mawsd`; the two retired settings fields; the idle bound's baseline, which only a tree from before M2 can re-measure; the router's `embedded` binding kind; main's graph into `src/services/` through `command-budgets`; the code the quit's simplification left; and acceptance 3's cross-build scene, which needs an embedded peer.

*From M2b, the resume after a wake.* Boards #565 (a remote tab's reads at a link's return), #563 (the reader's place lost at every reconnect), #566 (what the app draws while sessions work unattended) and #559 (a listed row ignores ⌘-click and middle-click); the rows of 2026-11-11 on a link's return and on the relay carrier's resume; and rows of 2026-11-13: `mawsd`'s own hub link after a wake; the direct carrier's token bucket and the race's losers; the project socket's wake path with no case in a built app; the owner label read from sminos's frame; the hub's time on every request; a live stream against a replay for numbers JSON cannot carry; the real dial's uncancelled lookups; and two unverified cases across a link's loss. O15's M5 text also carries the tracker's row of 2026-10-28 on a lost range drawing no gap; at master the transcript draws "Earlier history isn't available" for it (O7 M2), and the row is amended to say so.

*The org root's key.* The row of 2026-11-13, with the corrected Risks row.

*The registry.* Its M13, and its own rows of 2026-11-10 and 2026-11-12; the row of 2026-11-13 on the word a refused spawn gives for a location outside the hub.

*Flakes met on these branches* are in CI-opt's row of 2026-10-28 on hosted Macs (its items 29, 36 and 37) and the rows of 2026-10-31 and 2026-11-03 on the latency stub and `document-perf.test.ts`.

### Lessons

- **A bound judged by a load rule needs a runner nobody works on.** Both measured checks planned from the MacBook have no valid reading: the bench's precheck refused at a 1-minute load of 35.40 against 5, and 14a's window voided at a peak of 44.3. The MacBook read 19 to 108 in every reading of the week. On the mini the browser-idle case voided in six of eight runs, on one-second fan-outs of system daemons that move no footprint. No bound and no rule was moved to get a number, and each void reading is recorded as read. A margin of 38 KiB did not hold at the next landing that was measured, and it came back from one change, a collection after 30 s with no activity. What changes: a row names its runner when it is written, and a measured sitting starts from a read of that machine's own quiet.
- **A withdrawn experiment left the design depending on its answer.** O15's Decision Log 6 (e) withdrew the Login Items experiment because the design must not depend on what macOS does, and the launch as built bootstraps the unit whatever the switch reads. The live row showed it on its first try: the app ran `mawsd` with the owner's switch off. Where only the machine can say what the OS does, the experiment is run, on the macOS the owner uses.
- **The owner's machine asleep or idle was the scarcest input, and it came last.** Row 7 was the first of the inventory's largest gaps and the run's last row, and by then the owner had other sessions running and the MacBook could not sleep. The record holds the setup, two unplanned samples and the owner's deferral in place of the hour. A check that needs the owner to stop using a machine goes first in a closing run, or is built to run somewhere else.
- **Reading what each claim can be observed with, before the build was fixed, was worth its day.** That read found 7e could not pass by design and could not have been read off the machine: nothing reacted to the network's return, and main's log had no file and no times. M2b fixed both before F. Run on F as it stood, the owner's hour would have confirmed what the code already showed.
- **One build on the real machines found what no child's gate could.** Before the run 36 of the 67 claims had nothing live. The run added fourteen findings. Three of them: the Wi-Fi the two Macs share hands out addresses in 100.64/10, so `mawsd` listened on the LAN and the MacBook's link to the mini ran over it under the label "direct (tailnet)"; a configuration push that timed out under load at the swap refused every local lane B start for about four hours; and `sminos list --org` cannot hold 3b's 2 s by its design. None of the three shows on one host.
- **An agent driving the app is an instrument, and its faults were kept apart from the app's.** 4d's first presses of Stop sent nothing: the machine's own ledger held no interrupt for them and one for the retake, and main's log showed that each press had only focused the window. 3b's first loop lost its row to a title change. Each first take stays in the record beside its retake, and a row was retaken only once the instrument was shown at fault.
